- **Score**: 8/10

### MultiEdit: Advancing Instruction-based Image Editing on Diverse and Challenging Tasks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14638v1)
- **Authors**: Mingsong Li, Lin Liu, Hongjun Wang, Haoxing Chen, Xijun Gu, Shizhan Liu, Dong Gong, Junbo Zhao, Zhenzhong Lan, Jianguo Li
- **Abstract**: Current instruction-based image editing (IBIE) methods struggle with challenging editing tasks, as both editing types and sample counts of existing datasets are limited. Moreover, traditional dataset construction often contains noisy image-caption pairs, which may introduce biases and limit model capabilities in complex editing scenarios. To address these limitations, we introduce MultiEdit, a comprehensive dataset featuring over 107K high-quality image editing samples. It encompasses 6 challenging editing tasks through a diverse collection of 18 non-style-transfer editing types and 38 style transfer operations, covering a spectrum from sophisticated style transfer to complex semantic operations like person reference editing and in-image text editing. We employ a novel dataset construction pipeline that utilizes two multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) to generate visual-adaptive editing instructions and produce high-fidelity edited images, respectively. Extensive experiments demonstrate that fine-tuning foundational open-source models with our MultiEdit-Train set substantially improves models' performance on sophisticated editing tasks in our proposed MultiEdit-Test benchmark, while effectively preserving their capabilities on the standard editing benchmark. We believe MultiEdit provides a valuable resource for advancing research into more diverse and challenging IBIE capabilities. Our dataset is available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/inclusionAI/MultiEdit.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the "MultiEdit: Advancing Instruction-based Image Editing on Diverse and Challenging Tasks" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MultiEdit, a large-scale dataset for instruction-based image editing (IBIE). MultiEdit aims to address the limitations of existing datasets, which primarily focus on simple edits of natural images and often suffer from noisy image-caption pairs. MultiEdit contains over 107K high-quality image editing samples covering 6 challenging editing tasks, including object and person reference editing, text editing within images, GUI editing, view editing, and style transfer.  It includes 18 non-style-transfer editing types and 38 style transfer operations. The dataset construction leverages two multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) to generate visual-adaptive editing instructions and produce high-fidelity edited images. The authors demonstrate that fine-tuning foundational open-source models with MultiEdit-Train improves performance on sophisticated editing tasks, as benchmarked in their provided MultiEdit-Test dataset, while preserving performance on standard benchmarks.

**Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the combination of several factors:
    *   **Dataset Scale & Diversity:** MultiEdit provides a significant increase in the number of high-quality samples and the diversity of editing tasks compared to previous publicly available datasets. The focus on complex, real-world scenarios (like editing in structured images or semantic reasoning) fills a gap.
    *   **MLLM-Driven Pipeline:** The use of the SOTA MLLM and ImageGen models for instruction generation and image editing is a key differentiator. Bypassing the traditional caption-based approaches addresses issues of noise and information loss. The generation of *visual-adaptive editing instructions* and high-fidelity edited images is a strong point.
    *   **Comprehensive Task Coverage:** The sheer number of editing types (56 total, with the breakdown between non-style-transfer and style transfer) indicates a deliberate effort to comprehensively cover the IBIE landscape.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Bridging the Data Gap:** The paper directly addresses the critical data limitations hindering the progress of IBIE.  The dataset has the potential to drive significant improvements in model performance, particularly in more sophisticated editing tasks.
    *   **High-Quality Data:** Emphasis on high-quality data generation via MLLMs and rigorous data cleaning is crucial. This is a significant advantage over datasets that simply scale up with noisy data.
    *   **Benchmark for Complex Edits:** The MultiEdit-Test benchmark provides a more challenging and realistic evaluation platform for IBIE models, pushing the field towards more complex and practical applications.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined and Motivated Problem:** The paper clearly identifies a limitation in the IBIE field and provides a strong justification for addressing it.
    *   **Rigorous Methodology:** The data construction pipeline is well-described, and the choice of MLLMs is justified.  The experimental setup is well-defined, with appropriate baselines and metrics.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The results demonstrate the effectiveness of fine-tuning foundational models with MultiEdit-Train on the proposed benchmark.
    *   **Open Dataset:** Making the dataset publicly available promotes further research and development in this area.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Proprietary Models:** The core of their pipeline (MLLM and ImageGen) relies on proprietary OpenAI models. While this allowed the generation of high-quality data *now*, the dependence on these models poses risks to reproducibility and long-term sustainability. If these models change drastically or are unavailable, it impacts the usability of the *method* for creating similar datasets.  This also limits the accessibility for researchers who do not have API access.
    *   **Limited Ablation/Analysis:** While the paper explores MTL strategies, a more in-depth ablation study on different aspects of the dataset (e.g., the impact of visual-adaptive instructions vs. simpler instructions) would strengthen the claims.
    *   **Open Source Model Performance:** While the results demonstrate improvements for open source models after fine-tuning, it would be beneficial to see a comparison with models trained *from scratch* on MultiEdit-Train versus other datasets. This would more clearly isolate the value of MultiEdit.
    *   **Chinese Text Limitations:** The acknowledgment of limitations with Chinese text handling by the OpenAI models is a valid point, but it restricts the applicability of the dataset to certain domains.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to be highly influential by providing a valuable resource for the IBIE research community. It is expected to accelerate progress in complex instruction-based image editing tasks.

**Score: 8.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper offers a significant contribution by providing a high-quality, diverse, and large-scale dataset for IBIE. The MLLM-driven pipeline and comprehensive task coverage are strong points. The results demonstrate the value of the dataset, and the release of MultiEdit-Test fosters further research. The primary drawback is the dependence on proprietary OpenAI models for dataset construction, which introduces some issues of reproducibility and accessibility. While the results are convincingly demonstrated on open-source models, the reliance on proprietary elements within their generation framework slightly dims the practical impact score. Despite this concern, the dataset itself is a valuable addition to the field, hence the high score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### DyWPE: Signal-Aware Dynamic Wavelet Positional Encoding for Time Series Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14640v1)
- **Authors**: Habib Irani, Vangelis Metsis
- **Abstract**: Existing positional encoding methods in transformers are fundamentally signal-agnostic, deriving positional information solely from sequence indices while ignoring the underlying signal characteristics. This limitation is particularly problematic for time series analysis, where signals exhibit complex, non-stationary dynamics across multiple temporal scales. We introduce Dynamic Wavelet Positional Encoding (DyWPE), a novel signal-aware framework that generates positional embeddings directly from input time series using the Discrete Wavelet Transform (DWT). Comprehensive experiments in ten diverse time series datasets demonstrate that DyWPE consistently outperforms eight existing state-of-the-art positional encoding methods, achieving average relative improvements of 9.1\% compared to baseline sinusoidal absolute position encoding in biomedical signals, while maintaining competitive computational efficiency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel signal-aware positional encoding method called Dynamic Wavelet Positional Encoding (DyWPE) for time series transformers.  Unlike traditional positional encodings that rely solely on sequence indices, DyWPE leverages the Discrete Wavelet Transform (DWT) to extract signal characteristics and generates positional embeddings dynamically based on the content of the time series. The method involves channel projection, multi-level wavelet decomposition, learnable scale embeddings, dynamic modulation of these embeddings using wavelet coefficients, and reconstruction using the inverse DWT.  Experimental results on eleven diverse time series datasets demonstrate consistent performance improvements compared to eight existing positional encoding methods. The paper also includes ablation studies to validate the effectiveness of signal-awareness and multi-scale decomposition.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The central idea of signal-aware positional encoding is indeed novel. Existing methods predominantly ignore the underlying signal characteristics.  Applying wavelet transforms for this purpose is also a creative approach.  The dynamic modulation step, where wavelet coefficients influence the scale embeddings, is a significant innovation.
*   **Significance:** Time series analysis benefits greatly from encoding temporal relationships, and if this method genuinely improves upon existing ones, it is of value. Time series data is ubiquitous, spanning areas like finance, medicine, and environmental monitoring. The improved accuracy from the signal-aware positional encoding can lead to more reliable insights and better decision-making in these domains. The comprehensive empirical validation across diverse datasets bolsters the claim of improved performance and generalization ability. The ablation studies further strengthen the claims by isolating the contributions of different components.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novelty:** The core concept of signal-aware positional encoding in transformers for time series is a clear and impactful contribution.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments are thorough, covering a wide range of datasets and comparing against several strong baselines.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** Rigorous ablation studies are conducted to understand and validate the importance of the core components.
    *   **Computational Efficiency:** DyWPE maintains competitive computational efficiency compared to alternative methods, despite incorporating wavelet transforms.
    *   **Clarity:** The paper is well-written and presents the method and experimental results clearly.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Hyperparameter Sensitivity Analysis:**  While the experiments are thorough, it would be beneficial to understand the sensitivity of DyWPE's performance to key hyperparameters, such as the choice of wavelet family and the number of decomposition levels (J). The authors could include a sensitivity analysis.
    *   **Scalability to Very Long Sequences:** Although the authors claim O(L) complexity, the experimental results are limited in dataset size. Further evaluation is needed for significantly longer time series with thousands or millions of points, to verify and validate the impact of added computational cost for wavelet transformation with very long sequences.

*   **Impact:** The proposed DyWPE can influence the field by introducing a new paradigm for positional encoding in time series transformers, one that is inherently aware of the underlying signal characteristics. It can become a standard method to improve signal prediction performance, leading to further exploration of wavelet-based approaches in time series analysis. It sets a new direction for positional encoding by moving beyond index-based representations and considering signal-specific information.

**Justification for the Score:**

DyWPE represents a genuine advancement in the field of positional encoding for time series transformers. Its signal-aware approach is a significant departure from existing methods, and the thorough experimental validation demonstrates its effectiveness. While there are minor points for improvement, the paper is well-written, well-evaluated, and makes a clear contribution.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### SALT4Decompile: Inferring Source-level Abstract Logic Tree for LLM-Based Binary Decompilation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14646v1)
- **Authors**: Yongpan Wang, Xin Xu, Xiaojie Zhu, Xiaodong Gu, Beijun Shen
- **Abstract**: Decompilation is widely used in reverse engineering to recover high-level language code from binary executables. While recent approaches leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown promising progress, they typically treat assembly code as a linear sequence of instructions, overlooking arbitrary jump patterns and isolated data segments inherent to binary files. This limitation significantly hinders their ability to correctly infer source code semantics from assembly code. To address this limitation, we propose \saltm, a novel binary decompilation method that abstracts stable logical features shared between binary and source code. The core idea of \saltm is to abstract selected binary-level operations, such as specific jumps, into a high-level logic framework that better guides LLMs in semantic recovery. Given a binary function, \saltm constructs a Source-level Abstract Logic Tree (\salt) from assembly code to approximate the logic structure of high-level language. It then fine-tunes an LLM using the reconstructed \salt to generate decompiled code. Finally, the output is refined through error correction and symbol recovery to improve readability and correctness. We compare \saltm to three categories of baselines (general-purpose LLMs, commercial decompilers, and decompilation methods) using three well-known datasets (Decompile-Eval, MBPP, Exebench). Our experimental results demonstrate that \saltm is highly effective in recovering the logic of the source code, significantly outperforming state-of-the-art methods (e.g., 70.4\% TCP rate on Decompile-Eval with a 10.6\% improvement). The results further validate its robustness against four commonly used obfuscation techniques. Additionally, analyses of real-world software and a user study confirm that our decompiled output offers superior assistance to human analysts in comprehending binary functions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the SALT4Decompile paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SALT4Decompile, a novel binary decompilation method that aims to improve the accuracy and correctness of decompiled code by abstracting stable logical features shared between binary and source code.  Instead of treating assembly code as a linear sequence, SALT4Decompile constructs a Source-level Abstract Logic Tree (SALT) from assembly, representing the program's logic in a way that's more amenable to LLM-based semantic recovery. The SALT is then used to fine-tune an LLM, generate decompiled code, and subsequently refine the output through error correction and symbol recovery. The authors evaluate SALT4Decompile against general-purpose LLMs, commercial decompilers, and dedicated decompilation methods using well-known datasets.  Results demonstrate state-of-the-art performance, particularly in recovering source code logic and robustness against obfuscation techniques.  A user study confirms improved assistance to human analysts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:*

The core novelty lies in the SALT abstraction and its use to guide LLM-based decompilation.  Existing LLM-based decompilation approaches often struggle with the inherent complexity of assembly code's control flow and data handling. SALT addresses these challenges by explicitly extracting and representing the high-level logic structures. The idea of extracting control flow as trees is not completely new, but its application to LLM fine-tuning in this manner is novel. Additionally, combining SALT with targeted error correction and symbol recovery steps enhances the method's practical applicability. The technique distinguishes itself through its approach of working end-to-end directly from assembly code, which makes it more robust to architectural differences than refine-based methods.

*Significance:*

Decompilation is a crucial component of reverse engineering with important security applications. Improving decompilation accuracy and understandability directly contributes to more effective vulnerability analysis, malware analysis, and closed-source comprehension.  The performance improvements reported in the paper are significant, particularly the 10.6% gain in test case pass rate on the Decompile-Eval dataset. This translates to tangible benefits in terms of functional correctness. The robustness against obfuscation is another significant contribution, as malware often employs these techniques to evade detection and analysis. The user study provides valuable empirical evidence of the method's practical utility.  The release of the model weights and code also enables further research in the community.

*Strengths:*

*   **Well-defined Problem & Solution:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing LLM-based decompilation techniques and proposes a targeted solution (SALT) with a clear rationale.
*   **Rigorous Evaluation:** Comprehensive experimental evaluation using multiple datasets, diverse baselines, and various metrics. The evaluation also includes obfuscation robustness and real-world software analysis.
*   **Practical Relevance:** The user study demonstrates the real-world benefits of the approach for reverse engineering tasks.
*   **Reproducibility:**  The authors release model weights and code, enabling reproducibility and further research.
*   **Addresses limitations of refine-based methods:** Bypasses the architectural constraints introduced by commercial decompilers like Ghidra that end-to-end methods can face.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Reliance on Angr:** While Angr is used for convenience, the reliance on a specific binary analysis framework introduces a potential dependency and might limit the applicability to certain architectures if Angr support is lacking.  While the paper states that only disassembly and CFG extraction are needed, the practicality of reimplementing these functionalities for other architectures should be considered.
*   **Limited Scope of Logical Structures:**  The current implementation focuses primarily on loops.  Extending SALT to incorporate other common control flow structures (e.g., conditional statements, switch statements) could further improve performance. While these are mentioned as future work, their absence in the current implementation is a limitation.
*   **Component-wise Evaluation Depth:** More rigorous evaluations are needed for each of the stages. For example, better evaluations are needed for the Error Correction and Symbol Recovery modules to show the effectiveness of each stage.
*   **Scalability to Larger Programs:** The paper focuses on function-level decompilation.  Extending the approach to handle larger, more complex programs presents challenges related to context window limitations and inter-procedural analysis.
*   **API usage Costs:** Reliance on expensive APIs for models such as Claude-3.5 introduce significant costs that may be impractical for researchers to reproduce.

*Potential Influence:*

SALT4Decompile has the potential to significantly influence the field of binary decompilation. The approach provides a more structured and semantically rich representation of assembly code, which can be leveraged not only for LLM-based decompilation but also for other reverse engineering tasks. The method opens opportunities for developing more intelligent and robust decompilation tools and has implications for software security, malware analysis, and closed-source comprehension. The approach is particularly helpful in areas where precise control flow analysis is crucial.

**Justification of Score:**

SALT4Decompile presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of binary decompilation. While the method has some limitations, its strengths outweigh its weaknesses. The comprehensive evaluation demonstrates state-of-the-art performance and practical utility. The paper provides valuable insights into the challenges of LLM-based decompilation and presents a promising solution with considerable potential for future research and development. The combination of a novel approach with practical applications and open resources justifies a high score.

### AgentCompass: Towards Reliable Evaluation of Agentic Workflows in Production
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14647v1)
- **Authors**: NVJK Kartik, Garvit Sapra, Rishav Hada, Nikhil Pareek
- **Abstract**: With the growing adoption of Large Language Models (LLMs) in automating complex, multi-agent workflows, organizations face mounting risks from errors, emergent behaviors, and systemic failures that current evaluation methods fail to capture. We present AgentCompass, the first evaluation framework designed specifically for post-deployment monitoring and debugging of agentic workflows. AgentCompass models the reasoning process of expert debuggers through a structured, multi-stage analytical pipeline: error identification and categorization, thematic clustering, quantitative scoring, and strategic summarization. The framework is further enhanced with a dual memory system-episodic and semantic-that enables continual learning across executions. Through collaborations with design partners, we demonstrate the framework's practical utility on real-world deployments, before establishing its efficacy against the publicly available TRAIL benchmark. AgentCompass achieves state-of-the-art results on key metrics, while uncovering critical issues missed in human annotations, underscoring its role as a robust, developer-centric tool for reliable monitoring and improvement of agentic systems in production.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AgentCompass, a novel evaluation framework designed for monitoring and debugging agentic workflows in production environments. AgentCompass models expert debugger reasoning through a multi-stage analytical pipeline involving error identification and categorization, thematic clustering, quantitative scoring, and strategic summarization. It uses a dual memory system (episodic and semantic) for continual learning across executions. The framework is evaluated on real-world deployments and the publicly available TRAIL benchmark, demonstrating state-of-the-art results on key metrics and uncovering errors missed by human annotations. The authors emphasize AgentCompass's role as a robust, developer-centric tool for improving the reliability of agentic systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the comprehensive and structured approach to evaluating agentic workflows *post-deployment*. While individual components like error taxonomies, memory systems, and clustering algorithms exist, the integrated pipeline specifically tailored for agentic workflow debugging is a unique contribution. The integration of *developer-centric features* based on real-world deployments strengthens the paper by adding immediate practical value.

*   **Significance:** Agentic workflows are rapidly gaining adoption, but their complexity introduces new risks. The lack of adequate evaluation tools is a significant barrier to widespread, responsible deployment. AgentCompass addresses this gap by providing a tool designed for production environments that is able to diagnose and address production level bugs that human-level annotations would miss. Showing a substantial gap in error identification versus human annotation using a established public benchmark (TRAIL) highlights the significant value-add AgentCompass provides as a tool for AI Developers. The demonstrated state-of-the-art results on the TRAIL benchmark further validate the framework's efficacy.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The structured, multi-stage analytical pipeline provides a clear and actionable framework for debugging.
    *   The dual memory system allows for continual learning and improved diagnostic accuracy over time.
    *   The validation on real-world deployments adds practical relevance and demonstrates the framework's utility.
    *   The identification of errors missed by human annotations highlights the limitations of existing evaluation methods and underscores AgentCompass's value.
    *   Detailed trace analysis representation for LLM-based workflows improves observability and interpretability.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Reliance on a proprietary, fine-tuned LLM (Turing Large model) makes it difficult to assess the generalizability of the results. While the framework is designed to be model-agnostic, performance could vary with different LLMs.
    *   The error taxonomy, while comprehensive, might require further refinement and customization for specific domains or applications.
    *   The quantitative evaluation, while strong, could be enhanced with more detailed ablation studies to isolate the impact of individual components (e.g., the memory system, the plan-and-execute cycle).
    *   Future work should focus on extending the framework beyond trace-level analysis, to include system-level performance metrics and resource utilization.

*   **Overall Impact:** The paper presents a significant contribution to the field of agentic AI by addressing a critical need for reliable evaluation tools. AgentCompass has the potential to become a valuable asset for organizations deploying agentic workflows, enabling them to identify and mitigate risks, improve system performance, and ensure responsible AI practices. The paper’s impact is further enhanced by the authors working with established design partners to demonstrate real-world utility of the presented framework before conducting benchmark evaluations, illustrating the focus the authors have on creating a tool which is designed for production environments.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper demonstrates a well-designed and evaluated framework with significant practical implications. The novelty lies in the integration of existing techniques into a comprehensive pipeline specifically tailored for agentic workflow debugging. The identified weaknesses (reliance on proprietary models and lack of detailed ablation studies) are relatively minor and do not detract significantly from the overall contribution. The potential for AgentCompass to improve the reliability and trustworthiness of agentic systems warrants a high score.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### MUSE: MCTS-Driven Red Teaming Framework for Enhanced Multi-Turn Dialogue Safety in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14651v1)
- **Authors**: Siyu Yan, Long Zeng, Xuecheng Wu, Chengcheng Han, Kongcheng Zhang, Chong Peng, Xuezhi Cao, Xunliang Cai, Chenjuan Guo
- **Abstract**: As large language models~(LLMs) become widely adopted, ensuring their alignment with human values is crucial to prevent jailbreaks where adversaries manipulate models to produce harmful content. While most defenses target single-turn attacks, real-world usage often involves multi-turn dialogues, exposing models to attacks that exploit conversational context to bypass safety measures. We introduce MUSE, a comprehensive framework tackling multi-turn jailbreaks from both attack and defense angles. For attacks, we propose MUSE-A, a method that uses frame semantics and heuristic tree search to explore diverse semantic trajectories. For defense, we present MUSE-D, a fine-grained safety alignment approach that intervenes early in dialogues to reduce vulnerabilities. Extensive experiments on various models show that MUSE effectively identifies and mitigates multi-turn vulnerabilities. Code is available at \href{https://github.com/yansiyu02/MUSE}{https://github.com/yansiyu02/MUSE}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MUSE, a novel framework designed to enhance the safety of Large Language Models (LLMs) in multi-turn dialogues. It addresses the challenge of multi-turn jailbreaks, where attackers leverage conversational context to bypass safety mechanisms. MUSE consists of two main components:
*   **MUSE-A (Attack):** This module utilizes frame semantics and Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) to systematically explore diverse semantic trajectories and identify vulnerabilities. By framing attacks as transitions between related concepts (frames), MUSE-A overcomes limitations of previous methods that often get stuck in superficial modifications.
*   **MUSE-D (Defense):** This module is a fine-grained safety alignment approach that intervenes early in dialogues to mitigate vulnerabilities.  It uses risk scores derived from MCTS-guided attack exploration to weight training examples, enabling granular preference tuning and strengthening safety protocol activation at vulnerable decision points.

The authors conduct extensive experiments on various LLMs, demonstrating that MUSE effectively identifies and mitigates multi-turn vulnerabilities without harming the models' reasoning and task performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**
    *   **Strengths:** The paper introduces a fresh perspective by applying frame semantics to jailbreak attacks.  The combination of frame semantics and MCTS in MUSE-A appears to be a novel approach to exploring and exploiting vulnerabilities in multi-turn dialogues. MUSE-D offers a granular intervention strategy compared to traditional methods.
    *   **Weaknesses:**  The reliance on GPT-4o for automated judging, while practical, can introduce bias. The evaluation also appears to primarily focus on specific jailbreak scenarios, with perhaps less attention to broader safety implications. It is unclear how MUSE can adapt to new emerging jailbreak techniques.

*   **Significance:**
    *   **Strengths:** The increasing prevalence of multi-turn interactions with LLMs makes this work highly relevant.  The demonstrated ability to improve safety without sacrificing task performance is significant, as it addresses a key trade-off in LLM alignment. It provides a concrete framework and methodology for both attacking and defending against multi-turn vulnerabilities, potentially influencing future research directions.
    *   **Weaknesses:**  The study primarily examines the approach on a limited set of models and datasets. Further investigation is needed to validate the generalizability of MUSE across a wider range of LLMs and real-world application scenarios. The framework may be computationally expensive due to the use of MCTS and fine-grained training, potentially limiting its scalability.

* **Rigour:**

    * **Strengths:** The paper presents a well-defined methodology with clear explanations of MUSE-A and MUSE-D. The experimental setup is carefully described, including datasets, baselines, and evaluation metrics. Ablation studies are performed to assess the contribution of different components. The paper also discusses ethical considerations related to the attack strategies. Sensitivity analysis has also been conducted to investigate key parameter's impact on the performance.
    * **Weaknesses:** Although human evaluation is performed, the reliability is based on GPT-4's alignment with human judgement, the dataset can be considered limited.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by shifting the focus towards more comprehensive defenses that consider the nuances of multi-turn dialogues. The application of frame semantics could inspire new approaches to understanding and mitigating LLM vulnerabilities. The open-source release of the code will facilitate further research and development in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of LLM safety. The proposed MUSE framework demonstrates promising results in both attacking and defending against multi-turn jailbreaks. The application of frame semantics is a novel and insightful approach, and the granular intervention strategy of MUSE-D offers a significant improvement over existing methods. However, limitations regarding dataset diversity, reliance on GPT-4o for judging, and the potential computational cost prevent a higher score.

- **Score**: 7/10

### Understanding the Thinking Process of Reasoning Models: A Perspective from Schoenfeld's Episode Theory
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14662v1)
- **Authors**: Ming Li, Nan Zhang, Chenrui Fan, Hong Jiao, Yanbin Fu, Sydney Peters, Qingshu Xu, Robert Lissitz, Tianyi Zhou
- **Abstract**: While Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) generate extensive chain-of-thought reasoning, we lack a principled framework for understanding how these thoughts are structured. In this paper, we introduce a novel approach by applying Schoenfeld's Episode Theory, a classic cognitive framework for human mathematical problem-solving, to analyze the reasoning traces of LRMs. We annotated thousands of sentences and paragraphs from model-generated solutions to math problems using seven cognitive labels (e.g., Plan, Implement, Verify). The result is the first publicly available benchmark for the fine-grained analysis of machine reasoning, including a large annotated corpus and detailed annotation guidebooks. Our preliminary analysis reveals distinct patterns in LRM reasoning, such as the transition dynamics between cognitive states. This framework provides a theoretically grounded methodology for interpreting LRM cognition and enables future work on more controllable and transparent reasoning systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach to understanding the reasoning process of Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) by applying Schoenfeld's Episode Theory, a cognitive framework originally designed for analyzing human mathematical problem-solving. The authors annotate thousands of sentences and paragraphs from model-generated solutions to math problems using seven cognitive labels defined by Schoenfeld's theory (Read, Analyze, Plan, Implement, Explore, Verify, Monitor). This results in a publicly available benchmark for fine-grained analysis of machine reasoning, including a large annotated corpus and detailed annotation guidebooks. Preliminary analysis reveals distinct patterns in LRM reasoning, such as transition dynamics between cognitive states. The authors argue that this framework provides a theoretically grounded methodology for interpreting LRM cognition, enabling future work on more controllable and transparent reasoning systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in applying Schoenfeld's Episode Theory to the analysis of LRM reasoning. While some prior work has observed human-like behaviors in LRMs and even used cognitive views for analysis, this paper offers a more structured, theoretically grounded approach.  The creation of a publicly available, annotated dataset is also a significant contribution.  However, the novelty is somewhat tempered by the fact that other cognitive theories exist, and the core idea is leveraging an existing theory to a new domain.

*   **Significance:** The potential significance of this work is high. By providing a framework for understanding the internal structure of LRM reasoning, the authors open up possibilities for:

    *   More effective debugging and improvement of LRMs
    *   Developing more controllable and transparent reasoning systems
    *   Comparing LRM reasoning strategies to human problem-solving
    *   Better understanding the limitations of LRMs and identifying areas for improvement

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Theoretically Grounded:** The use of Schoenfeld's Episode Theory provides a solid foundation for the analysis.
    *   **Empirical Analysis:** The paper includes a large annotated dataset and preliminary analysis of LRM reasoning patterns.
    *   **Publicly Available Resources:**  Releasing the dataset and annotation guidebooks promotes reproducibility and further research.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the methodology and findings.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Analysis:** The preliminary analysis provides only a glimpse of the potential insights from the annotated dataset. More in-depth analysis is needed to fully demonstrate the framework's value.
    *   **Potential for Subjectivity:** Annotation, even with guidelines, can be subjective. While the authors likely have inter-rater reliability metrics, demonstrating high agreement is crucial.
    *   **Limited LRM Selection:** The study focuses on DeepSeek-R1. Expanding the analysis to other LRMs would increase the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Missing details about human-LLM agreement for the annotations:** The paper doesn't provide inter-annotator agreement or comparison with the annotation results given by other LRMs.
    *   **The framework could be too specific to math problems:** Schoenfeld's theory is very specific to mathematics which may reduce the transferability of this method to other domains.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence research in several areas, including:

    *   Explainable AI (XAI)
    *   Cognitive Science
    *   Natural Language Processing
    *   Mathematics Education

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper offers a novel application of an existing theory and provides a valuable resource in the form of an annotated dataset, the impact is somewhat limited by the relatively preliminary nature of the analysis and the fact that similar, though less structured, observations have been made before. A more comprehensive analysis demonstrating clear, actionable insights would significantly boost the score. The lack of comparisons between annotators and models, and concerns about the narrow scope to mathematics further diminishes the strength of the paper. Therefore, a score of 6 seems appropriate.

- **Score**: 6/10

### TableDART: Dynamic Adaptive Multi-Modal Routing for Table Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14671v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaobo Xing, Wei Yuan, Tong Chen, Quoc Viet Hung Nguyen, Xiangliang Zhang, Hongzhi Yin
- **Abstract**: Modeling semantic and structural information from tabular data remains a core challenge for effective table understanding. Existing Table-as-Text approaches flatten tables for large language models (LLMs), but lose crucial structural cues, while Table-as-Image methods preserve structure yet struggle with fine-grained semantics. Recent Table-as-Multimodality strategies attempt to combine textual and visual views, but they (1) statically process both modalities for every query-table pair within a large multimodal LLMs (MLLMs), inevitably introducing redundancy and even conflicts, and (2) depend on costly fine-tuning of MLLMs. In light of this, we propose TableDART, a training-efficient framework that integrates multimodal views by reusing pretrained single-modality models. TableDART introduces a lightweight 2.59M-parameter MLP gating network that dynamically selects the optimal path (either Text-only, Image-only, or Fusion) for each table-query pair, effectively reducing redundancy and conflicts from both modalities. In addition, we propose a novel agent to mediate cross-modal knowledge integration by analyzing outputs from text- and image-based models, either selecting the best result or synthesizing a new answer through reasoning. This design avoids the prohibitive costs of full MLLM fine-tuning. Extensive experiments on seven benchmarks show that TableDART establishes new state-of-the-art performance among open-source models, surpassing the strongest baseline by an average of 4.02%. The code is available at: https://anonymous.4open.science/r/TableDART-C52B
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TableDART, a novel framework for table understanding that uses a dynamic adaptive multi-modal routing approach.  Unlike existing methods that either flatten tables into text (losing structural information) or treat tables as images (struggling with fine-grained semantics), TableDART intelligently combines textual and visual modalities. It does so by learning a routing policy using a lightweight MLP gating network to dynamically select the optimal processing path for each table-query pair: either using a text-only model, an image-only model, or a fusion approach. When the fusion path is chosen, an LLM agent is used to either arbitrate between the outputs of the single-modality models or to synthesize a new answer.  A key advantage is that TableDART reuses pre-trained single-modality experts and only trains the gating network, making it significantly more training-efficient than fine-tuning large multimodal LLMs. Experimental results on several benchmarks demonstrate that TableDART achieves state-of-the-art performance among open-source models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the dynamic, adaptive routing mechanism. While multimodal approaches for table understanding exist, the ability to intelligently select the most appropriate modality or fuse modalities *based on the specific table and query* is a significant contribution. The use of a lightweight, trainable gating network coupled with frozen, pre-trained experts allows for efficient training. The agent for the fusion path is also a contribution, allowing reasoning about the two modalities without expensive end-to-end fine tuning of large multi-modal models.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical problem in table understanding: how to effectively combine different modalities without introducing redundancy, conflicts, or excessive computational cost. The proposed solution significantly improves performance compared to existing approaches, offering more efficient and robust table understanding. The results indicate that the dynamic routing mechanism generalizes well, as shown by the generalization performance on unseen datasets. The improvement in inference efficiency due to the adaptive routing is also significant.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed framework and its components.
    *   The experimental evaluation is comprehensive, using several benchmark datasets and comparing against a wide range of strong baselines.
    *   The ablation studies and analysis of the routing policy provide valuable insights into the behavior of the framework.
    *   The increased efficiency of the system is well validated.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on Gemini 2.0 Flash as the core of the fusion agent does introduce some limitations. Future work could explore other LLMs with different architectures, parameter scales or training paradigms.
    *   The construction of the 10,000 sample training mixture could be a source of potential bias or overfitting. While the experiments show good generalization, a larger and more diverse training set might further improve performance.
    *   The reported numbers could be better. Given the improvements in language modeling, it is not clear whether or not some of the numbers are artificially low. The data here could be improved to show the impact of this paper better, or it may be that the task is reaching a point of diminishing returns.

*   **Potential Impact:** The TableDART framework has the potential to influence future research in table understanding and multimodal reasoning. The dynamic routing mechanism could be applied to other multimodal tasks, such as visual question answering or document understanding. The training-efficient approach could encourage the development of more scalable and accessible multimodal systems.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**
The paper provides a clear and significant contribution with a novel approach and strong experimental results. The limitations are not crippling, and the potential impact is high. However, it does rely on a specific language model and has numbers that, while good, could potentially be inflated by previous works. The idea of dynamic routing is relatively new and unexplored in the context of table understanding, and the parameter-efficient approach makes it a promising avenue for future work. However, there are still some doubts on the impact given how close it is to current language model performance, so a high score cannot be completely justified.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### LEED: A Highly Efficient and Scalable LLM-Empowered Expert Demonstrations Framework for Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14680v1)
- **Authors**: Tianyang Duan, Zongyuan Zhang, Songxiao Guo, Dong Huang, Yuanye Zhao, Zheng Lin, Zihan Fang, Dianxin Luan, Heming Cui, Yong Cui
- **Abstract**: Multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) holds substantial promise for intelligent decision-making in complex environments. However, it suffers from a coordination and scalability bottleneck as the number of agents increases. To address these issues, we propose the LLM-empowered expert demonstrations framework for multi-agent reinforcement learning (LEED). LEED consists of two components: a demonstration generation (DG) module and a policy optimization (PO) module. Specifically, the DG module leverages large language models to generate instructions for interacting with the environment, thereby producing high-quality demonstrations. The PO module adopts a decentralized training paradigm, where each agent utilizes the generated demonstrations to construct an expert policy loss, which is then integrated with its own policy loss. This enables each agent to effectively personalize and optimize its local policy based on both expert knowledge and individual experience. Experimental results show that LEED achieves superior sample efficiency, time efficiency, and robust scalability compared to state-of-the-art baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LEED, a framework for multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) that integrates Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate expert demonstrations.  LEED employs a decentralized training approach where each agent learns from both LLM-generated demonstrations and its own experience.  The framework has two key modules: a demonstration generation module (DG) that uses LLMs to create task-specific instructions and a policy optimization module (PO) that blends expert knowledge with autonomous exploration through a mixed policy loss function.  LEED is evaluated on multi-agent traffic planning tasks, demonstrating improvements in sample efficiency, time efficiency, and scalability compared to existing MARL algorithms.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of leveraging LLMs for generating expert demonstrations in MARL is reasonably novel, though not entirely unexplored. Previous works have integrated LLMs into RL for tasks like reward shaping or state representation, but LEED's approach of using LLMs to create demonstrations and guide decentralized training in a multi-agent setting distinguishes it. A weakness is the incremental nature of the novelty. The core MARL algorithm (PPO) is established, and the LLM integration is a relatively modular addition. The refinement process of the LLM prompt through environmental feedback is a good feature, but not groundbreaking.

*   **Significance:** Improving sample efficiency, scalability, and coordination in MARL are crucial goals.  The empirical results showing LEED's superiority to established baselines (MAPPO, IPPO, QMIX) on real-world traffic planning tasks are significant. The paper makes a strong case for the potential of LLMs to address the coordination challenges in MARL. The positive scalability results indicate that the benefits of LLM-based guidance increase as the number of agents grows, which is a key factor for many practical applications. A weakness is the limitation to traffic planning, making it uncertain if the approach generalizes to other MARL tasks.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined framework with two distinct modules (DG and PO).
    *   Strong empirical results with comparisons to established baselines.
    *   Scalability analysis showing benefits for larger agent populations.
    *   Ablation studies demonstrating the importance of the dynamic loss weighting scheme.
    *   Qualitative analysis of LLM-generated demonstrations, showcasing improvement through refinement.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited generalizability. The experiments are primarily conducted on traffic planning. Its utility in other MARL domains needs verification.
    *   Dependence on LLM quality. The performance of LEED is tied to the quality of the demonstrations generated by the LLM.  The paper acknowledges this to some extent (discussing prompt engineering), but more analysis could be done on the sensitivity of LEED to different LLM architectures or prompt designs.
    *   The paper doesn't deeply investigate the failure modes of the LLM. Under what environmental conditions might the LLM give poor guidance, and how does this impact the overall performance?
    *   The "expert" demonstration may not always be the best path for all agents, which could lead to suboptimal learning.

*   **Potential Influence:** If validated across more diverse MARL problems, LEED could become a standard approach for integrating LLMs into MARL, accelerating progress in robotics, autonomous systems, and other applications. It motivates further research into the interaction between LLMs and RL, potentially leading to more sophisticated methods for knowledge transfer and adaptive learning.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

LEED presents a valuable and well-executed idea: integrating LLMs for expert demonstration within MARL, particularly to enhance scalability and efficiency. However, the novelty is incremental; it extends existing LLM-in-RL concepts to the MARL domain, utilizing a standard MARL algorithm. While the empirical results are compelling within the traffic planning context, the paper lacks broad validation across diverse MARL tasks and sensitivity analysis to potential LLM failure modes. Additionally, there is room for improvement in better understanding the role of 'expert' guidance and how it can be best integrated to benefit all the agents.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.MA
- **Score**: 7/10

### RationAnomaly: Log Anomaly Detection with Rationality via Chain-of-Thought and Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14693v1)
- **Authors**: Song Xu, Yilun Liu, Minggui He, Mingchen Dai, Ziang Chen, Chunguang Zhao, Jingzhou Du, Shimin Tao, Weibin Meng, Shenglin Zhang, Yongqian Sun, Boxing Chen, Daimeng Wei
- **Abstract**: Logs constitute a form of evidence signaling the operational status of software systems. Automated log anomaly detection is crucial for ensuring the reliability of modern software systems. However, existing approaches face significant limitations: traditional deep learning models lack interpretability and generalization, while methods leveraging Large Language Models are often hindered by unreliability and factual inaccuracies. To address these issues, we propose RationAnomaly, a novel framework that enhances log anomaly detection by synergizing Chain-of-Thought (CoT) fine-tuning with reinforcement learning. Our approach first instills expert-like reasoning patterns using CoT-guided supervised fine-tuning, grounded in a high-quality dataset corrected through a rigorous expert-driven process. Subsequently, a reinforcement learning phase with a multi-faceted reward function optimizes for accuracy and logical consistency, effectively mitigating hallucinations. Experimentally, RationAnomaly outperforms state-of-the-art baselines, achieving superior F1-scores on key benchmarks while providing transparent, step-by-step analytical outputs. We have released the corresponding resources, including code and datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "RationAnomaly: Log Anomaly Detection with Rationality via Chain-of-Thought and Reinforcement Learning" introduces a new framework for log anomaly detection. It tackles the limitations of existing deep learning and LLM-based methods by combining Chain-of-Thought (CoT) fine-tuning with reinforcement learning (RL).  The approach first uses CoT-guided supervised fine-tuning on a high-quality, expert-validated dataset to instill expert-like reasoning patterns.  Then, a reinforcement learning phase, utilizing a multi-faceted reward function, optimizes detection accuracy and logical consistency, reducing hallucinations. The experimental results demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on key benchmarks, with transparent, step-by-step analytical outputs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents a relatively novel approach by integrating CoT fine-tuning and reinforcement learning for log anomaly detection.  While individual components (CoT and RL) are not new, their specific combination for addressing the limitations of LLMs in this context (interpretability, hallucinations, lack of step-by-step reasoning) demonstrates a degree of innovation. The expert-driven data correction stage is also noteworthy, addressing a known weakness of existing benchmark datasets.

* **Significance:**  The paper addresses a critical need in the field of AIOps: reliable and interpretable log anomaly detection.  Current methods often lack transparency or suffer from inaccuracies, making them less trustworthy in production environments. RationAnomaly aims to improve both accuracy and interpretability, which is a significant step towards building more robust AIOps tools. The improvement over existing state-of-the-art methods, particularly on the expert-corrected datasets, adds to the significance.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Approach:** The paper integrates multiple techniques (expert data correction, CoT fine-tuning, reinforcement learning) to address the problem from various angles.
    * **Addressing Limitations of LLMs:** The paper directly tackles the issues of hallucinations and lack of step-by-step reasoning in LLMs for log analysis.
    * **Improved Performance:** The experimental results show significant improvements over existing baselines, especially in F1-score and achieving a balanced precision-recall profile.
    * **Emphasis on Interpretability:** Providing transparent, step-by-step reasoning is a valuable feature for practical application.
    * **High-Quality Dataset:** Addressing and correcting the benchmark data errors strengthens the results and contribution.
    * **Ablation Study:** the study dissecting the impact of different parts of the contribution showcases the value of the methodology.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on GPT-4o:**  The use of GPT-4o as a teacher model for CoT dataset generation might introduce biases or limitations inherent in that specific model.  A broader exploration of alternative teacher models could strengthen the work.
    * **Computational Cost:** Reinforcement learning can be computationally expensive.  The paper could benefit from a discussion of the computational resources required and potential optimizations for real-world deployment.
    * **Generalizability to Other Log Types:** While the results are promising, the experiments are conducted on specific datasets (BGL and Spirit). It would be beneficial to discuss the potential challenges and adaptations needed to apply RationAnomaly to other types of logs (e.g., application logs, security logs).

* **Potential Influence:** RationAnomaly has the potential to influence the development of more reliable and interpretable AIOps tools. The framework could serve as a blueprint for combining LLMs with reinforcement learning to address other challenges in system monitoring and diagnosis. The open-sourcing of code and datasets will also facilitate further research in this area.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:**

The paper offers a novel and significant contribution by combining CoT fine-tuning and reinforcement learning to address the key limitations of existing log anomaly detection methods. The emphasis on both accuracy and interpretability makes it valuable for real-world applications.  The weaknesses, particularly the reliance on a specific teacher model (GPT-4o) and limited discussion of computational cost/generalizability, prevent it from achieving a higher score.  Overall, the paper represents a solid advancement in the field and has the potential to influence future research.

### Transcoder-based Circuit Analysis for Interpretable Single-Cell Foundation Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14723v1)
- **Authors**: Sosuke Hosokawa, Toshiharu Kawakami, Satoshi Kodera, Masamichi Ito, Norihiko Takeda
- **Abstract**: Single-cell foundation models (scFMs) have demonstrated state-of-the-art performance on various tasks, such as cell-type annotation and perturbation response prediction, by learning gene regulatory networks from large-scale transcriptome data. However, a significant challenge remains: the decision-making processes of these models are less interpretable compared to traditional methods like differential gene expression analysis. Recently, transcoders have emerged as a promising approach for extracting interpretable decision circuits from large language models (LLMs). In this work, we train a transcoder on the cell2sentence (C2S) model, a state-of-the-art scFM. By leveraging the trained transcoder, we extract internal decision-making circuits from the C2S model. We demonstrate that the discovered circuits correspond to real-world biological mechanisms, confirming the potential of transcoders to uncover biologically plausible pathways within complex single-cell models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the application of transcoders, a type of sparse autoencoder, to enhance the interpretability of single-cell foundation models (scFMs).  scFMs, while achieving state-of-the-art performance in tasks like cell-type annotation and perturbation response prediction, are notoriously difficult to interpret. The authors focus on the cell2sentence (C2S) model and train transcoders on its MLP layers to extract internal decision circuits.  They demonstrate that these extracted circuits contain features that correspond to known biological mechanisms, specifically showing how circuits activated during endothelial cell classification include genes known to be endothelial markers (VWF, PTPRB, SPARCL1).  The authors conclude that transcoders offer a promising approach to uncover biologically plausible pathways within complex single-cell models and highlight challenges and future research directions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents a genuinely novel approach.  While sparse autoencoders have been used for feature extraction in related contexts (like genomic models), applying *transcoders* for circuit tracing within scFMs appears to be new.  Furthermore, the focus on relating these circuits to known biological pathways to improve interpretability is a significant contribution. Prior work extracted interpretable concepts but this work attempts to trace circuits.
* **Significance:** The significance of the work is high.  Interpretability is a major bottleneck in the application of deep learning methods to biology.  If transcoders can indeed provide a way to understand *why* scFMs make certain predictions, it could significantly increase their utility and trustworthiness in biomedical research. The ability to link model decisions to known biological pathways is crucial.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the interpretability challenge in scFMs.
    * **Novel Methodology:** The use of transcoders for circuit tracing is a promising approach.
    * **Biological Validation:** The demonstration of correspondence between extracted circuits and known biological mechanisms is a key strength, providing empirical evidence for the approach's validity.
    * **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and the methodology is explained in sufficient detail.
    * **Acknowledgement of Limitations:** The authors are upfront about the limitations of their work (manual interpretation, dataset dependence, complexity of the circuits).

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Scope:**  The evaluation is limited to a single model (C2S) and a specific cell type (endothelial cells).  More comprehensive testing is needed to demonstrate the generalizability of the approach.
    * **Manual Interpretation:**  The interpretation of the extracted circuits relies heavily on manual analysis.  This is subjective and time-consuming, and future work should focus on automated interpretation methods.  The complexity of the extracted circuits suggests that improvements in circuit extraction are necessary to create simpler, more interpretable representations.
    * **Computational Cost:** The paper doesn't delve into the computational cost of training and applying transcoders, which is an important consideration for large-scale scFMs.

* **Potential Impact:** If further developed and validated, the methodology could become a standard tool for interpreting scFMs, enabling researchers to gain deeper insights into cellular processes. The ability to identify and validate the biological underpinnings of model predictions will foster greater trust and wider adoption of scFMs. It could also lead to the discovery of new biological relationships.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper demonstrates a promising and novel approach, the limited scope of the evaluation and the reliance on manual interpretation hold it back from achieving a higher score. The single case study, while convincing, necessitates broader validation across different scFMs, cell types, and datasets to fully assess the method's robustness and general applicability.  The subjectivity inherent in manual interpretation also needs to be addressed with automated and more systematic approaches.  However, the potential for this method to enhance the interpretability of scFMs and contribute to biological discovery is significant.

### Decoupled Proxy Alignment: Mitigating Language Prior Conflict for Multimodal Alignment in MLLM
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14735v1)
- **Authors**: Chenkun Tan, Pengyu Wang, Shaojun Zhou, Botian Jiang, Zhaowei Li, Dong Zhang, Xinghao Wang, Yaqian Zhou, Xipeng Qiu
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have gained significant attention due to their impressive ability to integrate vision and language modalities. Recent advancements in MLLMs have primarily focused on improving performance through high-quality datasets, novel architectures, and optimized training strategies. However, in this paper, we identify a previously overlooked issue, language prior conflict, a mismatch between the inherent language priors of large language models (LLMs) and the language priors in training datasets. This conflict leads to suboptimal vision-language alignment, as MLLMs are prone to adapting to the language style of training samples. To address this issue, we propose a novel training method called Decoupled Proxy Alignment (DPA). DPA introduces two key innovations: (1) the use of a proxy LLM during pretraining to decouple the vision-language alignment process from language prior interference, and (2) dynamic loss adjustment based on visual relevance to strengthen optimization signals for visually relevant tokens. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DPA significantly mitigates the language prior conflict, achieving superior alignment performance across diverse datasets, model families, and scales. Our method not only improves the effectiveness of MLLM training but also shows exceptional generalization capabilities, making it a robust approach for vision-language alignment. Our code is available at https://github.com/fnlp-vision/DPA.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Decoupled Proxy Alignment: Mitigating Language Prior Conflict for Multimodal Alignment in MLLMs" identifies and addresses the problem of *language prior conflict* in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). This conflict arises when the inherent language priors of pre-trained LLMs clash with the language style present in the MLLM's training data. This mismatch can lead the MLLM to prioritize adapting to the training data's language style over effectively aligning visual and language modalities, resulting in suboptimal performance. To mitigate this issue, the authors propose a novel training method called Decoupled Proxy Alignment (DPA). DPA introduces two key innovations: 1) the use of a proxy LLM during pre-training to decouple the vision-language alignment from language prior interference and 2) dynamic loss adjustment based on visual relevance to strengthen the optimization signals for visually relevant tokens.  Experiments demonstrate that DPA mitigates language prior conflict and achieves superior alignment performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The identification and articulation of the "language prior conflict" in MLLMs as a specific and addressable problem is a strong point.  While the concept of language priors influencing model behavior is not entirely new, its application and analysis in the context of *multimodal alignment* and its negative consequences are relatively novel.  The proposed DPA method, including the proxy LLM and dynamic loss adjustment, represents a novel approach to address this specific conflict. However, the components individually (proxy model, dynamic loss weighting) have some precedents, so the novelty is primarily in the specific combination and application.

* **Significance:** The problem addressed is undoubtedly significant. MLLMs are an increasingly important area of research, and improving their alignment is crucial for their effective use. The paper provides strong empirical evidence that the identified language prior conflict is a real issue impacting performance. The DPA method demonstrably improves performance across a variety of models and datasets.  This demonstrates the potential for DPA to be a valuable technique for improving MLLM training.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines and explains the language prior conflict.
    * **Empirical Validation:** Thorough experiments on multiple datasets and model families provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of DPA. The ablation studies clearly demonstrate the contribution of each component of the DPA method.
    * **Detailed Analysis:** The word-level loss analysis provides insights into how DPA mitigates language prior conflict.
    * **Strong Results:** DPA consistently outperforms baseline methods and demonstrates good generalization ability.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Complexity:** The three-stage training process of DPA adds complexity to the training pipeline.  The paper sufficiently addresses computational costs but neglects any potential difficulties involved with hyperparameter tuning that would otherwise be relatively trivial when only fine-tuning the connectors.
    * **Reliance on GPT-4 for Labeling:** Using GPT-4 to classify words as "Language Prior" or "Visually Relevant" is somewhat subjective and relies on the quality of GPT-4's judgment.  While likely reasonable, exploring alternative, potentially more objective labeling methods could strengthen the analysis.
    * **Incremental Improvements:** While the improvements are consistent, their magnitude may be seen as incremental in some cases. Given the increasing complexity of MLLM training, the relative benefit of the approach compared to its implementation cost could be questioned in certain scenarios.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper's identification of the language prior conflict is likely to influence future research in MLLM training. The DPA method provides a practical solution that can be adopted and adapted by other researchers. The analysis and experimental results will likely inform the design of new training methods and datasets for MLLMs.

* **Overall:** The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of MLLMs by identifying and addressing the language prior conflict. The proposed DPA method is a novel and effective approach that improves alignment performance and demonstrates good generalization ability.  However, the complexity of the approach and the incremental nature of some improvements prevent it from being a truly transformative contribution.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**  The paper is well-written, technically sound, and addresses a relevant problem with a novel solution.  The strong empirical results justify the claim that DPA effectively mitigates language prior conflict. However, the limited individual contribution of each proposed innovation reduces the novelty in the broader scheme of things. The improvements, while consistently positive, are incremental, and the added complexity of DPA's training process is a consideration. Thus, while a valuable contribution, it stops short of being a truly groundbreaking paper worthy of a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### UnifiedVisual: A Framework for Constructing Unified Vision-Language Datasets
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14738v1)
- **Authors**: Pengyu Wang, Shaojun Zhou, Chenkun Tan, Xinghao Wang, Wei Huang, Zhen Ye, Zhaowei Li, Botian Jiang, Dong Zhang, Xipeng Qiu
- **Abstract**: Unified vision large language models (VLLMs) have recently achieved impressive advancements in both multimodal understanding and generation, powering applications such as visual question answering and text-guided image synthesis. However, progress in unified VLLMs remains constrained by the lack of datasets that fully exploit the synergistic potential between these two core abilities. Existing datasets typically address understanding and generation in isolation, thereby limiting the performance of unified VLLMs. To bridge this critical gap, we introduce a novel dataset construction framework, UnifiedVisual, and present UnifiedVisual-240K, a high-quality dataset meticulously designed to facilitate mutual enhancement between multimodal understanding and generation. UnifiedVisual-240K seamlessly integrates diverse visual and textual inputs and outputs, enabling comprehensive cross-modal reasoning and precise text-to-image alignment. Our dataset encompasses a wide spectrum of tasks and data sources, ensuring rich diversity and addressing key shortcomings of prior resources. Extensive experiments demonstrate that models trained on UnifiedVisual-240K consistently achieve strong performance across a wide range of tasks. Notably, these models exhibit significant mutual reinforcement between multimodal understanding and generation, further validating the effectiveness of our framework and dataset. We believe UnifiedVisual represents a new growth point for advancing unified VLLMs and unlocking their full potential. Our code and datasets is available at https://github.com/fnlp-vision/UnifiedVisual.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces UnifiedVisual, a novel framework for constructing unified vision-language datasets. The core idea is to create datasets that foster mutual enhancement between multimodal understanding and generation capabilities of vision-language models (VLLMs). The authors argue that existing datasets typically treat these two aspects in isolation, hindering the development of truly unified VLLMs.  To address this, they propose a framework that prioritizes: (1) diverse visual and textual inputs and outputs, (2) comprehensive cross-modal reasoning, and (3) precise text-to-image alignment.  They instantiate this framework with UnifiedVisual-240K, a dataset designed to facilitate mutual reinforcement between multimodal understanding and generation. Experiments demonstrate that models trained on this dataset achieve strong performance across various tasks and exhibit the desired mutual reinforcement between understanding and generation.

**Critical Evaluation**

**Novelty:**

The core novelty lies in the *design framework* for datasets, rather than individual components. While existing datasets might have some elements of what UnifiedVisual proposes, the integrated approach with a clear goal of mutual enhancement is a valuable contribution. The idea of interleaving visual and textual data in a manner that stimulates both understanding and generation in VLLMs is interesting. By explicitly designing the dataset to force these interactions, the authors are attempting to address a recognized weakness in existing VLLMs which treat understanding and generation as separate modules.

**Significance:**

The potential impact on the field lies in driving the development of more truly "unified" VLLMs. The paper identifies a key bottleneck: the lack of suitable training data. By providing a framework and a concrete dataset, they lower the barrier for researchers to explore this area.  The experimental results, while not groundbreaking in terms of absolute performance gains, do demonstrate the effectiveness of the framework in fostering the desired interaction between understanding and generation, further highlighting the potential significance of the work.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing datasets for unified VLLMs.
*   **Well-Defined Framework:** The UnifiedVisual framework is clearly described and justified with relevant arguments.
*   **Comprehensive Dataset:** The construction process for UnifiedVisual-240K is detailed and appears thorough.
*   **Experimental Validation:** The experiments offer solid evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed framework and dataset. The ablation studies specifically, showing that understanding and generation benefit from each other, are important.
*   **Extensibility:** The paper states that the framework is highly extensible, enabling efficient and large-scale dataset construction to support future unified VLLM development.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Improvement:** The performance gains over existing datasets, while consistent, are not dramatically significant. This could be due to the relatively small size of UnifiedVisual-240K compared to datasets used in pretraining large VLLMs.
*   **Reliance on GPT-4 for Rationale Generation:** The dataset construction relies heavily on GPT-4 for generating rationales. This could introduce biases or limitations inherent to GPT-4 into the dataset. While the authors acknowledge the need for better evaluation and benchmarking in the introduction, it's essential to address the potential biases introduced by using large-scale language models during training.
*   **Limited Exploration of Model Architectures:** The paper focuses primarily on a single (albeit representative) model architecture (Anole). While showing the dataset's efficacy on this architecture is important, the impact could be broader if multiple architectures are explored to showcase the generalizability of the framework.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a solid, well-reasoned contribution by identifying a key limitation in the data available for unified VLLMs and providing a concrete solution in the form of a dataset construction framework. The experimental results, while not revolutionary, support the claims and highlight the potential for future research. However, the reliance on large language models for generating rationales might introduce undesirable biases. I believe that the clear problem definition, well-executed solution, and supportive experiments justify a score reflecting the impact of designing datasets to specifically foster synergy between visual understanding and generation, even if the immediate performance gains are moderate. I believe this dataset construction framework will inspire much follow up work, justifying a decent score.
