### ReCoVeR the Target Language: Language Steering without Sacrificing Task Performance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14814v1)
- **Authors**: Hannah Sterz, Fabian David Schmidt, Goran Glavaš, Ivan Vulić
- **Abstract**: As they become increasingly multilingual, Large Language Models (LLMs) exhibit more language confusion, i.e., they tend to generate answers in a language different from the language of the prompt or the answer language explicitly requested by the user. In this work, we propose ReCoVeR (REducing language COnfusion in VEctor Representations), a novel lightweight approach for reducing language confusion based on language-specific steering vectors. We first isolate language vectors with the help of multi-parallel corpus and then effectively leverage those vectors for effective LLM steering via fixed (i.e., unsupervised) as well as trainable steering functions. Our extensive evaluation, encompassing three benchmarks and 18 languages, shows that ReCoVeR effectively mitigates language confusion in both monolingual and cross-lingual setups while at the same time -- and in contrast to prior language steering methods -- retaining task performance. Our data code is available at https://github.com/hSterz/recover.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ReCoVeR (REducing language COnfusion in Vector Representations), a novel lightweight approach to mitigate language confusion in multilingual Large Language Models (LLMs). Language confusion is the phenomenon where an LLM generates responses in a language different from the prompt or requested answer language. ReCoVeR utilizes language-specific steering vectors derived from multi-parallel corpora. It computes language vectors and then subtracts content vectors to get language-specific representations. These representations are then used in unsupervised or learned steering functions to bias the LLM's output towards the desired language. The authors evaluate ReCoVeR on several benchmarks and languages, demonstrating its effectiveness in reducing language confusion without significantly sacrificing task performance, unlike existing methods like LSI.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses an Important Problem:** Language confusion is a practical issue that limits the usability of multilingual LLMs, especially for low-resource languages.
    *   **Lightweight and Efficient:** ReCoVeR is designed to be computationally efficient, allowing for the addition of new languages without recomputing existing language vectors, which is a significant advantage over previous approaches.
    *   **Preserves Task Performance:** A key strength is the ability to reduce language confusion while maintaining task performance. Many previous language steering methods sacrifice performance for language fidelity, a problem ReCoVeR appears to solve well.
    *   **Extensive Evaluation:** The paper includes a thorough evaluation across multiple benchmarks (LCB, MultiQ, CrossSum) and 18 languages, offering robust evidence for the method's effectiveness. The authors also analyze results across different LLM sizes and types.
    *   **Effective Cross-Lingual Generalization:** ReCoVeR's ability to generalize to unseen languages is a strong point, demonstrating the potential for broad applicability.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Multi-Parallel Data:** While the paper states that multi-parallel datasets are readily available, the dependency on such data could be a limitation for very low-resource languages where such resources are scarce.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Justification:** While the paper provides an intuitive explanation for how ReCoVeR works, a more formal, theoretical justification for the approach could strengthen the paper.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While the paper shows ReCoVeR outperforms LSI, the overall improvement is often incremental, and not always substantial, especially for already well-performing models. More dramatic improvement is observed when combined with a learned steering function, but only when it is paired with a task specific training data.
    *   **Incomplete Ablation:** One could ask, could the simple inclusion of additional "task specific" tuning data be the source of improvement? More thorough exploration of ablation studies on this effect could strengthen the validity of this approach.
    *   **Reliance on Precomputed Language Vectors:** The need to calculate these values on flores is a limitation and a slight departure from zero shot.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper presents a novel approach to language steering that addresses a practical problem in multilingual LLMs. The key innovation lies in the way language vectors are computed (subtracting content vectors) and applied, allowing for efficient adaptation to new languages and preservation of task performance. The unsupervised and learned steering functions are well-designed and improve state-of-the-art across the board.
    *   The work builds on previous research in representation steering and language confusion mitigation. The main significant contribution is the efficient and effective mitigation of language confusion with minimal compromise to task performance, surpassing existing approaches.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   ReCoVeR has the potential to become a practical and widely-used technique for improving the reliability of multilingual LLMs, particularly in scenarios where language confusion is a concern. Its lightweight nature and ability to preserve task performance make it attractive for deployment.
    *   The paper's findings could inspire further research into representation steering and the development of more efficient and effective methods for controlling LLM behavior.

**Rigorous Rationale**

ReCoVeR provides a unique approach to the problem of language confusion within LLMs, and is demonstrated empirically that it out-performs the state of the art.

However, it has limitations regarding multi-parallel resources. Additionally, while improvements are demonstrated, are not substantial.

### Confirmation Bias as a Cognitive Resource in LLM-Supported Deliberation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14824v1)
- **Authors**: Sander de Jong, Rune Møberg Jacobsen, Niels van Berkel
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used in group decision-making, but their influence risks fostering conformity and reducing epistemic vigilance. Drawing on the Argumentative Theory of Reasoning, we argue that confirmation bias, often seen as detrimental, can be harnessed as a resource when paired with critical evaluation. We propose a three-step process in which individuals first generate ideas independently, then use LLMs to refine and articulate them, and finally engage with LLMs as epistemic provocateurs to anticipate group critique. This framing positions LLMs as tools for scaffolding disagreement, helping individuals prepare for more productive group discussions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Confirmation Bias as a Cognitive Resource in LLM-Supported Deliberation" explores how Large Language Models (LLMs) can be used to facilitate more productive group decision-making by strategically leveraging confirmation bias and epistemic vigilance. It argues that confirmation bias, often considered a negative trait, can be harnessed to encourage individuals to generate strong arguments in support of their initial positions.  The paper proposes a three-step process: (1) independent idea generation, (2) LLM-assisted articulation and strengthening of those ideas, and (3) LLM-driven epistemic provocation, simulating critiques. The goal is to help individuals refine their arguments and prepare for constructive disagreement within a group setting, ultimately leading to better reasoning outcomes.  The core idea is to position LLMs as tools that scaffold disagreement rather than as direct participants in the deliberation itself.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of re-framing confirmation bias as a potential *resource* rather than solely a *hindrance* in the context of LLM-supported deliberation is somewhat novel. The paper builds on existing work on the Argumentative Theory of Reasoning, applying it specifically to the challenges and opportunities presented by LLMs in group settings.  The three-step process is a practical contribution. Prior work has looked at LLMs as devil's advocates or persona models, but the specific framing of using LLMs to stimulate both confirmation bias *and* epistemic vigilance in a sequential, supportive way appears to be a contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in the integration of LLMs into collaborative tasks: the potential for conformity and the reduction of critical thinking. The authors rightly point out that LLMs, due to their persuasive abilities, can unintentionally stifle productive disagreement. By proposing a method to cultivate strong individual positions *before* group interaction, the paper aims to mitigate this risk. This approach has practical implications for designing LLM-supported collaborative platforms. If successful, this could make group deliberation more robust and lead to better decisions.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Strong theoretical grounding: The paper leverages the Argumentative Theory of Reasoning effectively to frame the problem and propose a solution.
    *   Practical framework: The three-step process provides a concrete roadmap for integrating LLMs into the deliberation process.
    *   Addresses a relevant problem: The conformity-inducing risk of LLMs in group settings is a growing concern.
    *   Clear and well-written.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Lack of empirical validation: The paper is primarily theoretical and conceptual. It doesn't include any empirical evidence to support the effectiveness of the proposed process.  There's no testing of the three-step approach.
    *   Simplification of social dynamics: The paper assumes a relatively straightforward social dynamic where individuals are genuinely interested in finding the "truth". It doesn't address situations where individuals are motivated by other factors (e.g., social status, political agendas) which might undermine the effectiveness of the approach.
    *   Potential for unintended consequences: The deliberate amplification of confirmation bias, even with epistemic provocation, could backfire if individuals become *more* entrenched in their positions and less willing to engage in genuine dialogue.
    *   Limited discussion of edge cases: The paper needs to be more nuanced in outlining scenarios where confirmation bias harnessing might be inappropriate or harmful, specifically outlining ethical considerations.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of LLM-supported collaborative systems and contribute to a more nuanced understanding of how cognitive biases can be strategically used to enhance group decision-making. It could also inspire further research into the interplay between LLMs, confirmation bias, and epistemic vigilance. The success hinges on whether the theoretical proposal proves effective in real-world scenarios.

**Justification:**

The paper offers a fresh perspective on using LLMs to improve group deliberation, but its lack of empirical validation significantly limits its impact. While the theoretical framework is sound and the proposed process is practical, the paper remains speculative. The risks associated with deliberately amplifying confirmation bias warrant careful consideration.  While the idea is important, without evidence, the practical impact is hard to gauge.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 6/10

### LLM Agents at the Roundtable: A Multi-Perspective and Dialectical Reasoning Framework for Essay Scoring
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14834v1)
- **Authors**: Jinhee Jang, Ayoung Moon, Minkyoung Jung, YoungBin Kim. Seung Jin Lee
- **Abstract**: The emergence of large language models (LLMs) has brought a new paradigm to automated essay scoring (AES), a long-standing and practical application of natural language processing in education. However, achieving human-level multi-perspective understanding and judgment remains a challenge. In this work, we propose Roundtable Essay Scoring (RES), a multi-agent evaluation framework designed to perform precise and human-aligned scoring under a zero-shot setting. RES constructs evaluator agents based on LLMs, each tailored to a specific prompt and topic context. Each agent independently generates a trait-based rubric and conducts a multi-perspective evaluation. Then, by simulating a roundtable-style discussion, RES consolidates individual evaluations through a dialectical reasoning process to produce a final holistic score that more closely aligns with human evaluation. By enabling collaboration and consensus among agents with diverse evaluation perspectives, RES outperforms prior zero-shot AES approaches. Experiments on the ASAP dataset using ChatGPT and Claude show that RES achieves up to a 34.86% improvement in average QWK over straightforward prompting (Vanilla) methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Roundtable Essay Scoring (RES), a multi-agent framework for zero-shot automated essay scoring (AES) using large language models (LLMs). RES employs multiple LLM-based agents, each with a specific persona and evaluation focus, to independently assess essays and generate trait-based rubrics. The agents then engage in a simulated roundtable discussion, where they exchange critiques and coordinate their judgments to arrive at a final, holistic score. Experiments on the ASAP dataset using ChatGPT and Claude demonstrate that RES outperforms existing zero-shot AES approaches, achieving significant improvements in Quadratic Weighted Kappa (QWK) scores.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach to zero-shot AES by leveraging multi-agent collaboration and dialectical reasoning. While multi-agent systems and LLM-based evaluation have been explored in other contexts, their application to AES with a focus on simulating a roundtable discussion is a unique contribution. The idea of incorporating diverse evaluation perspectives and then synthesizing them through collaborative reasoning to produce better human-aligned scores is a compelling idea.

*   **Significance:** Automated essay scoring is an important application in education. Human evaluation can be time-consuming and expensive, and LLMs provide a potential solution. By achieving improved performance over existing zero-shot methods, RES demonstrates the potential for LLMs to improve AES accuracy and alignment with human scoring.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The multi-agent framework allows for more comprehensive and nuanced essay evaluation compared to single-LLM approaches.
    *   The dialectical reasoning process enables agents to consider diverse perspectives and reach a consensus, leading to more reliable and human-aligned scores.
    *   The paper provides empirical evidence on a standard benchmark (ASAP dataset) to demonstrate the effectiveness of RES.
    *   The ablation studies offer valuable insights into the contributions of different components of RES (e.g., the number of agents, the inclusion of dialectical reasoning).

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on API-based proprietary language models raises concerns about cost and accessibility.
    *   The study focuses primarily on scoring, neglecting the potential for LLMs to generate informative feedback for students.
    *   The improvement is still from 0.364 to 0.483, for example using ChatGPT, which is not yet up to the standard of human grading.
    *   The study doesn't thoroughly discuss the computational cost or latency of the RES framework.

*   **Potential Impact:** The RES framework has the potential to influence future research in AES by promoting the use of multi-agent systems and dialectical reasoning. It also provides a practical solution for improving the accuracy and reliability of zero-shot AES systems.

*   **Justification:**
    *   The experimental setup is very standard; there is no element of challenge.
    *   The ablation studies are thorough and insightful.
    *   The paper lacks theoretical and practical justification of the method.
    *   The reliance on closed-source models is a concerning factor.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:**

The paper demonstrates a novel framework for AES that improves over current zero-shot approaches. While the benefits are clearly outlined and analyzed via ablation studies, the approach relies on expensive closed-source models, therefore limiting its real-world practical significance. There is also room for expanding the paper to contain a better analysis on feedback for the students. For these reasons, the paper is given a score of 6.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### [Re] Improving Interpretation Faithfulness for Vision Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14846v1)
- **Authors**: Izabela Kurek, Wojciech Trejter, Stipe Frkovic, Andro Erdelez
- **Abstract**: This work aims to reproduce the results of Faithful Vision Transformers (FViTs) proposed by arXiv:2311.17983 alongside interpretability methods for Vision Transformers from arXiv:2012.09838 and Xu (2022) et al. We investigate claims made by arXiv:2311.17983, namely that the usage of Diffusion Denoised Smoothing (DDS) improves interpretability robustness to (1) attacks in a segmentation task and (2) perturbation and attacks in a classification task. We also extend the original study by investigating the authors' claims that adding DDS to any interpretability method can improve its robustness under attack. This is tested on baseline methods and the recently proposed Attribution Rollout method. In addition, we measure the computational costs and environmental impact of obtaining an FViT through DDS. Our results broadly agree with the original study's findings, although minor discrepancies were found and discussed.
- **Summary**: This paper presents a reproduction and extension of the "Faithful Vision Transformers" (FViTs) research, which introduced Diffusion Denoised Smoothing (DDS) to improve the interpretability and robustness of Vision Transformers (ViTs). The authors reproduce the original experiments, confirming that DDS generally enhances model interpretability and resilience against adversarial attacks. They also extend the work by exploring DDS with other interpretability methods like Attribution Rollout and investigate the environmental impact (energy consumption) of using DDS. They report minor discrepancies compared to the original paper and highlight the substantial energy cost associated with DDS. Furthermore, they offer a critical assessment of the mathematical foundations and algorithmic aspects, suggesting refinements to some of the original theorems. The authors were unable to verify that some proposed theorems were correct, suggesting alternative definitions, and highlight instances that are not congruent with other interpretations.

**Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:**

The paper's primary contribution lies in its thorough reproduction and extension of existing research. While reproduction studies are vital for scientific rigor and reproducibility, they inherently lack significant novelty. The extensions, though valuable, are incremental.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Reproducibility:** The paper addresses a crucial aspect of scientific research - reproducibility. By replicating the original study's results, the authors validate the findings and identify potential inconsistencies.
    *   **Extension to Attribution Rollout:** Investigating DDS with Attribution Rollout (AR) adds a practical contribution, since there was no study examining DDS with AR.
    *   **Plug-in Validation:** The inclusion of baseline comparisons provides a more rigorous evaluation of the plug-in nature of DDS.
    *   **Environmental Impact Assessment:** The consideration of the environmental impact, which was missing from the original paper, is a valuable addition, highlighting the practical trade-offs of using DDS.
    *   **Theoretical Refinement:** The proposed refinements to the theoretical foundations contribute to a deeper understanding of FViTs.
    *   **Critical assessment of existing definitions and theorems:** The critical assessment of existing definitions and theorems allow for the scientific community to assess their validity with more ease.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Novelty:** The primary focus is on reproduction, limiting the overall novelty of the work.
    *   **Incremental Extensions:** The extensions are generally incremental, building upon existing methods rather than introducing entirely new concepts.
    *   **Discrepancies:** The discrepancies observed during the reproduction raise concerns about the robustness of the original findings and the transparency in reporting the experimental setup in the original paper.
    *   **Dependency on Existing Models:** The reliance on a specific diffusion model (class-unconditional ImageNet diffusion) limits the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Partial Reproducibility:** The authors mention that the official code release by the original researchers contains some inconsistencies, and is a now outdated release, with a newer version whose performance is unknown, making the reproducibility process complex.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance is in validating and refining existing knowledge rather than creating new paradigms. While not groundbreaking, such studies are essential for building a solid foundation for future research. The critical evaluation of the mathematical aspects adds value to the theoretical understanding of FViTs. The environmental impact assessment raises awareness about the practical limitations of DDS.

The main weakness of the study is that it focuses on re-doing and verifying existing work, rather than generating entirely new works. The strength of the study is that they uncover several inaccuracies in original work, and provide alternative directions of assessing this work.

**Score: 6**

**Justification:**

The score of 6 reflects the paper's valuable but limited contribution. The reproduction and extensions are well-executed and provide important insights into the FViTs and DDS. The identification of discrepancies and the environmental impact assessment are positive aspects. However, the lack of significant novelty and the incremental nature of the extensions prevent the paper from achieving a higher score. Also, it must be stated that a score lower than 5 would undervalue the study's contribution. The study should be praised for its honesty and accuracy in reporting several inaccuracies. The study should be also praised for reporting on environmental impact, which is often under-reported in machine learning studies. The theoretical contributions also allow other researchers to further validate or deny aspects of FViTs. While the study does not provide entirely new theory, the critique on existing theory allows for the scientific community to progress.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Empathy-R1: A Chain-of-Empathy and Reinforcement Learning Framework for Long-Form Mental Health Support
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14851v1)
- **Authors**: Xianrong Yao, Dong She, Chenxu Zhang, Yimeng Zhang, Yueru Sun, Noman Ahmed, Yang Gao, Zhanpeng Jin
- **Abstract**: Empathy is critical for effective mental health support, especially when addressing Long Counseling Texts (LCTs). However, existing Large Language Models (LLMs) often generate replies that are semantically fluent but lack the structured reasoning necessary for genuine psychological support, particularly in a Chinese context. To bridge this gap, we introduce Empathy-R1, a novel framework that integrates a Chain-of-Empathy (CoE) reasoning process with Reinforcement Learning (RL) to enhance response quality for LCTs. Inspired by cognitive-behavioral therapy, our CoE paradigm guides the model to sequentially reason about a help-seeker's emotions, causes, and intentions, making its thinking process both transparent and interpretable. Our framework is empowered by a new large-scale Chinese dataset, Empathy-QA, and a two-stage training process. First, Supervised Fine-Tuning instills the CoE's reasoning structure. Subsequently, RL, guided by a dedicated reward model, refines the therapeutic relevance and contextual appropriateness of the final responses. Experiments show that Empathy-R1 achieves strong performance on key automatic metrics. More importantly, human evaluations confirm its superiority, showing a clear preference over strong baselines and achieving a Win@1 rate of 44.30% on our new benchmark. By enabling interpretable and contextually nuanced responses, Empathy-R1 represents a significant advancement in developing responsible and genuinely beneficial AI for mental health support.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Empathy-R1: A Chain-of-Empathy and Reinforcement Learning Framework for Long-Form Mental Health Support."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Empathy-R1, a novel framework designed to improve the empathetic quality of responses generated by Large Language Models (LLMs) when providing mental health support through Long Counseling Texts (LCTs).  Empathy-R1 integrates a Chain-of-Empathy (CoE) reasoning process, inspired by cognitive-behavioral therapy (CBT), with Reinforcement Learning (RL). The CoE framework guides the model to sequentially reason about a help-seeker's emotions, causes, and intentions. The framework is trained using a new large-scale Chinese dataset, Empathy-QA, through a two-stage process: Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) to instill the CoE's structure, followed by RL guided by a dedicated reward model to refine therapeutic relevance.  Experiments demonstrate Empathy-R1's superior performance compared to existing baselines, as confirmed by human evaluations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a relevant and important problem:** The paper tackles the challenge of generating genuinely empathetic and therapeutic responses in mental health support, particularly within the context of long-form counseling texts and the Chinese language. This addresses a crucial gap in existing LLM applications, where semantic fluency often doesn't translate into meaningful psychological support.
    *   **Novel Chain-of-Empathy (CoE) framework:** The CoE paradigm, inspired by CBT principles, provides a structured and interpretable reasoning process for LLMs. This is a significant departure from generic, black-box LLM approaches and offers a valuable framework for developing more responsible AI in mental health. The four-layered approach (Emotions/Context, Causes/Beliefs, Intent Analysis, Response Strategy) is well-defined and grounded in psychological theory.
    *   **High-quality dataset (Empathy-QA):** The creation and release of a large-scale, contemporary Chinese dataset for LCTs is a major contribution. The Empathy-QA dataset addresses the scarcity of resources in this area and provides a valuable benchmark for future research. It also directly addresses the issue of western bias in LLMs for a Chinese context.
    *   **Rigorous experimental evaluation:** The paper employs a combination of automatic metrics and human evaluations to assess the performance of Empathy-R1. The use of multi-reference evaluation is a robust and insightful approach. Human evaluation emphasizes holistic judgement, and not just some individual facets of 'empathy'. The clear, significant win rates versus strong baselines strongly indicate superiority.
    *   **Ablation studies are well-designed and insightful:** These highlight the contribution of each component of the framework and offer an understanding of their synergistic effects.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Generalizability:** The framework and dataset are heavily tailored to the Chinese context. While this allows for targeted support, further research is needed to assess its applicability and adaptability to other languages and cultural contexts. Although the techniques should generally be transferable, the reward model and dataset (and potentially even the initial SFT) could need to be completely retrained.
    *   **Potential for harm:** As with any AI-based mental health support system, there is a risk of providing inappropriate or harmful advice. Although the paper highlights the emphasis on interpretable and contextually nuanced responses, it is critical to further evaluate the framework's potential biases and unintended consequences. The model is trained to mimic existing therapist responses, and biases in those responses could be reproduced.
    *   **Reliance on pre-trained models:** Although the use of Qwen3-8B as a backbone allows it to build effectively, it would be even more novel if they also showed that the whole framework could be applied in conjunction with training the pre-trained model as well.
    *   **Limited explanation for Reward Model:** The Reward model described in section 3.3 does not give adequate detail about how to create a good reward model, especially regarding where the negative samples were created, and the impact of the margin.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The structured reasoning framework (CoE) integrated with RL represents a significant step forward in developing empathetic LLMs for mental health support.
    *   The creation and release of Empathy-QA will enable further research and development in this field.
    *   The human evaluation provides strong evidence for the effectiveness of Empathy-R1 and its potential to provide genuinely helpful support.
    *   However, the reliance on pre-trained models, and the strong tailoring to the chinese-language market reduces the novelty somewhat, and makes transfer of the specific model to other markets difficult without significant further investment.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

Empathy-R1 addresses a critical gap in AI-based mental health support by introducing a structured reasoning framework and a high-quality dataset for Chinese LCTs. The human evaluation strongly validates its superior performance and genuine empathetic qualities. The main weaknesses are the limited generalizability (strong localisation to Chinese context) and the reliance on pre-trained models. The risk of generating harmful advice also limits the score, but this concern is present for any model and should be managed by appropriate regulatory frameworks. Overall, the paper makes a significant contribution to the field and demonstrates the potential of structured reasoning in developing more responsible and beneficial AI for mental health support.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### CodeFuse-CR-Bench: A Comprehensiveness-aware Benchmark for End-to-End Code Review Evaluation in Python Projects
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14856v1)
- **Authors**: Hanyang Guo, Xunjin Zheng, Zihan Liao, Hang Yu, Peng DI, Ziyin Zhang, Hong-Ning Dai
- **Abstract**: Automated code review (CR) is a key application for Large Language Models (LLMs), but progress is hampered by a "reality gap": existing benchmarks evaluate models on isolated sub-tasks using simplified, context-poor data. This fails to reflect the holistic context-rich nature of real-world CR. To bridge this gap, we introduce CodeFuse-CR-Bench, the first comprehensiveness-aware benchmark for repository-level CR evaluation. CodeFuse-CR-Bench comprises 601 high-quality instances from 70 Python projects covering nine Pull-Request (PR) problem domains, where each instance provides rich, multi-faceted context including the associated issue, PR details, and repository state, enabling end-to-end evaluation. Beyond superficial metrics, we also propose a novel evaluation framework that combines rule-based checks for location and syntax with model-based judgments of review quality. We present the first large-scale assessment of state-of-the-art LLMs on this comprehensive CR task. Our results establish crucial baselines and reveal that (1) no single LLM dominates all aspects of CR; (2) Gemini 2.5 Pro achieves the highest comprehensive performance; and (3) different LLMs exhibit varying robustness to redundant context. These findings highlight the necessity of holistic, multi-dimensional evaluation and provide actionable insights for advancing truly intelligent yet practical CR assistants.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CodeFuse-CR-Bench: A Comprehensiveness-aware Benchmark for End-to-End Code Review Evaluation in Python Projects":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CodeFuse-CR-Bench, a new benchmark designed to address the "reality gap" in automated code review (CR) evaluation.  Existing benchmarks often evaluate models on isolated sub-tasks with limited context, failing to capture the holistic, context-rich nature of real-world CR. CodeFuse-CR-Bench comprises 601 instances from 70 Python projects, providing rich context including associated issues, PR details, and repository state.  The authors propose a novel evaluation framework combining rule-based checks for location and syntax with model-based judgments of review quality. They perform a large-scale assessment of state-of-the-art LLMs on this benchmark, revealing that no single LLM dominates all aspects of CR and that different models exhibit varying robustness to redundant context.  The authors argue that their benchmark enables more realistic end-to-end evaluation and provides actionable insights for advancing CR assistants.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in creating a *comprehensive*, repository-level benchmark for code review.  While existing benchmarks exist for various aspects of CR (comment generation, code refinement, etc.), CodeFuse-CR-Bench attempts to capture the entire CR workflow, including understanding the initial problem, locating issues, and formulating a coherent review, all within a rich contextual setting. This focus on comprehensiveness, including PR and issue information, is a significant step forward. The novel evaluation framework is also a noteworthy contribution, moving beyond simple text similarity metrics to include location accuracy and model-based quality assessment.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance is that it highlights and addresses a crucial limitation of current automated CR research.  By creating a more realistic evaluation environment, the authors enable researchers to develop and evaluate models that are more likely to be effective in real-world settings. The empirical results demonstrate the limitations of current LLMs on the comprehensive CR task and highlight the importance of multi-dimensional evaluation. This will likely influence the direction of future research in automated CR.  The insights regarding the impact of different contexts are particularly valuable.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Benchmark:** The major strength is the creation and public availability of CodeFuse-CR-Bench, which includes rich contextual information that is often missing in existing benchmarks.
    *   **Rigorous Evaluation Framework:**  The combined rule-based and model-based evaluation approach offers a more holistic assessment of CR quality compared to relying solely on text similarity metrics or human judgment.
    *   **Extensive Experiments:** The paper provides a comprehensive evaluation of several state-of-the-art LLMs, establishing crucial baselines and revealing key insights.
    *   **Clear Problem Statement and Justification:** The paper clearly articulates the "reality gap" in automated CR evaluation and provides a strong rationale for the need for a more comprehensive benchmark.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Programming Language Limitation:** The benchmark is limited to Python, which restricts its generalizability to other programming languages.  The authors acknowledge this limitation and plan to address it in future work.
    *   **Potential Bias in Manual Selection:** The manual selection and annotation process, while necessary to ensure high quality, may introduce bias. The authors attempt to mitigate this by involving two experienced developers and using a detailed questionnaire, but the potential for subjective judgment remains.
    *   **Dependency on Existing LLMs for Evaluation:** The model-based evaluation component relies on existing LLMs (reward model, LLM-as-a-judge), which may have their own biases and limitations.  The authors acknowledge this and carefully design their prompts to mitigate potential issues, but the validity of the evaluation is still contingent on the performance of these LLMs.
    *   **Limited exploration of hyperparameter tuning:** Given the complex task at hand, the exploration of LLM hyperparameters for the LLMs included in the evaluation, such as the influence of Temperature or top-p, is relatively limited. It can have an impact on the study's findings if models are evaluated using sub-optimal parameters.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to have a significant influence on the field of automated CR, as it provides a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners. The benchmark will enable more realistic evaluation of CR models and the insights from the empirical study will inform future research directions. The focus on comprehensiveness is likely to become a key consideration in the development of future CR tools.

*   **Justification for Score:**  While the benchmark is currently limited to Python, the comprehensive nature, novel evaluation framework, and extensive empirical study justify a high score. The weaknesses are acknowledged by the authors and represent areas for future improvement.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### Exploring the Global-to-Local Attention Scheme in Graph Transformers: An Empirical Study
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14863v1)
- **Authors**: Zhengwei Wang, Gang Wu
- **Abstract**: Graph Transformers (GTs) show considerable potential in graph representation learning. The architecture of GTs typically integrates Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) with global attention mechanisms either in parallel or as a precursor to attention mechanisms, yielding a local-and-global or local-to-global attention scheme. However, as the global attention mechanism primarily captures long-range dependencies between nodes, these integration schemes may suffer from information loss, where the local neighborhood information learned by GNN could be diluted by the attention mechanism. Therefore, we propose G2LFormer, featuring a novel global-to-local attention scheme where the shallow network layers use attention mechanisms to capture global information, while the deeper layers employ GNN modules to learn local structural information, thereby preventing nodes from ignoring their immediate neighbors. An effective cross-layer information fusion strategy is introduced to allow local layers to retain beneficial information from global layers and alleviate information loss, with acceptable trade-offs in scalability. To validate the feasibility of the global-to-local attention scheme, we compare G2LFormer with state-of-the-art linear GTs and GNNs on node-level and graph-level tasks. The results indicate that G2LFormer exhibits excellent performance while keeping linear complexity.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces G2LFormer, a graph transformer architecture featuring a global-to-local attention scheme.  Unlike existing graph transformers that use local-and-global or local-to-global attention mechanisms, G2LFormer employs global attention layers in the shallow parts of the network to capture long-range dependencies and GNN layers in the deeper parts to learn local structural information. To mitigate information loss when transitioning from global to local layers, a cross-layer information fusion strategy called NOSAF is used. The authors compare G2LFormer with state-of-the-art linear graph transformers and GNNs on node-level and graph-level tasks, demonstrating superior performance while maintaining linear complexity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the global-to-local attention scheme and the implementation of the NOSAF cross-layer fusion strategy within this architecture. The idea of starting with global context and then refining with local structure is conceptually interesting. Existing GT methods often prioritize local followed by global (or concurrent local and global), and this proposes the reverse. The NOSAF strategy addresses a key potential weakness: information dilution from global attention overshadowing local structure.

*   **Significance:** The significance is twofold. First, it directly addresses a perceived limitation of existing graph transformers by reversing the typical attention flow. Second, demonstrating state-of-the-art or highly competitive performance with linear complexity is a valuable contribution. Scaling graph transformers is an active area of research, and this provides a different architectural approach.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the over-globalizing problem in existing GTs.
    *   **Novel Architecture:** The global-to-local attention scheme is a distinct architectural choice.
    *   **Effective Information Fusion:** The NOSAF strategy is presented as a key component for preserving information across layers.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides comprehensive experimental results across multiple datasets, comparing G2LFormer with strong baselines.
    *   **Linear Complexity:** Maintains linear complexity, a crucial consideration for large graphs.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While the architecture is novel, the components themselves (attention, GNN layers) are not. The NOSAF strategy, although beneficial, builds upon existing cross-layer fusion techniques. It would be beneficial to clarify how it differentiates with alternatives (such as [29] to a greater detail.
    *   **Choice of Attention and GNN Variants:** The paper uses SGFormer's attention and Cluster-GCN/GatedGCN. While they are well-established and linear, the performance could be strongly affected by these design choices.
    *   **Ablation Limitations:** The ablation study only considers a subset of models and datasets which prevents one from drawing strong conclusions.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the global-to-local attention scheme proves robust and generalizable, it could influence future graph transformer design.  It offers a potential pathway for balancing global context with local structure.  The NOSAF strategy may also be adopted in other architectures requiring cross-layer information fusion.

*   **Overall Assessment:** The paper introduces a worthwhile architectural variation in graph transformers and provides empirical evidence supporting its effectiveness. While the components aren't radically new, the overall configuration and the fusion strategy merit attention.

Score: 7

**Justification:**

I assign a score of 7 because while the paper presents a sound and empirically validated method, it is an incremental, rather than revolutionary, step in the field. The architectural innovation of "global-to-local" is interesting and potentially valuable, but the building blocks are borrowed and well-known. The paper demonstrates a clear understanding of the existing challenges in graph transformers and provides a compelling solution. The thorough experiments strengthen the claims. However, the work relies heavily on existing components, limiting its overall novelty and significance. Despite this, the combination of ideas presented provides a significant contribution to the literature.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Controllable Localized Face Anonymization Via Diffusion Inpainting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14866v1)
- **Authors**: Ali Salar, Qing Liu, Guoying Zhao
- **Abstract**: The growing use of portrait images in computer vision highlights the need to protect personal identities. At the same time, anonymized images must remain useful for downstream computer vision tasks. In this work, we propose a unified framework that leverages the inpainting ability of latent diffusion models to generate realistic anonymized images. Unlike prior approaches, we have complete control over the anonymization process by designing an adaptive attribute-guidance module that applies gradient correction during the reverse denoising process, aligning the facial attributes of the generated image with those of the synthesized target image. Our framework also supports localized anonymization, allowing users to specify which facial regions are left unchanged. Extensive experiments conducted on the public CelebA-HQ and FFHQ datasets show that our method outperforms state-of-the-art approaches while requiring no additional model training. The source code is available on our page.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework for controllable and localized face anonymization using diffusion inpainting.  It leverages the inpainting capabilities of latent diffusion models (specifically Stable Diffusion) and proposes an adaptive attribute-guidance module. This module performs gradient correction during the reverse denoising process, aligning the facial attributes of the generated image with those of a synthesized target image. Unlike prior methods, the proposed framework provides users with fine-grained control over the anonymization process, allowing them to specify which facial regions remain unchanged and what attributes should be modified. The method is evaluated on CelebA-HQ and FFHQ datasets and is shown to outperform existing methods in terms of anonymization effectiveness and image quality, without requiring additional training.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Controllability:**  The primary strength lies in the controllable and localized anonymization capabilities. Previous methods often lacked fine-grained control, either modifying the entire face or providing only coarse adjustments.  The adaptive attribute-guidance module is a significant contribution, enabling users to manipulate specific facial attributes and expressions.
    *   **Image Quality:** By utilizing Stable Diffusion, the method produces high-quality, realistic anonymized images, mitigating the utility degradation associated with traditional anonymization techniques. The experiments demonstrate improved FID and visual DNA scores compared to existing methods.
    *   **No Additional Training:** The fact that the method leverages pre-trained models and doesn't require additional training is a significant advantage. This reduces computational cost and allows for easier adaptation to new datasets.
    *   **Localized Anonymization:** The ability to preserve specific facial regions (e.g., eyes, lips) while anonymizing others is a valuable feature, particularly in sensitive applications like medical imaging.
    *   **Clear problem definition and novel contribution:** The paper starts by defining the problem clearly, and highlights the limitations in previous works. The proposed method addresses these limitation by introducing the adaptive attribute-guidance module.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on Target Image:** The framework relies on a target image to guide attribute control. While the paper argues this avoids ethical concerns related to face swapping, it introduces a dependency that might limit practicality in scenarios where a suitable target image isn't readily available. While the target is synthesized, this represents a synthetic bias. This dependency might hinder broader adoption.
    *   **Limited Novelty in Core Diffusion Technique:** While the application of diffusion models to face anonymization is not entirely new, existing methods lack the same level of control. There is novelty in the proposed approach.
    *   **Reliance on Face Parsing Model:** The method relies on a face parsing model for masking, which introduces another potential source of error if the face parsing is inaccurate.
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:** While Re-ID rate, FID, V-DNA, and SSIM are standard metrics, further evaluation of the *utility* of the anonymized images for downstream tasks (e.g., face recognition in different expressions, medical diagnosis) would strengthen the claims.

*   **Significance and Novelty:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of face anonymization by introducing a highly controllable and localized method that leverages the power of diffusion models. The adaptive attribute-guidance module is a novel contribution, enabling fine-grained control over facial features and expressions. The no-training requirement makes the method practical and accessible. The significance also lies in its potential for use in sensitive applications where privacy must be balanced with usability.

*   **Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of 7. The paper has several strong points, including its controllability, image quality, and no-training requirement. However, the dependency on a target image and the reliance on an accurate face parsing model are limitations.  While the framework leverages existing diffusion models, the introduction of the adaptive attribute-guidance module and the localized anonymization capabilities represents a novel and impactful contribution to the field. Evaluation could have been improved by incorporating performance in downstream task to better reflect the utility of the method.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Multi-To-One Interview Paradigm for Efficient MLLM Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14886v1)
- **Authors**: Ye Shen, Junying Wang, Farong Wen, Yijin Guo, Qi Jia, Zicheng Zhang, Guangtao Zhai
- **Abstract**: The rapid progress of Multi-Modal Large Language Models (MLLMs) has spurred the creation of numerous benchmarks. However, conventional full-coverage Question-Answering evaluations suffer from high redundancy and low efficiency. Inspired by human interview processes, we propose a multi-to-one interview paradigm for efficient MLLM evaluation. Our framework consists of (i) a two-stage interview strategy with pre-interview and formal interview phases, (ii) dynamic adjustment of interviewer weights to ensure fairness, and (iii) an adaptive mechanism for question difficulty-level chosen. Experiments on different benchmarks show that the proposed paradigm achieves significantly higher correlation with full-coverage results than random sampling, with improvements of up to 17.6% in PLCC and 16.7% in SRCC, while reducing the number of required questions. These findings demonstrate that the proposed paradigm provides a reliable and efficient alternative for large-scale MLLM benchmarking.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a "multi-to-one interview paradigm" for efficiently evaluating Multi-Modal Large Language Models (MLLMs). This paradigm draws inspiration from human interview processes. It consists of three main components: a two-stage interview (pre-interview for difficulty calibration and formal interview for in-depth assessment), dynamic adjustment of interviewer weights to ensure fairness, and an adaptive difficulty mechanism to tailor question difficulty based on the model's performance. The authors demonstrate that this paradigm achieves higher correlation with full-coverage evaluations than random sampling, while significantly reducing the number of questions needed.  Experiments are conducted on MMT-Bench, ScienceQA, and SEED-Bench.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novelty:** The interview paradigm is a relatively novel approach to MLLM evaluation.  It moves away from the standard full-coverage Q&A testing. The analogy to human interviews is insightful.
    *   **Efficiency:** The core argument of increased efficiency is compelling. The demonstrated reduction in questions while maintaining, or even improving, correlation with full-coverage results is a significant advantage.
    *   **Fairness:** The dynamic adjustment of interviewer weights and adaptive difficulty levels contribute to a potentially fairer evaluation process, mitigating biases from individual interviewers or fixed question sets.
    *   **Experimental Results:** The experiments on several well-established benchmarks (MMT-Bench, ScienceQA, SEED-Bench) provide strong empirical support for the effectiveness of the proposed paradigm.  The reported improvements in PLCC and SRCC are quantifiable and demonstrate the value of the method.
    *   **Well-defined Components:** The three key components (two-stage interview, dynamic weighting, adaptive difficulty) are clearly defined and contribute logically to the overall framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Complexity:**  The interview paradigm introduces more complexity than simple full-coverage Q&A.  The implementation of the interviewer weights, difficulty adjustment, and two-stage process requires careful tuning of parameters (α, β, n) and potentially introduces new sources of error.
    *   **Limited Generalizability:**  While the paper presents positive results on the tested benchmarks, the generalizability to other types of MLLM tasks or evaluation scenarios isn't fully explored. The benchmarks used are all Q&A based.
    *   **Subjectivity/Automation:** While attempting to be less subjective, the paradigm still relies on interviewer judgments (even if aggregated and weighted). The paper could benefit from a discussion on how to further automate the judgment process to reduce human bias further or how the interviewers were standardized and trained. The "relatively simple selection of interviewers" is a weakness acknowledged in the conclusion.
    *   **Lack of theoretical justification:** There is no theoretical justification of why this interview paradigm should work. A formal analysis of the information content of a question and redundancy between questions would greatly strengthen the paper.

*   **Significance:**

    The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of MLLM evaluation by:
    *   Offering a more efficient and potentially fairer method for benchmarking.
    *   Inspiring further research into adaptive and interactive evaluation paradigms.
    *   Providing a framework that can be adapted and extended to address specific challenges in MLLM evaluation.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a novel and potentially valuable approach to MLLM evaluation. The experimental results are compelling, and the proposed paradigm addresses important limitations of existing methods. However, the increased complexity and potential for subjectivity are areas that require further attention. The absence of theoretical grounding is also a limitation.

**Score: 7**

*Justification:* A score of 7 reflects the strengths of the paper in terms of novelty, efficiency, and experimental validation. However, it also accounts for the weaknesses related to complexity, limited generalizability, subjectivity/automation, and the lack of theoretical underpinning. The paper is a good contribution, but more work is required to increase its robustness and impact.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Leveraging Reinforcement Learning, Genetic Algorithms and Transformers for background determination in particle physics
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14894v1)
- **Authors**: Guillermo Hijano Mendizabal, Davide Lancierini, Alex Marshall, Andrea Mauri, Patrick Haworth Owen, Mitesh Patel, Konstantinos Petridis, Shah Rukh Qasim, Nicola Serra, William Sutcliffe, Hanae Tilquin
- **Abstract**: Experimental studies of beauty hadron decays face significant challenges due to a wide range of backgrounds arising from the numerous possible decay channels with similar final states. For a particular signal decay, the process for ascertaining the most relevant background processes necessitates a detailed analysis of final state particles, potential misidentifications, and kinematic overlaps, which, due to computational limitations, is restricted to the simulation of only the most relevant backgrounds. Moreover, this process typically relies on the physicist's intuition and expertise, as no systematic method exists. This paper has two primary goals. First, from a particle physics perspective, we present a novel approach that utilises Reinforcement Learning (RL) to overcome the aforementioned challenges by systematically determining the critical backgrounds affecting beauty hadron decay measurements. While beauty hadron physics serves as the case study in this work, the proposed strategy is broadly adaptable to other types of particle physics measurements. Second, from a Machine Learning perspective, we introduce a novel algorithm which exploits the synergy between RL and Genetic Algorithms (GAs) for environments with highly sparse rewards and a large trajectory space. This strategy leverages GAs to efficiently explore the trajectory space and identify successful trajectories, which are used to guide the RL agent's training. Our method also incorporates a transformer architecture for the RL agent to handle token sequences representing decays.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel AI-driven method to automatically determine the relevant background processes in particle physics analyses, using beauty hadron decays as a case study.  It formulates the background determination problem as a sequential decision-making task, enabling the use of Reinforcement Learning (RL). The RL agent learns to construct candidate background decays as sequences of tokens. To address the challenges of a large trajectory space and episodes where feedback is provided only at the end, the authors utilize and adapt the AlphaZero algorithm. Furthermore, to tackle the sparse reward problem, a Genetic Algorithm (GA) is employed to discover successful action trajectories, serving as expert demonstrations for the RL agent and improving learning efficiency. Two distinct approaches for leveraging GA-discovered demonstrations are explored: Policy Gradient with Supervised Updates (PGSU) and Periodic Expert Guidance (PEG).  The method is tested on a toy scenario, achieving beyond-expert performance after fine-tuning. The authors also provide visualizations of the learned embeddings, revealing that the model captures both structural and compositional differences in its internal representations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a genuinely interesting and potentially useful application of AI techniques to a challenging problem in particle physics. The core strength of the work lies in its attempt to automate a task that is traditionally heavily reliant on expert intuition and manual analysis. Here's a breakdown of the paper's merits and shortcomings regarding novelty and significance:

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty of Application:**  The application of RL, particularly the AlphaZero algorithm (with modifications for a single-player setting), to background determination in particle physics appears to be novel. This is a distinct application, and the paper explicitly mentions the lack of previous attempts in this area, adding to the paper's originality. While some related works utilize RL in detector design or particle tracking, automated background selection represents a new frontier.
*   **Integration of Techniques:**  The synergistic approach combining RL and GAs is well-motivated and addresses a crucial weakness of RL in sparse reward environments. The GA component demonstrably enhances exploration and guides the RL agent toward promising solutions.  The addition of customized variation mechanisms to the GA, to address the particularities of the problem is interesting.
*   **Transformer Architecture:** The use of a transformer-based architecture for representing decay chains as token sequences is appropriate and leverages the strengths of transformers in handling sequential data and capturing dependencies.
*   **Clear Problem Formulation:** The paper clearly defines the problem, the challenges, and the proposed solution. The toy scenario, while simplified, provides a proof-of-concept for the method's effectiveness.
*   **Demonstrated Improvement:**  The experimental results demonstrate that the proposed method can achieve beyond-expert performance, indicating that it can potentially identify relevant backgrounds that human experts might miss.
*   **Careful Implementation:** Exploration of two distinct demonstration-based training methodologies (PGSU and PEG) and careful fine-tuning steps demonstrate a meticulous approach to training and evaluating their RL agents.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Simplification of the Toy Scenario:** While the toy scenario is helpful for demonstrating the basic principles, it is significantly simplified compared to the complexity of real-world particle physics analyses.  The features used in the reward function are relatively crude (e.g., using a constant misidentification penalty instead of modeling it).  It remains to be seen how well the method will scale to more realistic scenarios with a much larger number of particles, decay channels, and detector effects.  The reward function also needs to be refined for real-world deployment.
*   **Limited Evaluation of Scalability:** The experiments focus on a relatively small set of training and generalization signals. While the results are promising, they do not provide strong evidence for the scalability of the method to much larger and more diverse datasets of beauty hadron decays or other particle physics processes.  Furthermore, one should assess the limitations and performance in environments with a much greater complexity.
*   **Lack of Comparison to Existing Heuristics:** While the paper argues against existing methods relying on human intuition, a more direct comparison to commonly used heuristics or simplified systematic methods would provide stronger validation. It is stated that there is no method like this in existence but more details can be provided with the commonly used heuristics employed and comparison of results.
*   **Limited Insight into Generalization:**  While the results demonstrate some generalization ability, a more in-depth analysis of the factors that contribute to successful generalization would be valuable. For example, what characteristics of the training data are most important for achieving good performance on unseen signals? How robust is the method to variations in the detector simulation or experimental conditions? One should explore the limitations in generalizing to other experiments or data-sets, i.e. experiments with different parameters.
*   **Absence of Real-World Application:** The validation on the "toy scenario" needs to be complemented by testing and validation on real experimental datasets or more realistic simulation datasets, ideally with comparisons to established methodologies. Without such analysis, it's difficult to gauge the true applicability and utility of the proposed approach.
*   **Minor algorithmic points:** Certain algorithm points such as the use of the "best achievable performance" in results needs to be more specified and defined with the reasons it is used.

**Significance:**

Despite the limitations, the paper represents a significant step toward automating background determination in particle physics. If the method can be successfully scaled to more realistic scenarios, it has the potential to accelerate the analysis workflow, reduce the risk of human error, and potentially lead to the discovery of new physics. This kind of approach could find applications far beyond particle physics where sparse rewards and large search spaces pose a challenge.

**Justification of Score:**

Taking the strengths and weaknesses into consideration, the paper demonstrates both novelty and potential impact. The paper has a clear vision of future development and application and should help expand the field of particle physics model analysis.
However, the reliance on a toy scenario and the lack of extensive validation on real-world data limit the paper's immediate impact and credibility. The degree of simplification compared to real-world scenarios also reduces the paper's novelty.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### CARGO: A Framework for Confidence-Aware Routing of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14899v1)
- **Authors**: Amine Barrak, Yosr Fourati, Michael Olchawa, Emna Ksontini, Khalil Zoghlami
- **Abstract**: As large language models (LLMs) proliferate in scale, specialization, and latency profiles, the challenge of routing user prompts to the most appropriate model has become increasingly critical for balancing performance and cost. We introduce CARGO (Category-Aware Routing with Gap-based Optimization), a lightweight, confidence-aware framework for dynamic LLM selection. CARGO employs a single embedding-based regressor trained on LLM-judged pairwise comparisons to predict model performance, with an optional binary classifier invoked when predictions are uncertain. This two-stage design enables precise, cost-aware routing without the need for human-annotated supervision. To capture domain-specific behavior, CARGO also supports category-specific regressors trained across five task groups: mathematics, coding, reasoning, summarization, and creative writing. Evaluated on four competitive LLMs (GPT-4o, Claude 3.5 Sonnet, DeepSeek V3, and Perplexity Sonar), CARGO achieves a top-1 routing accuracy of 76.4% and win rates ranging from 72% to 89% against individual experts. These results demonstrate that confidence-guided, lightweight routing can achieve expert-level performance with minimal overhead, offering a practical solution for real-world, multi-model LLM deployments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the CARGO paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CARGO, a lightweight, confidence-aware framework for dynamically routing user prompts to the most appropriate Large Language Model (LLM) within a multi-model deployment. CARGO uses a single embedding-based regressor trained on LLM-judged pairwise comparisons to predict model performance, augmented with a binary classifier for situations where the regressor's predictions are uncertain.  This two-stage design avoids the need for human-annotated supervision and allows for precise, cost-aware routing. CARGO also supports category-specific regressors, trained across mathematical, coding, reasoning, summarization, and creative writing tasks.  Evaluations across four competitive LLMs (GPT-4o, Claude 3.5 Sonnet, DeepSeek V3, Perplexity Sonar) demonstrated a top-1 routing accuracy of 76.4% and win rates ranging from 72% to 89% against individual expert models.  The authors argue that CARGO achieves expert-level performance with minimal overhead, providing a practical solution for real-world LLM deployments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**

    * **Lightweight and efficient:** The reliance on embedding-based regressors and LLM-judged comparisons makes CARGO computationally efficient and reduces the need for expensive human annotation. This is a significant advantage over methods that rely on large, labeled datasets or sequential cascade strategies.
    * **Confidence-Aware Routing:** Incorporating a binary classifier to resolve uncertain predictions from the regressor enhances the accuracy of LLM selection, allowing the system to make more informed decisions without always resorting to the most powerful (and expensive) model.
    * **Category-Specific Adaptation:** The ability to train category-specific regressors allows CARGO to tailor its routing decisions to the specific task at hand, improving performance across diverse domains.
    * **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper presents comprehensive experimental results demonstrating the effectiveness of CARGO across various LLMs and task categories, showing significant improvements over individual expert models.
    * **Scalability Demonstrated:** The addition of a new expert and a new domain show CARGO's extensibility.

* **Weaknesses:**

    * **Reliance on LLM Judges:** While the use of LLM-judged comparisons reduces the need for human annotation, it introduces a dependence on the quality and biases of the LLM judges. The authors acknowledge this and attempt to mitigate it through multiple judges and reporting agreement scores, but the potential for bias remains a concern.
    * **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** The evaluation is limited to five task categories and four LLMs. Further evaluation across a wider range of tasks, models, and real-world applications would strengthen the generalizability of the findings. Specifically the models are selected based on early 2025 market.
    * **Binary Classifier Improvement:** While the binary classifier provides an increase, an explanation of cases where it made incorrect choices could improve the analysis.

* **Novelty and Significance:**

The novelty of the paper lies in its unique combination of techniques to achieve efficient and accurate LLM routing. The confidence-aware approach, using a regressor-classifier cascade guided by LLM-judged comparisons, represents a practical and scalable solution to the challenges of multi-model LLM deployments. While other routing frameworks exist, CARGO distinguishes itself through its lightweight nature, minimal supervision requirements, and adaptability to diverse task categories. The paper addresses a growing need for intelligent resource allocation in the LLM landscape, where balancing performance, cost, and accuracy is crucial. The modular design and demonstrated extensibility suggest it can be easily adapted to new LLMs or domains.

**Justification for Score:**

CARGO presents a well-engineered solution with demonstrable advantages over existing routing approaches, especially in terms of its light-weight nature and minimal supervision costs. The empirical results are solid and clearly demonstrate the system's effectiveness. While the reliance on LLM judges is a potential weakness, the authors have taken reasonable steps to mitigate this concern. The paper's significance comes from its potential to improve the efficiency and accessibility of LLM-powered applications in real-world deployments.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Comparative Evaluation of Large Language Models for Persian Sentiment Analysis and Emotion Detection in Social Media Texts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14922v1)
- **Authors**: Kian Tohidi, Kia Dashtipour, Simone Rebora, Sevda Pourfaramarz
- **Abstract**: This study presents a comprehensive comparative evaluation of four state-of-the-art Large Language Models (LLMs)--Claude 3.7 Sonnet, DeepSeek-V3, Gemini 2.0 Flash, and GPT-4o--for sentiment analysis and emotion detection in Persian social media texts. Comparative analysis among LLMs has witnessed a significant rise in recent years, however, most of these analyses have been conducted on English language tasks, creating gaps in understanding cross-linguistic performance patterns. This research addresses these gaps through rigorous experimental design using balanced Persian datasets containing 900 texts for sentiment analysis (positive, negative, neutral) and 1,800 texts for emotion detection (anger, fear, happiness, hate, sadness, surprise). The main focus was to allow for a direct and fair comparison among different models, by using consistent prompts, uniform processing parameters, and by analyzing the performance metrics such as precision, recall, F1-scores, along with misclassification patterns. The results show that all models reach an acceptable level of performance, and a statistical comparison of the best three models indicates no significant differences among them. However, GPT-4o demonstrated a marginally higher raw accuracy value for both tasks, while Gemini 2.0 Flash proved to be the most cost-efficient. The findings indicate that the emotion detection task is more challenging for all models compared to the sentiment analysis task, and the misclassification patterns can represent some challenges in Persian language texts. These findings establish performance benchmarks for Persian NLP applications and offer practical guidance for model selection based on accuracy, efficiency, and cost considerations, while revealing cultural and linguistic challenges that require consideration in multilingual AI system deployment.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a comparative evaluation of four large language models (LLMs) – Claude 3.7 Sonnet, DeepSeek-V3, Gemini 2.0 Flash, and GPT-4o – for Persian sentiment analysis and emotion detection.  It addresses a gap in research, as most LLM evaluations focus on English. The study uses balanced Persian datasets for sentiment analysis (900 texts) and emotion detection (1800 texts).  The methodology involves consistent prompts and uniform processing parameters to ensure fair comparison. Performance metrics like precision, recall, F1-score, and misclassification patterns are analyzed. Results indicate all models achieve acceptable performance, with GPT-4o demonstrating slightly higher raw accuracy, while Gemini 2.0 Flash proves the most cost-effective.  Emotion detection is more challenging than sentiment analysis. The paper establishes performance benchmarks for Persian NLP, offering practical guidance for model selection based on accuracy, efficiency, and cost. It also reveals linguistic and cultural challenges that must be considered for multilingual AI deployment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its focus on Persian, a language relatively under-represented in LLM evaluation literature. Conducting a comparative analysis on a non-English language, using balanced datasets tailored to that language, and investigating the specific challenges related to its unique linguistic and cultural characteristics, distinguishes this research from many existing LLM evaluations. This aspect is particularly important given the known biases and performance variations of LLMs across languages.

*   **Significance:** The significance is high.

    *   **Benchmark Creation:** Establishing benchmarks for Persian sentiment analysis and emotion detection using state-of-the-art LLMs is a valuable contribution.  It provides a baseline for future research and development in this area.
    *   **Practical Guidance:** The comparative cost/performance analysis offers practical guidance for practitioners working with Persian NLP.  This is especially useful for those with limited resources.
    *   **Linguistic Insights:**  The detailed analysis of misclassification patterns sheds light on the challenges faced by LLMs when dealing with the intricacies of the Persian language, including cultural nuances, morphology, and orthography.  This can inform the development of more robust and culturally sensitive NLP models for Persian.
    *   **Generalizable Methodology:** The rigorous methodology, involving balanced datasets, consistent prompts, and comprehensive error analysis, is a strength and could be adapted for evaluating LLMs on other low-resource languages.
    *   **Real-World Impact:** Highlighting that it is important to select a model based on the end use case (i.e. cost vs. accuracy) makes the paper very helpful for those looking to implement an NLP solution.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Rigorous methodology with controls for fair comparison.
    *   Comprehensive analysis of performance metrics and misclassification patterns.
    *   Balanced datasets contribute to reliability.
    *   The focus on a underrepresented language is valuable.
    *   Practical recommendations for model selection.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited dataset size. Although balanced, the datasets of 900 and 1800 texts are relatively small compared to datasets commonly used for evaluating LLMs in English. This could potentially limit the generalizability of the findings.
    *   The study mentions cultural difficulties in Persian sentiment analysis but doesn't deeply explore them. A more detailed discussion on how cultural context influences sentiment and emotion expression in Persian texts could be added.
    *   Zero-shot Evaluation Limitation: While zero-shot evaluation provides valuable insights, it might not fully represent the potential of these models when fine-tuned for specific Persian tasks. Exploring fine-tuning approaches in future research could be beneficial.
    *   The use of only social media data limits the generalizability of the findings to other types of Persian text.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence research directions in multilingual NLP, particularly for Persian. The insights into model performance and linguistic challenges can guide the development of better Persian NLP models.  The established benchmarks can be used for evaluating future advancements.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution by focusing on a less-studied language and providing a comparative analysis with practical implications. The rigorous methodology enhances its reliability. While the dataset size and depth of cultural exploration could be improved, the paper is a valuable step towards understanding LLM performance in Persian.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Cross-Modal Knowledge Distillation for Speech Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14930v1)
- **Authors**: Enzhi Wang, Qicheng Li, Zhiyuan Tang, Yuhang Jia
- **Abstract**: In this work, we present the first systematic evaluation of catastrophic forgetting and modality inequivalence in speech large language models, showing that introducing speech capabilities can degrade knowledge and reasoning even when inputs remain textual, and performance further decreases with spoken queries. To address these challenges, we propose a cross-modal knowledge distillation framework that leverages both text-to-text and speech-to-text channels to transfer knowledge from a text-based teacher model to a speech LLM. Extensive experiments on dialogue and audio understanding tasks validate the effectiveness of our approach in preserving textual knowledge, improving cross-modal alignment, and enhancing reasoning in speech-based interactions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**
