The paper addresses the problems of catastrophic forgetting and modality inequivalence in speech-based Large Language Models (LLMs).  The authors observe that introducing speech capabilities to text LLMs often degrades performance on text-based tasks (catastrophic forgetting) and that the same query expressed as speech performs worse than text (modality inequivalence). To combat these issues, they propose a cross-modal knowledge distillation framework. This framework leverages both text-to-text and speech-to-text distillation.  The speech LLM (student) is trained to mimic the output of a stronger text LLM (teacher) using both text input and synthesized speech versions of the text input. Experiments on dialogue and audio understanding tasks demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method in preserving textual knowledge, improving cross-modal alignment, and enhancing reasoning in speech-based interactions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Problem Identification:** The paper clearly identifies and articulates two significant challenges in developing effective speech-based LLMs: catastrophic forgetting and modality inequivalence.  The systematic evaluation presented in the paper substantiates these claims with experimental evidence.
*   **Solution Approach:** The proposed cross-modal knowledge distillation framework is a logical and well-motivated approach to address the identified problems. Using the text LLM as a teacher to guide the speech LLM seems sensible. The use of both text-to-text and speech-to-text distillation channels allows the student to learn from the teacher directly in text mode and to better align semantic understanding across speech and text modalities.
*   **Experimental Validation:** The paper includes comprehensive experiments across multiple tasks and models, providing solid evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed method. The results demonstrate improvements in both text-based and speech-based performance after applying knowledge distillation.  The ablation studies exploring the impact of different distillation components further strengthen the findings.
*   **Clarity of Writing:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow. The problem, proposed solution, and experimental results are clearly presented.
*   **Application to Practical Settings:** It targets a very practical and growing area of research where LLMs are being extended for speech-based interactions, which are expected to see significant adoption.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Novelty of Technique:** Knowledge distillation and the use of adapters are well-established techniques. The primary novelty lies in the specific application of these techniques to the speech LLM domain and in the combination of text-to-text and speech-to-text distillation. While this is a valuable contribution, it does not represent a completely groundbreaking technical innovation. It leverages established methods in a new setting.
*   **Dependence on Synthesized Speech:** The approach relies on a TTS system to generate synthetic speech data for speech-to-text distillation. The quality of the TTS system can directly impact the effectiveness of the distillation process. The paper does not discuss this dependence at length nor how it would affect the overall results using different TTS options and qualities.
*   **Limited Scale of Distillation Data:** The paper mentions using approximately 60,000 samples for distillation. While this improves performance, it's fair to wonder if scaling up the distillation data would lead to even more significant gains. Future research exploring the effect of scaling would be valuable.
*   **Lack of comparisons to other approaches:** While comparison to the base models (non-distilled version) is made, the paper lacks explicit comparisons against fine-tuned or adapter-based speech LLMs. If adapter-based methods are the main alternative for this task, it would strengthen the argument that the authors' technique is superior.

**Significance and Impact:**

The paper makes a meaningful contribution to the growing field of speech-based LLMs. By identifying and addressing the issues of catastrophic forgetting and modality inequivalence, the authors help advance the development of more robust and reliable speech-based interaction systems.  The proposed cross-modal knowledge distillation framework provides a practical approach for mitigating these challenges, potentially influencing future research in this area. The work could also be useful for companies developing speech-enabled applications based on LLMs.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

Score: 7.5

Justification: The paper is strong in terms of its problem identification, solution approach, and experimental validation. It tackles a relevant problem in speech LLMs using knowledge distillation and achieves good results. However, the novelty of the technique is incremental, the paper relies on TTS which might affect generalization, and it doesn't show comparative results with alternative speech LLM adaption. Nevertheless, this is a solid and impactful contribution to the community.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Mitigating data replication in text-to-audio generative diffusion models through anti-memorization guidance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14934v1)
- **Authors**: Francisco Messina, Francesca Ronchini, Luca Comanducci, Paolo Bestagini, Fabio Antonacci
- **Abstract**: A persistent challenge in generative audio models is data replication, where the model unintentionally generates parts of its training data during inference. In this work, we address this issue in text-to-audio diffusion models by exploring the use of anti-memorization strategies. We adopt Anti-Memorization Guidance (AMG), a technique that modifies the sampling process of pre-trained diffusion models to discourage memorization. Our study explores three types of guidance within AMG, each designed to reduce replication while preserving generation quality. We use Stable Audio Open as our backbone, leveraging its fully open-source architecture and training dataset. Our comprehensive experimental analysis suggests that AMG significantly mitigates memorization in diffusion-based text-to-audio generation without compromising audio fidelity or semantic alignment.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores methods to mitigate data replication (memorization) in text-to-audio generative diffusion models. It adapts Anti-Memorization Guidance (AMG), a technique previously used for image generation, to the audio domain. AMG works during inference by steering the model away from generating content similar to the training data.  The paper investigates three guidance strategies within the AMG framework: despecification guidance (to reduce the influence of overly specific prompts), caption deduplication guidance (to avoid replicating samples with common captions), and dissimilarity guidance (to move generations away from their nearest training neighbors). The experiments use Stable Audio Open, an open-source model and dataset, allowing for full transparency and control. The results demonstrate that AMG effectively reduces memorization without significantly compromising audio quality or semantic alignment to the input text prompt.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in being, to the authors' knowledge, the first to apply and evaluate anti-memorization techniques in text-to-audio generative diffusion models. While AMG itself is not novel (it was previously used for image generation), its adaptation and evaluation in the context of audio generation is a new contribution. The exploration of different types of guidance specific to audio (e.g., caption deduplication in the context of audio samples' textual descriptions) also adds value. The choice of Stable Audio Open enhances the novelty, as it facilitates reproducible research and provides a clear view on data usage.

*   **Significance:** Data replication is a critical concern in generative AI, raising significant ethical and legal questions regarding copyright and intellectual property. By addressing this issue in the emerging field of text-to-audio generation, the paper tackles a pressing problem. The demonstrated effectiveness of AMG suggests a viable approach for mitigating memorization risks in these models. The open-source nature of the model, data and code is a significant boon to the research community, fostering further exploration and development.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the issue of data replication and its implications in the context of text-to-audio models.
    *   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The AMG framework and its components (despecification, deduplication, dissimilarity) are clearly explained, facilitating understanding and reproducibility.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper uses a mix of qualitative (spectrograms, structural similarity matrices, t-SNE plots) and quantitative metrics (similarity scores, prompt adherence, audio quality metrics) to evaluate the effectiveness of AMG. The ablation study is particularly valuable for understanding the contribution of each guidance strategy.
    *   **Use of Open-Source Resources:** The use of Stable Audio Open makes the research transparent and reproducible, encouraging further investigation.
    *   **Careful Attention to Limitations:** The paper acknowledges the trade-off between memorization reduction and prompt adherence, and points out the counter-intuitive result showing that AMG could improve the FAD score, which needs further investigation.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Nature:** While the application of AMG to audio is novel, the underlying technique is not. The conceptual leap from images to audio is not particularly large, which somewhat limits the contribution's impact.
    *   **Limited Dataset Size:** The dataset used for evaluation (6000 tracks) is relatively small compared to the massive datasets used to train the original models. A larger-scale evaluation would strengthen the conclusions.
    *   **Parameter Tuning:** The reliance on empirical tuning for the dissimilarity guidance coefficient (c3) is a potential limitation. A more principled approach to parameter selection could improve the robustness of the method. The lack of human evaluation also is an important limitation.
    *   **Metrics Discussion Could Be Deeper**: While the paper describes the metrics and results, a more critical evaluation of the limitations of those metrics and the nuances of interpreting the data would be valuable.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more responsible and ethical text-to-audio generation models. The insights gained from this study can inform the design of future mitigation strategies and encourage the adoption of techniques that reduce the risk of data replication.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field by adapting an existing anti-memorization technique to the novel context of text-to-audio generation. While the underlying technique is not entirely novel, the paper offers a clear and comprehensive evaluation, with actionable insights for mitigating data replication risks in audio generation. The open-source nature of the work is a significant strength. The score reflects both the importance of the problem addressed and the value of the presented solution, balanced by the incremental nature of the technical contribution and some limitations in the experimental evaluation and metric analysis.

- **Classification**: eess.AS
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Comparative Analysis of Transformer Models in Social Bot Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14936v1)
- **Authors**: Rohan Veit, Michael Lones
- **Abstract**: Social media has become a key medium of communication in today's society. This realisation has led to many parties employing artificial users (or bots) to mislead others into believing untruths or acting in a beneficial manner to such parties. Sophisticated text generation tools, such as large language models, have further exacerbated this issue. This paper aims to compare the effectiveness of bot detection models based on encoder and decoder transformers. Pipelines are developed to evaluate the performance of these classifiers, revealing that encoder-based classifiers demonstrate greater accuracy and robustness. However, decoder-based models showed greater adaptability through task-specific alignment, suggesting more potential for generalisation across different use cases in addition to superior observa. These findings contribute to the ongoing effort to prevent digital environments being manipulated while protecting the integrity of online discussion.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper, including a novelty/significance score with a detailed justification.

**Summary:**

The paper "A Comparative Analysis of Transformer Models in Social Bot Detection" explores the effectiveness of different transformer-based models for identifying social bots on social media platforms like Twitter. The study compares encoder-based (BERT, RoBERTa, DistilBERT) and decoder-based (LLama3.1, Mistral, Qwen2.5) transformer architectures in bot detection pipelines. The researchers built pipelines utilizing these models and evaluated their performance under various conditions, including limited and enriched feature sets, and by tuning both encoder and decoder models with prompt engineering and hyperparameter optimization. The experiments use the TwiBot-22 dataset and metrics like accuracy, precision, recall, F1-score, and null response rate. The main findings suggest that encoder-based models generally outperform decoder-based models in this task, but decoder models showed greater responsiveness to task-specific tuning, implying potential for improvement with prompt engineering.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Research Question:** The paper addresses a relevant and important problem: the detection of social bots, which is a critical issue for maintaining the integrity of online discussions.
    *   **Comprehensive Comparison:** The direct comparison of encoder and decoder-based transformer models is valuable, as most prior works tend to focus on one architecture type. The use of multiple model sizes also contributes positively.
    *   **Systematic Experimentation:** The experimental setup is relatively well-designed, with variations in feature sets and model tuning. The use of standard metrics enables comparison with other bot detection studies.
    *   **Practical Considerations:** The discussion about the "null response rate" of decoder models highlights a practical consideration for real-world deployment.
    *   **Accessible Tuning of Decoder Models:** The emphasis on the accessible tuning of decoder models through prompt engineering makes the work relevant in practice.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Dataset:** Relying solely on the TwiBot-22 dataset is a significant limitation. While TwiBot-22 is a good dataset, using only one dataset hampers the generalizability of the findings. Evaluations on other datasets would make the conclusions far more robust.
    *   **Scope of Models:**  The model selection within each architecture type is limited. While common models are used, a wider range could reveal more nuanced performance characteristics.  Especially for decoder models, evaluation of models like Gemini is warranted.
    *   **Limited Feature Engineering Exploration:** The "enriched" feature set primarily focuses on tweet content. The study could have delved deeper into more sophisticated feature engineering, including user network analysis or temporal patterns of activity. The dataset allows for this and the paper does not utilize it.
    *   **Lack of theoretical justification:** The paper would benefit from a clearer discussion of why one architecture might be more effective for the task than the other, or an exploration of why the models exhibited the performance differences they did. The paper notes a significant performance reduction in the decoder model when the full dataset is added, but does not provide an explicit explanation for this.
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the comparison is valuable, it could be argued that the individual components (using transformers for bot detection, prompt engineering, etc.) are not entirely novel in isolation. The main value comes from the specific comparison undertaken.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The direct comparison of encoder vs. decoder models for this task is a positive contribution.
    *   The emphasis on the tunability of decoder models through prompt engineering is also a valuable practical finding.
    *   The exploration into size variance for encoder models shows insight.
    *   The paper's main weakness is the limited validation of its findings across datasets and lack of thorough explanation for the behavior. This restricts its immediate impact.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses, the paper demonstrates some useful insights and a well executed empirical evaluation on an important topic. However, the limited dataset, lack of strong theoretical justification and incremental novelty somewhat restricts the impact of its conclusions. While the findings offer useful information for practitioners interested in social bot detection, the limitations prevent this being a highly influential piece.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Explainable AI for Infection Prevention and Control: Modeling CPE Acquisition and Patient Outcomes in an Irish Hospital with Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14942v1)
- **Authors**: Minh-Khoi Pham, Tai Tan Mai, Martin Crane, Rob Brennan, Marie E. Ward, Una Geary, Declan Byrne, Brian O Connell, Colm Bergin, Donncha Creagh, Nick McDonald, Marija Bezbradica
- **Abstract**: Carbapenemase-Producing Enterobacteriace poses a critical concern for infection prevention and control in hospitals. However, predictive modeling of previously highlighted CPE-associated risks such as readmission, mortality, and extended length of stay (LOS) remains underexplored, particularly with modern deep learning approaches. This study introduces an eXplainable AI modeling framework to investigate CPE impact on patient outcomes from Electronic Medical Records data of an Irish hospital. We analyzed an inpatient dataset from an Irish acute hospital, incorporating diagnostic codes, ward transitions, patient demographics, infection-related variables and contact network features. Several Transformer-based architectures were benchmarked alongside traditional machine learning models. Clinical outcomes were predicted, and XAI techniques were applied to interpret model decisions. Our framework successfully demonstrated the utility of Transformer-based models, with TabTransformer consistently outperforming baselines across multiple clinical prediction tasks, especially for CPE acquisition (AUROC and sensitivity). We found infection-related features, including historical hospital exposure, admission context, and network centrality measures, to be highly influential in predicting patient outcomes and CPE acquisition risk. Explainability analyses revealed that features like "Area of Residence", "Admission Ward" and prior admissions are key risk factors. Network variables like "Ward PageRank" also ranked highly, reflecting the potential value of structural exposure information. This study presents a robust and explainable AI framework for analyzing complex EMR data to identify key risk factors and predict CPE-related outcomes. Our findings underscore the superior performance of the Transformer models and highlight the importance of diverse clinical and network features.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, followed by a justified novelty/significance score:

**Summary:**

The paper presents an eXplainable AI (XAI) framework for analyzing Carbapenemase-Producing Enterobacteriaceae (CPE) acquisition and related patient outcomes using Electronic Medical Record (EMR) data from an Irish hospital. It benchmarks various machine learning models, including traditional methods and Transformer-based architectures (TabTransformer, ResNet, etc.), for predicting 30-day readmission, in-hospital mortality, length of stay, and CPE acquisition risk. The study integrates contact network features derived from patient ward co-location to model potential transmission pathways. XAI techniques, primarily Integrated Gradients, are used to interpret model predictions and identify key risk factors. The study finds that Transformer-based models generally outperform traditional methods, identifies relevant features for CPE risk prediction, and explores cohort-level representations through embedding visualization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Practical Application:** The paper addresses a real-world healthcare problem (CPE infections) and provides a practical framework for analyzing EMR data. This increases its potential for adoption in hospital settings.
    *   **Comprehensive Benchmarking:** The study compares a wide range of machine learning models, providing a solid basis for understanding their relative performance on the given tasks.
    *   **Integration of Contact Networks:** Incorporating contact network features derived from ward co-location is a valuable addition for modeling potential transmission dynamics.
    *   **Focus on Explainability:** The use of XAI techniques, especially Integrated Gradients, enhances the transparency and interpretability of the models, building trust and facilitating actionable insights.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The availability of code and reproducibility guidelines contributes to the transparency and usability of the research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Dataset:** The study relies on data from a single Irish hospital, limiting the generalizability of the findings to other settings with different patient populations and healthcare practices. The low prevalence of CPE cases (0.2%) creates a significant class imbalance, potentially affecting model performance and reliability of identified associations. The data also has a limited timeframe, further exacerbating the data imbalance.
    *   **Coarse-Grained Temporal Data:** The lack of fine-grained timestamps within the EMRs is a limitation. The aggregation of data features within time periods may have eliminated key data.
    *   **Relatively Limited Novelty in Methods:** While the application to CPE and the integration of contact networks is worthwhile, the core modeling techniques (TabTransformer, ResNet, Integrated Gradients) are not novel in themselves. The novelty lies more in the application and evaluation.
    *   **Modest Gains in Precision/Recall:** The paper acknowledges that the performance on precision-recall metrics remains modest due to the class imbalance, which is a significant limitation for practical applications where high precision is crucial.
    *   **Correlation vs. Causation:** The study identifies associations between features and outcomes but does not establish causation.  The reliance on observational data limits the ability to draw strong causal inferences.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper contributes to the growing body of research on applying AI and machine learning to healthcare, specifically in the area of infection prevention and control.
    *   The integration of contact networks and XAI techniques offers valuable insights for understanding CPE transmission dynamics and informing targeted interventions.
    *   The study provides a practical and reproducible framework that can be adapted and applied to other healthcare settings.
    *   The paper highlights the importance of addressing class imbalance and ensuring data quality when developing AI-based models for healthcare applications.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of **6** to this paper.

*   While the paper addresses a significant healthcare problem and presents a well-executed analysis, its novelty is somewhat limited by the use of established machine learning techniques. While the model may predict outcomes, further analysis, specifically in clinical risk factor analysis, would solidify its significance.

*   The reliance on data from a single hospital, coupled with the limited timeline and class imbalance issues, casts some doubt on the generalizability of the findings and their immediate practical applicability. These factors significantly limit the potential impact of the research.

*   However, the integration of contact network features and the focus on explainability are valuable contributions, providing insights into CPE transmission dynamics and enhancing the trustworthiness of the models. The provision of reproducible code also increases the potential for adoption and further research.

*   Overall, the paper represents a solid piece of applied research with some interesting findings and a practical framework, but its limitations prevent it from being a truly exceptional and transformative contribution to the field.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Explicit vs. Implicit Biographies: Evaluating and Adapting LLM Information Extraction on Wikidata-Derived Texts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14943v1)
- **Authors**: Alessandra Stramiglio, Andrea Schimmenti, Valentina Pasqual, Marieke van Erp, Francesco Sovrano, Fabio Vitali
- **Abstract**: Text Implicitness has always been challenging in Natural Language Processing (NLP), with traditional methods relying on explicit statements to identify entities and their relationships. From the sentence "Zuhdi attends church every Sunday", the relationship between Zuhdi and Christianity is evident for a human reader, but it presents a challenge when it must be inferred automatically. Large language models (LLMs) have proven effective in NLP downstream tasks such as text comprehension and information extraction (IE). This study examines how textual implicitness affects IE tasks in pre-trained LLMs: LLaMA 2.3, DeepSeekV1, and Phi1.5. We generate two synthetic datasets of 10k implicit and explicit verbalization of biographic information to measure the impact on LLM performance and analyze whether fine-tuning implicit data improves their ability to generalize in implicit reasoning tasks. This research presents an experiment on the internal reasoning processes of LLMs in IE, particularly in dealing with implicit and explicit contexts. The results demonstrate that fine-tuning LLM models with LoRA (low-rank adaptation) improves their performance in extracting information from implicit texts, contributing to better model interpretability and reliability.
- **Summary**: Okay, I'll provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper.

**Summary**

The paper "Explicit vs. Implicit Biographies: Evaluating and Adapting LLM Information Extraction on Wikidata-Derived Texts" investigates how well Large Language Models (LLMs) perform in Information Extraction (IE) tasks when dealing with implicit versus explicit textual information. The authors generate two synthetic datasets, one with explicit statements and another with implicit statements derived from Wikidata biographies. They then fine-tune several LLMs (LLaMA 2.3, DeepSeekV1, and Phi1.5) using LoRA and evaluate their ability to extract information from both explicit and implicit texts. The study seeks to determine whether fine-tuning on implicit data improves an LLM's ability to generalize to implicit reasoning tasks. Their results show that fine-tuning with both explicit and implicit data improves performance on implicit IE, suggesting that exposure to implicit patterns during training is crucial.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a relevant and important challenge in NLP: the ability of LLMs to handle implicit information. While the general area of IE and LLMs is well-trodden, the specific focus on *explicit vs. implicit* information in *biographical contexts* and the systematic evaluation of fine-tuning as a mitigation strategy adds a layer of novelty. The creation of synthetic datasets to control for levels of explicitness is also a useful approach. However, the core idea of fine-tuning LLMs for specific tasks is not, in itself, groundbreaking.

*   **Significance:** The paper has significance for several reasons:

    *   **Practical Implications:**  The findings suggest a practical approach to improve LLMs' performance in IE tasks when dealing with real-world text, which often contains implicit information. This could improve the reliability and accuracy of systems relying on LLMs for information extraction.
    *   **Understanding LLMs:** The study sheds light on the internal reasoning processes of LLMs when faced with varying degrees of textual implicitness. This contributes to a better understanding of LLMs' strengths and weaknesses.
    *   **Methodological Contribution:** The creation of controlled synthetic datasets and the methodical evaluation provide a framework for future research on the impact of textual implicitness on other NLP tasks.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Research Questions:**  The research questions are well-defined and focused on a specific issue.
    *   **Controlled Experiments:** The use of synthetic datasets allows for careful control of the level of implicitness.
    *   **Rigorous Evaluation:**  The evaluation uses established metrics (accuracy, F1-score), statistical tests (Wilcoxon signed-rank test), and ablation studies to provide a comprehensive assessment.
    *   **Reproducibility:**  The authors have made their implementation available, which promotes reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Synthetic Data Limitations:** The synthetic nature of the data is a major limitation.  While it allows for controlled experiments, it might not fully capture the complexity and nuances of implicit information in real-world text. There is a risk that the patterns learned from the synthetic data might not generalize well to real-world scenarios. The prompt used for data generation is also critical, and potential biases within that prompt could influence the generated data and the subsequent results.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The study focuses specifically on biographical information. It's unclear whether the findings would generalize to other domains or types of text.
    *   **Model Selection:** While the selected models are popular, it's important to acknowledge that the field is constantly evolving. The results might not be directly transferable to other LLMs or future iterations. It would also be good to compare to larger LLMs in a zero-shot setting to see where the gap is.
    *   **Lack of qualitative analysis:** The paper lacks deeper qualitative analysis. It would be more insightful to analyze the error the models make when reasoning implicitly, and compare that to the errors that are made when reasoning explicitly.

*   **Justification of Score:** Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, I assign a score of **6**. The paper addresses an important problem with a relatively rigorous methodology. The findings have practical implications and contribute to our understanding of LLMs. However, the reliance on synthetic data and the limited scope of the study temper the overall impact. A more thorough investigation using real-world data and a broader range of tasks would significantly strengthen the conclusions and warrant a higher score. The insights gained are interesting, but more validation is required. Also the evaluation part can be extended to look deeper into qualitative analysis.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Stochastic Bilevel Optimization with Heavy-Tailed Noise
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14952v1)
- **Authors**: Zhuanghua Liu, Luo Luo
- **Abstract**: This paper considers the smooth bilevel optimization in which the lower-level problem is strongly convex and the upper-level problem is possibly nonconvex. We focus on the stochastic setting that the algorithm can access the unbiased stochastic gradient evaluation with heavy-tailed noise, which is prevalent in many machine learning applications such as training large language models and reinforcement learning. We propose a nested-loop normalized stochastic bilevel approximation (N$^2$SBA) for finding an $\epsilon$-stationary point with the stochastic first-order oracle (SFO) complexity of $\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\big(\kappa^{\frac{7p-3}{p-1}} \sigma^{\frac{p}{p-1}} \epsilon^{-\frac{4 p - 2}{p-1}}\big)$, where $\kappa$ is the condition number, $p\in(1,2]$ is the order of central moment for the noise, and $\sigma$ is the noise level. Furthermore, we specialize our idea to solve the nonconvex-strongly-concave minimax optimization problem, achieving an $\epsilon$-stationary point with the SFO complexity of $\tilde{\mathcal O}\big(\kappa^{\frac{2p-1}{p-1}} \sigma^{\frac{p}{p-1}} \epsilon^{-\frac{3p-2}{p-1}}\big)$. All above upper bounds match the best-known results under the special case of the bounded variance setting, i.e., $p=2$.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

This paper addresses stochastic bilevel optimization in scenarios where the stochastic gradients exhibit heavy-tailed noise. The authors focus on smooth bilevel problems with a strongly convex lower-level problem and a potentially non-convex upper-level problem. They propose a nested-loop normalized stochastic bilevel approximation (N2SBA) algorithm and analyze its performance under the p-th bounded central moment (p-BCM) gradient noise condition. The algorithm aims to find an epsilon-stationary point, and the authors provide complexity bounds in terms of the condition number, noise level, and the order of the central moment.  They also specialize the algorithm for non-convex strongly concave minimax optimization achieving a rate of $\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^2)$.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in addressing bilevel optimization *specifically* under heavy-tailed noise conditions. While stochastic bilevel optimization and heavy-tailed stochastic optimization have been studied separately, the combination is less explored. Specifically, the prior SOTA bilevel rates under bounded gradients, the rate achieved in this paper matches those under the bounded gradients setting. However, to guarantee a similar rate, the authors use gradient clipping, the novelty lies in constructing a tighter analysis using clipped gradients.
*   **Significance:** Heavy-tailed noise is realistic for many machine learning problems (LLMs, RL, etc.). This paper provides a theoretical grounding for handling such noise in bilevel settings, which is important for hyperparameter optimization, meta-learning, and related applications. Also, the high probability results are significant, as prior approaches tend to focus on expectation.
*   **Technical Soundness:** The paper appears technically sound. The algorithm is well-described, and the convergence analysis is rigorous. The complexity bounds are clearly stated and seem reasonable given the problem setting. The specialization of the algorithm to the nonconvex-strongly-concave minimax problem is also a valuable addition, as much prior work makes stronger assumptions like convexity-concavity or star-cocoercivity.
*   **Clarity and Presentation:** The paper's presentation could be slightly improved. The introduction is clear, but the technical sections are dense and require careful reading. The experimental section provides reasonable validation of the theoretical results.
*   **Limitations:** The improvement is not too dramatic on a technical level, since N2SBA relies on gradient clipping. This implies a restriction to problems which have large gradients but the bulk of the data isn't sparse to avoid clipping too much. A more sophisticated approach might consider alternative gradient estimators less dependent on the clipping procedure. The experiments could be more extensive, with comparisons on a wider range of real-world bilevel problems.
*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the design and analysis of algorithms for bilevel optimization in practical settings. It provides useful insights and a theoretically sound algorithm for dealing with heavy-tailed noise, a common problem in machine learning. Also, the high probability rates add value to the analysis.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper makes a valuable contribution by addressing the realistic scenario of heavy-tailed noise in bilevel optimization and producing matching rates under the bounded gradients setting, the technical novelty is somewhat limited and the analysis relies extensively on gradient clipping (the same can be said of a multitude of recent papers). The improvement (if there is improvement at all) is limited and the experimental section could be more expansive.

- **Score**: 6/10

### Sentinel Agents for Secure and Trustworthy Agentic AI in Multi-Agent Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14956v1)
- **Authors**: Diego Gosmar, Deborah A. Dahl
- **Abstract**: This paper proposes a novel architectural framework aimed at enhancing security and reliability in multi-agent systems (MAS). A central component of this framework is a network of Sentinel Agents, functioning as a distributed security layer that integrates techniques such as semantic analysis via large language models (LLMs), behavioral analytics, retrieval-augmented verification, and cross-agent anomaly detection. Such agents can potentially oversee inter-agent communications, identify potential threats, enforce privacy and access controls, and maintain comprehensive audit records. Complementary to the idea of Sentinel Agents is the use of a Coordinator Agent. The Coordinator Agent supervises policy implementation, and manages agent participation. In addition, the Coordinator also ingests alerts from Sentinel Agents. Based on these alerts, it can adapt policies, isolate or quarantine misbehaving agents, and contain threats to maintain the integrity of the MAS ecosystem. This dual-layered security approach, combining the continuous monitoring of Sentinel Agents with the governance functions of Coordinator Agents, supports dynamic and adaptive defense mechanisms against a range of threats, including prompt injection, collusive agent behavior, hallucinations generated by LLMs, privacy breaches, and coordinated multi-agent attacks. In addition to the architectural design, we present a simulation study where 162 synthetic attacks of different families (prompt injection, hallucination, and data exfiltration) were injected into a multi-agent conversational environment. The Sentinel Agents successfully detected the attack attempts, confirming the practical feasibility of the proposed monitoring approach. The framework also offers enhanced system observability, supports regulatory compliance, and enables policy evolution over time.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel security architecture called Sentinel Agents designed to enhance the safety and reliability of multi-agent systems (MAS), particularly those leveraging agentic AI and operating in shared conversational spaces.  Sentinel Agents act as a distributed security layer, monitoring inter-agent communications using techniques like LLMs for semantic analysis, behavioral analytics, retrieval-augmented verification, and anomaly detection. Coordinator Agents oversee policy implementation and manage agent participation, adapting policies and isolating misbehaving agents based on Sentinel alerts. The architecture is designed to defend against prompt injection, collusive behavior, hallucinations, privacy breaches, and coordinated attacks. The paper presents a simulation study demonstrating the feasibility of the monitoring approach in a multi-agent conversational environment.  It explores architectural patterns for Sentinel Agent deployment, implementation considerations, ethical implications, and use cases.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of a dedicated security layer in MAS, employing Sentinel Agents with a combination of LLM-based analysis and behavioral monitoring, presents a noteworthy advancement. While security in AI and agent-based systems is an active area, the specific architecture and the focus on shared conversational spaces appear innovative. The separation of security concerns into dedicated agents (Sentinel and Coordinator) instead of distributing it to all agents is a key differentiating factor.

*   **Significance:** The growing complexity of MAS, particularly with the integration of LLMs, makes security and trust essential. Prompt injection, hallucinations, and privacy breaches pose significant risks. The paper addresses these emerging threats directly and offers a viable solution. The use cases presented are relevant and demonstrate the potential applicability of Sentinel Agents in various domains. The integration of the proposed architecture with existing frameworks and protocols would be a significant contribution. The connection to AI safety frameworks like NIST AI RMF and OWASP Top 10 strengthens the paper.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The clear articulation of the threat landscape in agentic AI MAS.
    *   The well-defined architectural components and their interactions.
    *   The presentation of various deployment patterns with trade-offs.
    *   The inclusion of a simulation study, even with a limited scope, to demonstrate feasibility.
    *   The discussion of ethical considerations.
    *   The comprehensive comparison with existing frameworks.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The simulation study is limited.  It focuses primarily on prompt injection and data exfiltration with only a few hallucination probes. A more comprehensive evaluation with a balanced dataset and measurement of false positives is needed.
    *   The paper currently relies on a conceptual model. Greater details around how to implement LLM-based semantic analysis and behavioral detection would strengthen its practical value.
    *   While the architecture is presented well, actual deployment in different MAS environments and integration with legacy systems may raise additional challenges that are not fully addressed.
    * The paper is very high level which is good for an introduction, but lacks low level details around the implementation.

*   **Impact:** If fully realized, the Sentinel Agent architecture could significantly impact the development and deployment of secure and trustworthy MAS. It provides a blueprint for addressing security challenges in complex AI-driven systems. However, the level of implementation details need to be improved for other developers and researchers to implement these solutions.

*   **Justification for Score:** While the paper presents a valuable contribution with clear novelty and potential significance, its limitations in empirical validation and concrete implementation details prevent it from receiving a higher score. The concept is strong, but requires further rigorous testing and refinement.

### FAWN: A MultiEncoder Fusion-Attention Wave Network for Integrated Sensing and Communication Indoor Scene Inference
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14968v1)
- **Authors**: Carlos Barroso-Fernández, Alejandro Calvillo-Fernandez, Antonio de la Oliva, Carlos J. Bernardos
- **Abstract**: The upcoming generations of wireless technologies promise an era where everything is interconnected and intelligent. As the need for intelligence grows, networks must learn to better understand the physical world. However, deploying dedicated hardware to perceive the environment is not always feasible, mainly due to costs and/or complexity. Integrated Sensing and Communication (ISAC) has made a step forward in addressing this challenge. Within ISAC, passive sensing emerges as a cost-effective solution that reuses wireless communications to sense the environment, without interfering with existing communications. Nevertheless, the majority of current solutions are limited to one technology (mostly Wi-Fi or 5G), constraining the maximum accuracy reachable. As different technologies work with different spectrums, we see a necessity in integrating more than one technology to augment the coverage area. Hence, we take the advantage of ISAC passive sensing, to present FAWN, a MultiEncoder Fusion-Attention Wave Network for ISAC indoor scene inference. FAWN is based on the original transformers architecture, to fuse information from Wi-Fi and 5G, making the network capable of understanding the physical world without interfering with the current communication. To test our solution, we have built a prototype and integrated it in a real scenario. Results show errors below 0.6 m around 84% of times.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FAWN, a Multi-Encoder Fusion-Attention Wave Network designed for indoor scene inference using Integrated Sensing and Communication (ISAC). FAWN leverages passive sensing by fusing information from both Wi-Fi and 5G signals. The architecture is based on the transformer architecture, utilizing a dual-encoder setup (one for Wi-Fi and one for 5G) and a single decoder. The paper details the model architecture, the data collection process in a real laboratory setting, and the experimental results demonstrating the model's ability to detect and locate objects with good accuracy. The results show the model achieves an accuracy of 96% in detecting objects and localizes objects with a precision of 0.6m in 84% of the cases. It also provides performance metrics in classifying the detected entity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The main novelty lies in the *fusion* of Wi-Fi and 5G passive sensing data using a transformer-based architecture. While individual use of Wi-Fi or 5G for sensing is not new, the integration of both technologies, processed through a fusion-attention mechanism, is a significant step forward. The modular design of the FAWN architecture, allowing for the easy integration of additional sensing modalities, is another valuable contribution. It is also notable that this approach doesn't require any extra spectrum to be used, making it a very practical implementation of ISAC passive sensing.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial challenge in intelligent environments: the need for accurate and cost-effective sensing. Passive sensing offers a solution by reusing existing communication infrastructure. FAWN improves upon existing solutions by integrating multiple technologies, which is important since those technologies provides different levels of precision for the inference. The achieved accuracy in object detection and localization has important implications for applications in human-robot collaboration, smart factories, and indoor navigation.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Solid architecture:** The transformer-based architecture is well-suited for the task of fusing data from different sources, and the attention mechanism allows the model to focus on the most relevant features.
    *   **Real-world validation:** The deployment of the model in a real laboratory setting and the collection of data from commercial Wi-Fi and 5G equipment increase the practical relevance of the work.
    *   **Clear presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the model architecture, experimental setup, and results.
    *   **Good performance:** the model achieves state-of-the-art performance regarding to detecting objects and localizes them with good precision.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited dataset size:** The dataset size (400 samples) is relatively small, which may limit the generalizability of the results. Expanding the dataset to include more diverse scenarios and environments would strengthen the conclusions.
    *   **Comparison is not great:** The comparison with CNN-ABLSTM is a bit unfair, as it's a bit dated. While a better solution would take more time, it would be preferable to compare with more recent works.
    *   **Limited scope:** The experiment is centered in a laboratory, so conclusions should be taken with some restrain.
    *   **Scalability issues:** In bigger installations, it may be hard to cover the whole area with just one 5G dot and a WiFi AP.
    *   **5G limitations:** It is worth noting that although the 5G performance has been enhanced with the proposed technology, the 5G donut pattern limitations is still present, hindering the overall performance.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work has the potential to influence the development of more accurate and robust indoor sensing systems. The fusion approach and the transformer-based architecture could be adopted and extended by other researchers in the field. The modularity of the framework also allows further deployments with new sensing modalities. Furthermore, it could inspire new research into the integration of sensing and communication in next-generation wireless networks.

*   **Score:** 7.5

    *   **Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to indoor scene inference by fusing Wi-Fi and 5G data. The experimental results are promising, and the architecture is well-designed. However, the limited dataset size, the not so great comparison with state-of-the-art methods and the experimental setting, hinder the potential influence. Despite these limitations, the paper represents a valuable contribution to the field of ISAC and has the potential to inspire further research.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### What Matters in LLM-Based Feature Extractor for Recommender? A Systematic Analysis of Prompts, Models, and Adaptation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14979v1)
- **Authors**: Kainan Shi, Peilin Zhou, Ge Wang, Han Ding, Fei Wang
- **Abstract**: Using Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate semantic features has been demonstrated as a powerful paradigm for enhancing Sequential Recommender Systems (SRS). This typically involves three stages: processing item text, extracting features with LLMs, and adapting them for downstream models. However, existing methods vary widely in prompting, architecture, and adaptation strategies, making it difficult to fairly compare design choices and identify what truly drives performance. In this work, we propose RecXplore, a modular analytical framework that decomposes the LLM-as-feature-extractor pipeline into four modules: data processing, semantic feature extraction, feature adaptation, and sequential modeling. Instead of proposing new techniques, RecXplore revisits and organizes established methods, enabling systematic exploration of each module in isolation. Experiments on four public datasets show that simply combining the best designs from existing techniques without exhaustive search yields up to 18.7% relative improvement in NDCG@5 and 12.7% in HR@5 over strong baselines. These results underscore the utility of modular benchmarking for identifying effective design patterns and promoting standardized research in LLM-enhanced recommendation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "What Matters in LLM-Based Feature Extractor for Recommender? A Systematic Analysis of Prompts, Models, and Adaptation" investigates the effectiveness of using Large Language Models (LLMs) as feature extractors for sequential recommendation systems (SRS).  The authors propose a modular analytical framework called RecXplore, which decomposes the LLM-as-feature-extractor pipeline into four modules: data processing, feature extraction, feature adaptation, and sequential modeling. They then systematically explore different design choices within each module through controlled experiments on four public datasets. The authors find that simple attribute concatenation works best for data processing, mean pooling for feature aggregation, a two-stage fine-tuning (CPT+SFT) process for LLMs, PCA combined with a Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architecture for feature adaptation, and direct replacement of item ID embeddings. Combining these optimal choices in RecXplore leads to significant performance improvements compared to strong baselines, highlighting the value of modular benchmarking.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Systematic Approach:** The key strength lies in the systematic and modular approach. RecXplore provides a well-defined framework for analyzing and comparing different components of the LLM-as-feature-extractor pipeline.  This addresses a significant gap in the literature, where isolated techniques were often studied without a clear understanding of their interactions.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:**  The paper conducts a thorough experimental evaluation, covering various design choices for each module across multiple datasets. This lends credibility to the findings and allows for generalization across different scenarios.
    *   **Actionable Insights:**  The paper provides actionable insights for practitioners. By identifying effective design patterns, the authors offer valuable guidance for building and deploying LLM-enhanced recommendation systems. Specifically, the combination of CPT+SFT and MoE is significant.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors emphasize reproducibility, which is crucial for advancing research in this area. The framework is designed to facilitate fair comparisons and standardized research practices.
    *   **Performance Improvement:** The substantial performance gains achieved by combining the best-performing components underscore the importance of the systematic analysis conducted.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Backbones:** While SASRec is a strong baseline, the analysis focuses solely on it.  The generalizability of the findings to other sequential recommendation architectures (e.g., Transformer variants, Mamba-based models, or graph-based models) is not fully explored. Although the core findings seem general, explicit evaluation would add confidence.
    *   **Greedy Optimization:** The paper acknowledges the use of a greedy optimization strategy. This means the optimal configuration may not be truly global but represents a locally optimal point based on individual module optimization. While practical, it's a limitation.
    *   **Limited LLM Tuning strategies:** Although they explore various strategies, LoRA is one specific form of parameter-efficient fine-tuning. Exploring other strategies or comparing PEFT methods directly (e.g., prefix tuning, adapters) would be valuable.
    *   **Dataset Diversity:** While four datasets are used, expanding to more varied datasets or specifically including very sparse datasets would strengthen the generalizability of the conclusions.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The novelty lies in providing a clear, modular framework for analyzing and optimizing LLM-enhanced recommendation pipelines. While individual components may have been explored in prior work, the systematic and comprehensive analysis is unique.
    *   The significance is high because the paper offers a practical and reproducible approach to building better recommendation systems using LLMs. The findings provide valuable guidance for researchers and practitioners in this rapidly evolving field.  The modularity makes it easier to adopt and extend these methods.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a strong contribution by systematically dissecting the LLM-as-feature-extractor paradigm. The clear framework and comprehensive experiments provide valuable and actionable insights. The modularity of RecXplore and the tangible performance improvements observed bolster its significance. However, the reliance on a single sequential backbone (SASRec) and the use of a greedy optimization strategy limit the generalizability and optimality of the findings, keeping it from being a top score. The breadth of LLM fine-tuning strategies could be improved, also.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 8/10

### SPATIALGEN: Layout-guided 3D Indoor Scene Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14981v1)
- **Authors**: Chuan Fang, Heng Li, Yixun Liang, Jia Zheng, Yongsen Mao, Yuan Liu, Rui Tang, Zihan Zhou, Ping Tan
- **Abstract**: Creating high-fidelity 3D models of indoor environments is essential for applications in design, virtual reality, and robotics. However, manual 3D modeling remains time-consuming and labor-intensive. While recent advances in generative AI have enabled automated scene synthesis, existing methods often face challenges in balancing visual quality, diversity, semantic consistency, and user control. A major bottleneck is the lack of a large-scale, high-quality dataset tailored to this task. To address this gap, we introduce a comprehensive synthetic dataset, featuring 12,328 structured annotated scenes with 57,440 rooms, and 4.7M photorealistic 2D renderings. Leveraging this dataset, we present SpatialGen, a novel multi-view multi-modal diffusion model that generates realistic and semantically consistent 3D indoor scenes. Given a 3D layout and a reference image (derived from a text prompt), our model synthesizes appearance (color image), geometry (scene coordinate map), and semantic (semantic segmentation map) from arbitrary viewpoints, while preserving spatial consistency across modalities. SpatialGen consistently generates superior results to previous methods in our experiments. We are open-sourcing our data and models to empower the community and advance the field of indoor scene understanding and generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the SPATIALGEN paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces SPATIALGEN, a novel layout-guided 3D indoor scene generation framework. Addressing the limitations of existing methods in balancing visual quality, diversity, semantic consistency, and user control, SPATIALGEN leverages a new large-scale synthetic dataset featuring 12,328 structured annotated scenes. The core of SPATIALGEN is a multi-view multi-modal diffusion model conditioned on 3D layouts.  This model generates realistic and semantically consistent 3D indoor scenes from arbitrary viewpoints, jointly synthesizing appearance, geometry, and semantic information. The framework also incorporates an iterative multi-view generation strategy and a 3D Gaussian splatting optimization step for free-viewpoint rendering. The paper demonstrates superior results compared to previous methods in text-to-3D and image-to-3D scene generation tasks, and the dataset and models are to be open-sourced.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several elements of novelty, although the overall architecture builds on existing concepts. The primary novelty lies in:

    *   **The SPATIALGEN dataset:** The large-scale synthetic dataset with detailed 3D layouts and comprehensive annotations is a significant contribution. The previous work has been limited by the scale and quality of the dataset.
    *   **Layout-Guided Multi-view Multi-modal Diffusion model** The specific combination of layout guidance with a multi-view, multi-modal diffusion architecture, and the custom-designed alternating attention mechanism, adds a unique flavor to existing research. Iterative dense view generation enables sampling images from many different viewpoints.

*   **Significance:**  The paper has potential for significant impact on the field. The ability to generate high-quality, controllable 3D indoor scenes has wide-ranging applications in design, VR/AR, robotics, and gaming. The open-sourcing of the dataset and models will likely spur further research and development in this area. The paper tackles the important problem of improving scene generation and addressing the inherent constraints associated with the existing approaches. The ability to create more diverse, realistic, and controllable 3D environments is highly valuable.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Dataset:** The large-scale, high-quality dataset is a major strength. It addresses a critical bottleneck in the field and enables more robust training of generative models.
    *   **Architecture:** The multi-view multi-modal diffusion model is well-designed and addresses the challenges of semantic consistency and viewpoint extrapolation.
    *   **Results:**  The experimental results demonstrate superior performance compared to existing methods, both quantitatively and qualitatively.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Synthetic Data:** While a strength in terms of annotation completeness, the reliance on synthetic data may limit the realism and generalizability of the generated scenes to real-world scenarios. The domain gap between synthetic and real images remains a challenge.
    *   **Computational Cost:**  The use of multi-view diffusion and iterative generation may lead to significant computational cost, potentially limiting the practicality of the method for real-time applications.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:** The paper presents a significant contribution to the field of 3D indoor scene generation. The creation of the large-scale SPATIALGEN dataset is a major step forward, addressing a critical limitation of previous research. The proposed multi-view multi-modal diffusion model, coupled with the iterative refinement strategy, demonstrates superior performance in terms of visual quality, semantic consistency, and controllability. The weaknesses, primarily related to the reliance on synthetic data and the potential for high computational cost, are acknowledged and represent areas for future research. Overall, the paper's novelty and potential impact warrant a high score. The open-sourcing of resources will further accelerate the progress in the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### A Knowledge-driven Adaptive Collaboration of LLMs for Enhancing Medical Decision-making
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.14998v1)
- **Authors**: Xiao Wu, Ting-Zhu Huang, Liang-Jian Deng, Yanyuan Qiao, Imran Razzak, Yutong Xie
- **Abstract**: Medical decision-making often involves integrating knowledge from multiple clinical specialties, typically achieved through multidisciplinary teams. Inspired by this collaborative process, recent work has leveraged large language models (LLMs) in multi-agent collaboration frameworks to emulate expert teamwork. While these approaches improve reasoning through agent interaction, they are limited by static, pre-assigned roles, which hinder adaptability and dynamic knowledge integration. To address these limitations, we propose KAMAC, a Knowledge-driven Adaptive Multi-Agent Collaboration framework that enables LLM agents to dynamically form and expand expert teams based on the evolving diagnostic context. KAMAC begins with one or more expert agents and then conducts a knowledge-driven discussion to identify and fill knowledge gaps by recruiting additional specialists as needed. This supports flexible, scalable collaboration in complex clinical scenarios, with decisions finalized through reviewing updated agent comments. Experiments on two real-world medical benchmarks demonstrate that KAMAC significantly outperforms both single-agent and advanced multi-agent methods, particularly in complex clinical scenarios (i.e., cancer prognosis) requiring dynamic, cross-specialty expertise. Our code is publicly available at: https://github.com/XiaoXiao-Woo/KAMAC.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces KAMAC (Knowledge-driven Adaptive Multi-Agent Collaboration), a novel framework designed to enhance medical decision-making using Large Language Models (LLMs).  KAMAC addresses limitations in existing multi-agent collaboration (MAC) approaches, which often rely on static, pre-assigned roles that hinder adaptability and dynamic knowledge integration.  KAMAC dynamically forms and expands expert teams based on the evolving diagnostic context. It initiates with one or more expert agents, conducts a knowledge-driven discussion to identify and fill knowledge gaps by recruiting additional specialists. The final decision is reached after reviewing updated agent comments. The authors evaluated KAMAC on two real-world medical benchmarks, MedQA and Progn-VQA, demonstrating its superior performance compared to single-agent and advanced multi-agent methods, particularly in complex clinical scenarios like cancer prognosis. The code is publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The key novelty lies in the dynamic and adaptive nature of the expert team formation.  Unlike previous MAC methods that rely on pre-defined roles or static expert assignments, KAMAC allows LLM agents to actively identify and fill knowledge gaps by recruiting new specialists during the discussion. This "on-demand" expertise acquisition mimics the real-world collaborative processes of multidisciplinary medical teams.  The integration of a knowledge gap detection mechanism is also a distinct contribution. The idea of using LLMs to simulate collaborative environments isn't entirely new, but the adaptive recruitment aspect significantly advances the state-of-the-art.

*   **Significance:** Medical decision-making is inherently complex, requiring expertise from multiple specialties.  By demonstrating that LLMs can effectively emulate this collaborative process in a dynamic and adaptable way, the paper holds substantial potential. It offers a promising approach for improving diagnostic accuracy, particularly in complex cases where cross-specialty expertise is crucial. The improved performance on the Progn-VQA dataset, which requires integrating clinical and imaging information, highlights KAMAC's ability to handle complex, real-world scenarios. The public availability of the code will facilitate further research and potentially real-world applications.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Adaptive collaboration:** The framework's core contribution lies in the dynamic and adaptive way agents form and expand expert teams, which contrasts with the static approach of previous works.
    *   **Knowledge-driven approach:** The explicit focus on detecting and filling knowledge gaps is a unique and potentially valuable contribution.
    *   **Empirical validation:** The paper presents strong empirical evidence on two challenging medical datasets, demonstrating significant improvements over existing methods.
    *   **Clear writing and code availability:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow. The public code release is a major plus, enabling reproducibility and future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on GPT-4.1-mini:** While the authors justify their choice of GPT-4.1-mini due to its reliability and deterministic behavior for controlled evaluations, the reliance on this particular model might limit the generalizability of the results.  It would be beneficial to see how KAMAC performs with other state-of-the-art LLMs (especially open-source models).  The DeepSeek-R1 comparison is a step in the right direction.
    *   **Limited Scale and Deployment Considerations:**  The experiments are conducted in a controlled environment. Real-world clinical implementation will likely present challenges related to patient data privacy, security, integration with existing healthcare systems, and the need for human oversight and validation. The paper doesn't fully address these practical deployment considerations.
    *   **Cost implications:** Even though a cost analysis is included, the actual expenses of implementing and maintaining KAMAC in a clinical setting, accounting for model usage, API calls, and potential personnel training, remain unclear.
    *   **Evaluation of "wrong" expert recruitment**. One could imagine that some experts recommended by the approach are not helpful for the particular patient. It would be insightful to have some analysis regarding this aspect.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in multi-agent collaboration for medical decision-making. It introduces a valuable framework and a compelling argument for adaptive expertise acquisition.  The knowledge gap detection mechanism can be further refined and integrated into other collaborative systems.  The findings could also encourage more research into the ethical implications of using LLMs in critical domains like healthcare.

**Score:** 8

**Justification:**

KAMAC presents a significant advancement in multi-agent collaboration for medical decision-making by introducing a novel adaptive expert recruitment mechanism. The empirical results are compelling, demonstrating clear improvements over existing methods on challenging medical datasets. While the paper has some limitations, particularly its reliance on a specific LLM and limited discussion of real-world deployment challenges, its contribution to the field is substantial. The knowledge-driven approach and dynamic team formation represent a valuable step towards creating more robust and adaptable AI systems for healthcare. The code release further strengthens the paper's impact and facilitates future research.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### Sea-ing Through Scattered Rays: Revisiting the Image Formation Model for Realistic Underwater Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15011v1)
- **Authors**: Vasiliki Ismiroglou, Malte Pedersen, Stefan H. Bengtson, Andreas Aakerberg, Thomas B. Moeslund
- **Abstract**: In recent years, the underwater image formation model has found extensive use in the generation of synthetic underwater data. Although many approaches focus on scenes primarily affected by discoloration, they often overlook the model's ability to capture the complex, distance-dependent visibility loss present in highly turbid environments. In this work, we propose an improved synthetic data generation pipeline that includes the commonly omitted forward scattering term, while also considering a nonuniform medium. Additionally, we collected the BUCKET dataset under controlled turbidity conditions to acquire real turbid footage with the corresponding reference images. Our results demonstrate qualitative improvements over the reference model, particularly under increasing turbidity, with a selection rate of 82. 5\% by survey participants. Data and code can be accessed on the project page: vap.aau.dk/sea-ing-through-scattered-rays.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the limitations of existing underwater image formation models (IFMs) when generating synthetic data, especially in turbid environments. It argues that many approaches oversimplify the model, particularly by neglecting the forward scattering term and assuming a uniform medium.  The authors propose an improved synthetic data generation pipeline that reintroduces the forward scattering term, considers non-uniformity in the medium using Gaussian Random Fields (GRFs), and adjusts the attenuation coefficient.  They also introduce a new dataset, BUCKET, collected under controlled turbidity conditions with corresponding reference images, to evaluate the realism of synthetic images.  A user study demonstrates qualitative improvements over the standard IFM, especially in high turbidity conditions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of reintroducing the forward scattering term and modeling medium non-uniformity in IFMs is not entirely novel. Some previous works acknowledge the forward scattering component (though often neglect its implementation for simplicity), and the concept of varying water parameters isn’t groundbreaking. The innovation lies in their explicit focus on *turbid* environments, where the impact of these factors is more pronounced, and in the methodical way they re-evaluate these components within a complete pipeline. The introduction of the BUCKET dataset is a more significant contribution. The dataset provides a valuable resource for evaluating synthetic underwater image generation under controlled conditions, which is often missing. The use of GRFs to model medium non-uniformity is an interesting, albeit not revolutionary, application.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper is moderate. The improvements in the realism of synthetic images, as indicated by the user study, are valuable.  More realistic synthetic data can benefit training and evaluation of underwater image processing algorithms, reducing the sim-to-real gap.  The BUCKET dataset can serve as a benchmark for future research in this area. However, the qualitative evaluation through a user study has inherent limitations. A quantitative analysis on downstream tasks such as image restoration or object detection trained on synthetic images would have further strengthened the paper.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear explanation of the limitations of the standard IFM, especially in turbid environments.
    *   Well-motivated reintroduction of the forward scattering term and modeling of non-uniformity.
    *   The introduction and release of the BUCKET dataset is a strong contribution.
    *   Demonstrated (qualitative) improvements in realism through a user study.
    *   Thorough references and discussion of related work.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The quantitative evaluation is missing. While the user study provides valuable insights, it is still subjective.  Measuring the performance of algorithms trained on the generated data on real-world images would provide more objective evidence of the method's effectiveness.
    *   The description of how the spectral response of Nikon D90 was obtained in the evaluation survey is vague.
    *   The claim of full-wavelength computation of the model is not fully elaborated.
    *   The selection of the empirical limits for parameters such as g and μ is not explained in detail. A sensitivity analysis of these parameters may strengthen the paper.
    *   The non-uniformity model is simplified with GRFs. It might not be a comprehensive representation of actual underwater turbulence.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper can influence future research by highlighting the importance of forward scattering in turbid underwater environments.  The BUCKET dataset can provide a valuable benchmark for future methods. It's likely that the more realistic synthetic data will be used to pre-train models for underwater computer vision tasks.

*   **Conclusion:**
    The paper provides a valuable contribution by revisiting the image formation model with a focus on turbid environments, an area frequently overlooked. The reintroduction of forward scattering and modeling medium inhomogeneities demonstrably increase the generated image realism. The BUCKET dataset also provides a foundation for controlled experimental data. However, the lack of rigorous, quantifiable metrics and specific analysis of the downstream task performance limit its broader impact.
**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Mind the Gap: A Closer Look at Tokenization for Multiple-Choice Question Answering with LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15020v1)
- **Authors**: Mario Sanz-Guerrero, Minh Duc Bui, Katharina von der Wense
- **Abstract**: When evaluating large language models (LLMs) with multiple-choice question answering (MCQA), it is common to end the prompt with the string "Answer:" to facilitate automated answer extraction via next-token probabilities. However, there is no consensus on how to tokenize the space following the colon, often overlooked as a trivial choice. In this paper, we uncover accuracy differences of up to 11% due to this (seemingly irrelevant) tokenization variation as well as reshuffled model rankings, raising concerns about the reliability of LLM comparisons in prior work. Surprisingly, we are able to recommend one specific strategy -- tokenizing the space together with the answer letter -- as we observe consistent and statistically significant performance improvements. Additionally, it improves model calibration, enhancing the reliability of the model's confidence estimates. Our findings underscore the importance of careful evaluation design and highlight the need for standardized, transparent evaluation protocols to ensure reliable and comparable results.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper, along with a novelty/significance score:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the impact of a seemingly trivial choice in prompting Large Language Models (LLMs) for multiple-choice question answering (MCQA): how to tokenize the space character following the "Answer:" string. The authors compare two approaches: tokenizing the space independently or including it as part of the answer option token.  Their experiments across various LLMs, datasets, and prompt formulations reveal that tokenizing the space *together* with the answer letter consistently leads to statistically significant gains in accuracy and calibration, even altering model rankings. The paper argues that this overlooked tokenization choice can significantly affect LLM evaluation, highlighting the need for standardized evaluation protocols and greater transparency, especially for closed-source models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Identifies a Neglected Factor:** The paper brings attention to a genuinely overlooked aspect of LLM evaluation.  While prompt engineering and choice order bias have received attention, the tokenization of whitespace is often treated as an implementation detail.
    *   **Rigorous Empirical Analysis:**  The experiments are comprehensive, covering a wide range of LLMs (both open-source and differing architectures), multiple MCQA datasets, and several prompt variations (few-shot, chain-of-thought, and cross-lingual testing). This strengthens the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Statistically Sound:** The use of McNemar's test and bootstrap resampling on ECE provides statistical rigor, bolstering the claims of significant improvements.
    *   **Demonstrates Practical Impact:**  The paper shows that this subtle tokenization choice can significantly affect model accuracy (up to 11%) and influence model ranking in leaderboards, thus highlighting practical implications.
    *   **Improves Calibration:** Improved calibration is a valuable finding, making the model's confidence scores more reliable, which can inform real-world applications.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors clearly describe their experimental setup, enabling future work to replicate and extend their findings.
*   **Weaknesses:**
