    *   **Limited to MCQA format:** The findings are primarily applicable to MCQA tasks using the "Answer:" prompting strategy. Although MCQA is a common setting, the conclusions may not directly transfer to other generation or evaluation paradigms.
    *   **Lacks Mechanistic Explanation:** While the paper provides empirical evidence, it offers limited mechanistic explanation as to *why* this tokenization strategy improves performance. The discussion of embedding similarity is a start, but more in-depth analysis is needed to understand the underlying reasons.
    *   **Focus on smaller-to-medium sized LLMs** The paper acknowledges a limitation in using smaller to medium sized LLMs. It suggests that further testing with larger models would benefit their overall findings.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   **Novelty:** The identification and systematic analysis of the impact of whitespace tokenization on LLM performance in MCQA tasks are novel. The study demonstrates that factors previously considered insignificant can have major influence in model performance.
    *   **Significance:** The work highlights a source of inconsistency in LLM evaluation that can lead to unreliable comparisons.  The findings call for greater transparency and standardization in evaluation protocols, potentially influencing how LLM benchmarks are designed and interpreted. By directly calling for improvements to be made to benchmarks, the paper will likely influence the direction of future research.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by uncovering a previously overlooked factor in LLM evaluation. The rigorous experimental design and statistical validation support the claims effectively. The practical implications for leaderboard rankings and model calibration elevate the significance of the work. The weakness comes from the lack of mechanistic explanation of how the whitespace contributes to the overall improvements in accuracy. A clear and convincing explanation of the process would elevate the paper.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### CLEAR: A Comprehensive Linguistic Evaluation of Argument Rewriting by Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15027v1)
- **Authors**: Thomas Huber, Christina Niklaus
- **Abstract**: While LLMs have been extensively studied on general text generation tasks, there is less research on text rewriting, a task related to general text generation, and particularly on the behavior of models on this task. In this paper we analyze what changes LLMs make in a text rewriting setting. We focus specifically on argumentative texts and their improvement, a task named Argument Improvement (ArgImp). We present CLEAR: an evaluation pipeline consisting of 57 metrics mapped to four linguistic levels: lexical, syntactic, semantic and pragmatic. This pipeline is used to examine the qualities of LLM-rewritten arguments on a broad set of argumentation corpora and compare the behavior of different LLMs on this task and analyze the behavior of different LLMs on this task in terms of linguistic levels. By taking all four linguistic levels into consideration, we find that the models perform ArgImp by shortening the texts while simultaneously increasing average word length and merging sentences. Overall we note an increase in the persuasion and coherence dimensions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CLEAR, a comprehensive evaluation pipeline for assessing how Large Language Models (LLMs) rewrite argumentative texts. CLEAR utilizes 57 metrics across four linguistic levels (lexical, syntactic, semantic, and pragmatic) to analyze changes made by LLMs in an "Argument Improvement" (ArgImp) setting.  The authors apply CLEAR to a set of argumentation corpora, compare the behavior of different LLMs, and investigate biases exhibited during rewriting.  The key findings indicate that LLMs tend to shorten arguments, increase average word length, merge sentences, and enhance coherence and persuasiveness.  The analysis also explores verbosity and positivity biases.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:* The paper's novelty lies primarily in its *systematic and multi-faceted* evaluation framework (CLEAR) specifically designed for analyzing LLM behavior in the ArgImp task. While individual metrics used in CLEAR are not new, the combination and mapping to the four linguistic levels, along with the specific focus on argumentative text rewriting, is a significant contribution.  The investigation of length and positivity biases in this context is also a novel exploration. However, the paper does not introduce new methodologies to measure each linguistic level.

*Significance:* The paper addresses a relevant and relatively underexplored area: understanding the linguistic transformations LLMs perform when improving argumentative texts.  This is significant for several reasons:

1.  *ArgImp is a valuable task:* Improving arguments has practical applications in education, communication, and automated reasoning.
2.  *LLM capabilities need scrutiny:* Understanding *how* LLMs perform ArgImp, beyond just measuring overall improvement, is crucial for building trust and control. The CLEAR pipeline offers this nuanced understanding.
3.  *Bias detection is important:* Identifying and mitigating biases in LLM-generated argumentative texts is essential for fairness and objectivity.

*Strengths:*

*   *Comprehensive Evaluation Pipeline:* CLEAR offers a detailed and systematic approach to evaluate LLM-rewritten arguments.
*   *Multi-level Linguistic Analysis:* Analyzing the outputs at lexical, syntactic, semantic, and pragmatic levels provides a rich understanding of the LLM's behavior.
*   *Bias Investigation:* The paper identifies and explores potential length and positivity biases.
*   *Quantitative and Qualitative Analysis:* The combination of metrics and manual analysis provides a more holistic assessment.
*  *Open Source:* CLEAR being open source makes the work highly valuable and reproducible.

*Weaknesses:*

*   *Lack of Novelty in individual metrics:* While the framework itself is a contribution, the individual metrics are mostly existing techniques. The true value stems from their careful curation, mapping and application to ArgImp.
*   *Limited user study:*  While the manual analysis is useful, the study relies on internal annotators, and a more formal user study would strengthen the assessment of the perceived quality and persuasiveness of the rewritten arguments.
*   *Scope limitations:* The study acknowledges limitations in its analysis of content-based aspects and argumentation theory; however, the paper would be significantly strengthened by adding more fine grained details regarding the prompt design process, and a more thorough discussion about the selection of prompts.

*Potential Influence:* The CLEAR pipeline can serve as a valuable tool for researchers working on LLM-based argumentation, text rewriting, and controllable text generation.  It could also inform the development of more robust and unbiased LLMs for ArgImp tasks. The pipeline design offers flexibility, allowing others to extend and refine it with additional metrics or focus on specific linguistic levels. Furthermore, the approach is readily adapted to other text generation tasks, contributing to a more holistic assessment of the language generation pipeline.

*Score:* 7.5

*Rationale:* While the paper does not present groundbreaking new individual techniques or theoretical breakthroughs, the rigorous application and structured framework (CLEAR) provide a valuable tool for the field. The thorough analysis of LLM behavior, coupled with the investigation of biases, makes a significant contribution. The paper would be further strengthened by including a more extensive and fine grained analysis in the prompt design and selection process, and also, additional user studies to evaluate the perceived effectiveness of the arguments. However, given the current state, the significance of addressing a relevant yet under-explored task and creating a tool that can aid the study and mitigation of LLM behaviors in argumentation supports the assigned score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### AutoEdit: Automatic Hyperparameter Tuning for Image Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15031v1)
- **Authors**: Chau Pham, Quan Dao, Mahesh Bhosale, Yunjie Tian, Dimitris Metaxas, David Doermann
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in diffusion models have revolutionized text-guided image editing, yet existing editing methods face critical challenges in hyperparameter identification. To get the reasonable editing performance, these methods often require the user to brute-force tune multiple interdependent hyperparameters, such as inversion timesteps and attention modification, \textit{etc.} This process incurs high computational costs due to the huge hyperparameter search space. We consider searching optimal editing's hyperparameters as a sequential decision-making task within the diffusion denoising process. Specifically, we propose a reinforcement learning framework, which establishes a Markov Decision Process that dynamically adjusts hyperparameters across denoising steps, integrating editing objectives into a reward function. The method achieves time efficiency through proximal policy optimization while maintaining optimal hyperparameter configurations. Experiments demonstrate significant reduction in search time and computational overhead compared to existing brute-force approaches, advancing the practical deployment of a diffusion-based image editing framework in the real world.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "AutoEdit: Automatic Hyperparameter Tuning for Image Editing" addresses the challenge of manually tuning hyperparameters in text-guided image editing using diffusion models. The authors propose a reinforcement learning (RL) framework where hyperparameter selection during the diffusion denoising process is treated as a sequential decision-making task. A Markov Decision Process (MDP) is established to dynamically adjust hyperparameters across denoising steps, and a reward function integrates prompt alignment and background preservation objectives.  The method utilizes Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) for efficient learning, aiming to reduce the computational overhead associated with brute-force hyperparameter search. The paper demonstrates, through experiments, that AutoEdit can significantly reduce search time and computational cost while achieving comparable or better results compared to manual tuning or brute-force search baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel approach by framing hyperparameter optimization in diffusion-based image editing as a reinforcement learning problem. While RL has been used in image generation and prompt optimization, its application to *hyperparameter tuning within the denoising process* for image editing is a more specific and arguably less explored area. The concept of dynamically adjusting hyperparameters at each denoising step guided by a reward function that balances competing objectives (prompt alignment and background preservation) is a key aspect of its novelty.
*   **Significance:** The practical significance of the work lies in its potential to reduce the computational burden and usability barriers associated with existing image editing methods.  Manual hyperparameter tuning is a significant bottleneck for non-expert users and consumes considerable computational resources. AutoEdit aims to automate this process, making diffusion-based editing more accessible and efficient. By demonstrating a reduction in search time compared to brute-force methods, the paper suggests a tangible improvement in workflow. The focus on both quantitative and qualitative improvements, alongside the generalizability across various editing frameworks (DDIM/DDPM-Inversion, P2P, MasaCtrl, UltraEdit, InstructPix2Pix) adds to the paper's significance.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined RL framework with a suitable MDP formulation and reward function.
    *   Empirical validation demonstrates performance improvements over baselines in terms of speed and editing quality (as measured by various metrics).
    *   Demonstrates generalizability across different editing frameworks and diffusion models.
    *   The ablation study provides insights into the importance of phase-1 policy initialization and the influence of reward function coefficients.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The improvement gains, while present, may not be overwhelmingly large in certain cases.  A more detailed analysis of the situations where AutoEdit excels or struggles would be beneficial.
    *   The choice of reward function components (CLIP Score and MSE) is fairly standard. While the balancing act is important, the core components aren't groundbreaking.
    *   The paper could benefit from more rigorous comparisons to other existing hyperparameter optimization techniques (e.g., Bayesian Optimization, evolutionary algorithms). The brute force baseline is a low bar.
    *   The experiments are primarily focused on image editing. While this is the scope of the paper, it would be beneficial to explore the potential for applying this framework to other tasks, such as image generation or super-resolution.
    *   The method relies on the underlying editing methods’ ability to perform the requested edits. If the underlying method is poor, the hyperparameter search cannot overcome this limitation, as acknowledged in the paper.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by encouraging more research into automated hyperparameter tuning for diffusion-based image editing. It provides a viable approach and could inspire the development of more sophisticated RL-based or other optimization techniques. It could also lead to more user-friendly image editing tools that require less manual intervention.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper makes a worthwhile contribution by tackling a practical problem in diffusion-based image editing. The RL framework is well-formulated, and the experimental results demonstrate tangible improvements in efficiency and editing quality. However, the novelty is somewhat incremental (RL application to a specific sub-problem), and the reliance on standard reward components and the basic brute-force baseline comparison somewhat limits the impact. While the generalizability across different editing frameworks is a plus, a more comprehensive comparative analysis against other hyperparameter optimization methods is needed. The reliance on the quality of the underlying editing method is also a limitation. Considering these factors, a rigorous assessment suggests a score of **7**.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Communication Efficient Split Learning of ViTs with Attention-based Double Compression
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15058v1)
- **Authors**: Federico Alvetreti, Jary Pomponi, Paolo Di Lorenzo, Simone Scardapane
- **Abstract**: This paper proposes a novel communication-efficient Split Learning (SL) framework, named Attention-based Double Compression (ADC), which reduces the communication overhead required for transmitting intermediate Vision Transformers activations during the SL training process. ADC incorporates two parallel compression strategies. The first one merges samples' activations that are similar, based on the average attention score calculated in the last client layer; this strategy is class-agnostic, meaning that it can also merge samples having different classes, without losing generalization ability nor decreasing final results. The second strategy follows the first and discards the least meaningful tokens, further reducing the communication cost. Combining these strategies not only allows for sending less during the forward pass, but also the gradients are naturally compressed, allowing the whole model to be trained without additional tuning or approximations of the gradients. Simulation results demonstrate that Attention-based Double Compression outperforms state-of-the-art SL frameworks by significantly reducing communication overheads while maintaining high accuracy.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents Attention-based Double Compression (ADC), a communication-efficient Split Learning (SL) framework specifically designed for Vision Transformers (ViTs).  ADC aims to reduce the communication overhead during SL training by compressing the intermediate ViT activations. The method employs a two-pronged compression strategy: first, it merges activations from similar samples based on their average attention scores from the last client layer. Second, it discards the least meaningful tokens from the merged activations.  The paper demonstrates through simulations that ADC outperforms existing SL frameworks by significantly reducing communication overhead while maintaining high accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**
    * **Relevance:** The problem addressed (communication bottlenecks in Split Learning) is highly relevant and timely, especially given the growing interest in edge computing and privacy-preserving machine learning.
    * **Methodological Novelty:** The two-stage compression strategy (sample merging followed by token selection) is a novel combination. Using attention scores for both merging and selection creates a consistent and arguably more semantically aware compression strategy. The fact that it's class-agnostic is also a significant advantage.
    * **Empirical Evaluation:** The experimental setup seems comprehensive, comparing ADC against several relevant baselines (BottleNet++, Top-K, RandTopK, C3-SL) across multiple datasets (CIFAR100, Food101) and ViT architectures (DeiT-S, DeiT-T).  The ablation studies provide further insights into the method's components.
    * **Performance Gains:** The results clearly demonstrate ADC's superior performance in reducing communication while preserving accuracy, particularly in the low-compression regime. The stability of convergence is also noteworthy.
    * **Well-Written:** The paper is clearly written and organized, making it easy to follow the proposed method and understand the experimental results.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Generality:** While the paper focuses on ViTs, it is unclear how well the approach generalizes to other types of neural networks. The core idea of using attention for compression is tied to the Transformer architecture, potentially limiting its applicability to models without attention mechanisms.
    * **Computational Overhead:** Although the paper emphasizes communication efficiency, it does not thoroughly analyze the computational overhead of the ADC method itself (K-means clustering, attention score calculations). This overhead could be significant, especially on resource-constrained edge devices, partially offsetting the communication benefits. This aspect deserves more scrutiny.
    * **Ablation Study Depth:** While the ablation studies explore batch size, splitting point, and merging vectors, they could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the impact of each compression stage separately. What is the relative contribution of batch merging and token selection? How does the sensitivity of the performance vary with respect to the number of clusters `T` and top-k tokens `k`?
    * **Dataset Specifics:** Using primarily image datasets limits the scope. Showing performance on text or other modalities would strengthen the claim of broad applicability.
    * **Scalability to Multi-Client Setting:** The current setup is suitable for SL between a single client and server. However, it does not address challenges related to a more complex, multi-client federated learning setting.

* **Significance:**

The paper contributes a practically useful and effective method for communication-efficient Split Learning with Vision Transformers.  If the computational overhead is well-managed, it offers a real improvement over existing techniques.  The attention-based approach is a valuable contribution and could inspire further research into semantically-aware compression strategies for distributed machine learning. The work has the potential to be adopted in scenarios where bandwidth is limited, and privacy is a concern.

**Justification for Score:**

The ADC method presents a novel and effective way to tackle the communication bottleneck in Split Learning. It exhibits superior performance compared to baseline approaches, as evidenced by the experimental results. The work is well-motivated and contributes a practically relevant solution to a key challenge in distributed machine learning. However, the limited generality of the method (tied to attention mechanisms) and the lack of detailed analysis of its computational cost detract from its overall impact. Also, the scalability has to be assessed.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### QuizRank: Picking Images by Quizzing VLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15059v1)
- **Authors**: Tenghao Ji, Eytan Adar
- **Abstract**: Images play a vital role in improving the readability and comprehension of Wikipedia articles by serving as `illustrative aids.' However, not all images are equally effective and not all Wikipedia editors are trained in their selection. We propose QuizRank, a novel method of image selection that leverages large language models (LLMs) and vision language models (VLMs) to rank images as learning interventions. Our approach transforms textual descriptions of the article's subject into multiple-choice questions about important visual characteristics of the concept. We utilize these questions to quiz the VLM: the better an image can help answer questions, the higher it is ranked. To further improve discrimination between visually similar items, we introduce a Contrastive QuizRank that leverages differences in the features of target (e.g., a Western Bluebird) and distractor concepts (e.g., Mountain Bluebird) to generate questions. We demonstrate the potential of VLMs as effective visual evaluators by showing a high congruence with human quiz-takers and an effective discriminative ranking of images.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, "QuizRank: Picking Images by Quizzing VLMs."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces QuizRank, a novel approach for selecting images to illustrate Wikipedia articles. It leverages Vision-Language Models (VLMs) to evaluate images based on their ability to answer multiple-choice questions generated from the article's textual content. The core idea is that images that are more helpful in answering these questions are better "illustrative aids."  The method includes a contrastive refinement step, where questions are designed to distinguish the target concept from visually similar distractors. This helps in selecting images that highlight unique features. The paper evaluates QuizRank by comparing VLM performance with human quiz-takers and assessing its ability to identify better images compared to Wikipedia's crowdsourced approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers a fresh perspective on image selection by reframing it as a learning assessment task. Using VLMs to generate quizzes and subsequently evaluate images based on quiz performance is a novel contribution.  The contrastive question generation step further enhances the system's ability to select visually discriminative images. While previous works have used VLMs to assess image-text alignment and relevance, QuizRank introduces the novel idea of using the VLM as an evaluator for image quality by proxy of quiz taking performance.

*   **Significance:** Image selection in Wikipedia is a challenging task with significant impact on readability, comprehension, and user engagement. Automating this process using a data-driven approach can greatly benefit Wikipedia editors and improve the overall quality of the encyclopedia. The system's ability to select high-quality images with reduced reliance on human labor is particularly valuable.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined Approach:** The QuizRank methodology is clearly explained and well-structured, including question generation, VLM quiz-taking, and image ranking.
    *   **Contrastive Question Generation:** The concept of generating questions that discriminate between similar entities is a significant strength, as it addresses the challenge of selecting images that highlight unique features.
    *   **Evaluation:** The experiments are well-designed, comparing VLM performance with human participants and also assessing whether VLM relies on external knowledge vs information within the image.
    *   **Open-source Comparison:**  The comparison to open-source models and their related metrics allows one to assess the generalizability of the approach.
    *   **Real World Significance:**  The evaluation with real Wikipedia concepts and distractor pairs provides a solid grounding for the work.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Scope of applicability:** The approach works best for objects that have physical characteristics and are described in some detail in their Wikipedia entries. It is less suitable for abstract concepts or topics where visual representation is inherently limited.  As the authors acknowledge, questions focus on simpler visual properties so concepts that are purely biographical are difficult to evaluate visually.
    *   **Limited Evaluation:** The experiments are primarily focused on English Wikipedia and a curated set of concepts. It would be beneficial to evaluate the approach on a larger scale and across different languages to assess its generalizability.
    *   **Reliance on LLMs:** The performance of QuizRank is heavily dependent on the quality of the LLM and VLM. Advances or limitations in these models will directly impact the system's performance.
    *   **Potential biases:** While the paper controls for external knowledge use by VLMs (e.g., via training data memorization), the prompts themselves could introduce bias or affect the nature of the questions generated. The evaluation shows that baseline quiz scores produced by the VLM can be higher than humans.

*   **Impact:** The QuizRank approach has the potential to significantly impact image selection in Wikipedia and other similar platforms. It offers a scalable and data-driven method for identifying high-quality illustrative aids, reducing reliance on manual selection and subjective judgment. The methodology can be further refined and extended to address a wider range of concepts and visual representations. This type of framework is also a solid basis for further investigation.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of 7.5. While the idea of using VLMs to evaluate images as learning interventions is novel, the overall contribution has some limitations. The dependence on textual information and specific types of concepts restricts generalizability. The evaluation is well done, demonstrating the approach's potential but also highlighting the need for larger-scale experiments and addressing potential biases. Further, the approach relies heavily on state-of-the-art LLM and VLM models. Nevertheless, the paper contributes a significant step towards automated image selection for educational purposes and serves as a solid foundation for future research in this area. The contrastive question generation approach is particularly significant. The analysis on existing images vs. novel ones and how this affects the VLM model performance is also well done. The weaknesses do not significantly distract from the paper's overall potential and the authors address the main limitations appropriately.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Learning in Context: Personalizing Educational Content with Large Language Models to Enhance Student Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15068v1)
- **Authors**: Joy Jia Yin Lim, Daniel Zhang-Li, Jifan Yu, Xin Cong, Ye He, Zhiyuan Liu, Huiqin Liu, Lei Hou, Juanzi Li, Bin Xu
- **Abstract**: Standardized, one-size-fits-all educational content often fails to connect with students' individual backgrounds and interests, leading to disengagement and a perceived lack of relevance. To address this challenge, we introduce PAGE, a novel framework that leverages large language models (LLMs) to automatically personalize educational materials by adapting them to each student's unique context, such as their major and personal interests. To validate our approach, we deployed PAGE in a semester-long intelligent tutoring system and conducted a user study to evaluate its impact in an authentic educational setting. Our findings show that students who received personalized content demonstrated significantly improved learning outcomes and reported higher levels of engagement, perceived relevance, and trust compared to those who used standardized materials. This work demonstrates the practical value of LLM-powered personalization and offers key design implications for creating more effective, engaging, and trustworthy educational experiences.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PAGE, a novel framework that leverages large language models (LLMs) to personalize educational content, aiming to improve student engagement and learning outcomes. PAGE adapts standardized educational materials by incorporating information about a student's major and personal interests. The framework utilizes a retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) approach to generate contextually relevant and pedagogically sound content. A user study was conducted to evaluate the impact of PAGE in a semester-long intelligent tutoring system. The results demonstrate that students who received personalized content via PAGE experienced improved learning outcomes and higher engagement, perceived relevance, and trust compared to those using standardized materials. The paper demonstrates the practicality of LLM-powered personalization and offers design considerations for creating effective and trustworthy educational experiences.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its comprehensive approach to educational content personalization using LLMs and the rigorous evaluation conducted in a real-world educational setting. While prior work has explored LLMs in education, PAGE stands out by integrating cognitive and affective student profiling with a RAG approach and grounding content adaptation in established pedagogical principles like Bloom's Taxonomy and UDL. The study also identifies the importance of foundation model selection and the need for retrieval-augmentation for factual trustworthiness, which provides practical insights for future research.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its demonstration of the potential for LLMs to address the persistent challenge of engaging students with standardized educational content. The findings suggest that LLM-powered personalization can improve learning outcomes and foster a more positive learning experience. The detailed design implications presented could significantly impact the development of future educational technologies.
*   **Strengths:** The study's strengths include:
    *   A well-defined framework grounded in established pedagogical theories.
    *   A comprehensive user study conducted in a realistic educational setting.
    *   A mixed-methods approach combining quantitative and qualitative data.
    *   Clear identification of design implications for LLM-based personalization.
    *   Thorough discussion of ethical considerations and limitations.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The study's scope is limited to two introductory university courses.
    *   The participant sample is relatively small and potentially lacks demographic diversity.
    *   The evaluation captures only the immediate effects of personalization (short-term evaluation).
    *   The "novelty effect" of using a new system may have influenced the engagement metrics.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design and development of future educational technologies by demonstrating the feasibility and effectiveness of LLM-powered personalization. It could also inspire further research on how to create more engaging, effective, and trustworthy learning experiences.

**Rigorous Rationale:**
This paper showcases a strong methodology and addresses a vital challenge in the education sector with practical solutions, while critically assessing the limitations. The evaluation using established teaching theories to steer the LLM generates trust and improves educational experience. This is a major improvement over the standard methods.

Score: 8.

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 8/10

### Forecasting and Visualizing Air Quality from Sky Images with Vision-Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15076v1)
- **Authors**: Mohammad Saleh Vahdatpour, Maryam Eyvazi, Yanqing Zhang
- **Abstract**: Air pollution remains a critical threat to public health and environmental sustainability, yet conventional monitoring systems are often constrained by limited spatial coverage and accessibility. This paper proposes an AI-driven agent that predicts ambient air pollution levels from sky images and synthesizes realistic visualizations of pollution scenarios using generative modeling. Our approach combines statistical texture analysis with supervised learning for pollution classification, and leverages vision-language model (VLM)-guided image generation to produce interpretable representations of air quality conditions. The generated visuals simulate varying degrees of pollution, offering a foundation for user-facing interfaces that improve transparency and support informed environmental decision-making. These outputs can be seamlessly integrated into intelligent applications aimed at enhancing situational awareness and encouraging behavioral responses based on real-time forecasts. We validate our method using a dataset of urban sky images and demonstrate its effectiveness in both pollution level estimation and semantically consistent visual synthesis. The system design further incorporates human-centered user experience principles to ensure accessibility, clarity, and public engagement in air quality forecasting. To support scalable and energy-efficient deployment, future iterations will incorporate a green CNN architecture enhanced with FPGA-based incremental learning, enabling real-time inference on edge platforms.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel AI-driven system for forecasting and visualizing air quality using sky images. The system combines several components: (1) statistical texture analysis and supervised learning (CNN) to classify pollution levels from sky images, (2) a Vision-Language Model (VLM) like BLIP-2 to generate realistic sky images based on the predicted pollution level (AQI category) using descriptive text prompts, and (3) a user-centered interface for improved clarity and public engagement.  The system aims to address the limitations of traditional air quality monitoring systems, like limited spatial coverage and interpretability, by providing both numerical forecasts and visual representations of pollution scenarios. The authors also discuss the potential for incorporating energy-efficient green CNN architectures for scalable deployment on edge platforms.  Experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the system in both pollution level estimation and visual synthesis.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper's primary novelty lies in the **integration of existing techniques in a specific way** to address the problem of air quality forecasting and visualization.  The individual components (CNNs, VLMs, Gabor features) are well-established, but their combination to create a unified system for visual and numerical air quality forecasting represents a novel contribution.  Specifically, the use of a VLM *as a semantic controller* to guide generative synthesis based on classification results is a unique approach in this domain.  The human-centered design aspect, although not fundamentally new, contributes to the overall impact by focusing on accessibility and user understanding. The concept of "Green CNN" architecture although mentioned only as future work, adds novelty when implemented as it targets energy-efficient edge deployment.

**Significance:**

The paper's significance comes from its potential to improve public awareness and engagement in environmental decision-making. By providing intuitive visual representations of air quality scenarios, the system can help individuals understand the impact of pollution and make informed choices. The system's focus on scalability and edge deployment also makes it potentially useful in resource-constrained environments where traditional monitoring systems are not feasible. The integration of VLM technology offers a pathway to systems that not only provide information, but also translate information into actionable insights.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-defined problem and clear goals:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing air quality monitoring systems and sets out to address them with a novel AI-driven solution.
*   **Modular and well-integrated architecture:** The system is composed of well-defined modules (classification, VLM control, generative synthesis) that work together seamlessly.
*   **Demonstrated effectiveness:** The experimental results show that the system achieves strong classification accuracy and generates realistic visual representations of pollution scenarios.
*   **User-centered design:** The focus on accessibility and user understanding enhances the potential impact of the system.
*   **Future direction towards Green AI:** Intends to incorporate a Green CNN architecture with FPGA-based incremental learning.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental improvements on existing techniques:**  While the integration is novel, the individual components are not groundbreaking. The paper primarily builds on established techniques.
*   **Limited novelty of results:** No ground breaking results in terms of accuracy or realism when compared to existing research. The innovation is in the approach.
*   **User Study Detail:** The user study could benefit from a larger sample size and more detailed analysis of the feedback.
*   **Green CNN just an idea:** The energy-efficient CNN architecture with FPGA-based incremental learning is mentioned only as a future plan and has not been implemented.
*   **Limited Evaluation:**  The evaluation focuses primarily on accuracy and visual realism. Other important aspects, such as the system's robustness to different weather conditions and its ability to generalize to new geographic locations, are not thoroughly evaluated.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of environmental AI by demonstrating a novel approach to air quality forecasting and visualization. While the individual components are not groundbreaking, their integration into a unified system with a user-centered design is a significant step forward. The future directions towards green AI and improved user experience are also promising.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper demonstrates a worthwhile integration of existing techniques, leading to a potentially impactful system. The score reflects the paper's strong potential and the novelty of its integration, while acknowledging that it relies on previously developed techniques and requires further investigation on Green AI and user evaluation to maximize its impact.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Adaptive LoRA Experts Allocation and Selection for Federated Fine-Tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15087v1)
- **Authors**: Lei Wang, Jieming Bian, Letian Zhang, Jie Xu
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated impressive capabilities across various tasks, but fine-tuning them for domain-specific applications often requires substantial domain-specific data that may be distributed across multiple organizations. Federated Learning (FL) offers a privacy-preserving solution, but faces challenges with computational constraints when applied to LLMs. Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) has emerged as a parameter-efficient fine-tuning approach, though a single LoRA module often struggles with heterogeneous data across diverse domains. This paper addresses two critical challenges in federated LoRA fine-tuning: 1. determining the optimal number and allocation of LoRA experts across heterogeneous clients, and 2. enabling clients to selectively utilize these experts based on their specific data characteristics. We propose FedLEASE (Federated adaptive LoRA Expert Allocation and SElection), a novel framework that adaptively clusters clients based on representation similarity to allocate and train domain-specific LoRA experts. It also introduces an adaptive top-$M$ Mixture-of-Experts mechanism that allows each client to select the optimal number of utilized experts. Our extensive experiments on diverse benchmark datasets demonstrate that FedLEASE significantly outperforms existing federated fine-tuning approaches in heterogeneous client settings while maintaining communication efficiency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Adaptive LoRA Experts Allocation and Selection for Federated Fine-Tuning" introduces FedLEASE, a novel federated learning framework designed to address the challenges of fine-tuning Large Language Models (LLMs) with LoRA in heterogeneous client settings. FedLEASE tackles two key problems: 1) determining the optimal number and allocation of LoRA experts across clients and 2) enabling clients to selectively utilize these experts based on their specific data. The framework adaptively clusters clients based on LoRA parameter similarity, trains domain-specific LoRA experts, and employs a novel adaptive top-M Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) mechanism for flexible expert selection. Experiments on NLU and NLG tasks demonstrate FedLEASE's superior performance compared to existing federated fine-tuning methods while maintaining communication efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper presents a well-motivated and reasonably novel approach to federated LoRA fine-tuning. While the idea of using MoE in federated learning isn't entirely new, the specific contributions of FedLEASE show clear innovation:

*   **Adaptive Clustering for Expert Allocation:**  The approach of clustering clients based on the similarity of LoRA 'B' matrices after a preliminary training phase to guide expert allocation is a novel and practical contribution. The justification for using 'B' matrices is also convincing.
*   **Adaptive Top-M MoE:** The introduced adaptive top-M MoE mechanism, which allows each client to dynamically determine the number of experts to use, improves upon existing static top-k MoE strategies. The innovative router architecture that expands the output space to allow for both single and ensemble expert selection is also a strong point.
*   **Systematic Solution to Dual Challenges:**  The framing of the problem as a need to address both expert *allocation* and *selection* is a valuable contribution in itself, clarifying the key challenges within this subfield of federated learning.

**Significance:**

The paper addresses a relevant and important problem: efficient and effective fine-tuning of LLMs in federated settings with heterogeneous data. FedLEASE offers a practical solution that is particularly significant for scenarios where data privacy is paramount.

*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The comprehensive experiments on diverse benchmark datasets (GLUE and FLAN) provide strong evidence supporting the effectiveness of FedLEASE. The ablation studies provide insight into the contribution of the key components, further bolstering the claims.
*   **Communication Efficiency:** Maintaining communication efficiency in federated learning is crucial. The paper clearly highlights this and emphasizes that FedLEASE achieves performance gains without sacrificing communication efficiency.

**Weaknesses and Limitations:**

*   **Complexity:** FedLEASE is more complex than simple federated LoRA fine-tuning methods, which may increase the implementation burden.  A detailed discussion of the memory overhead associated with the adaptive router would have been valuable.
*   **Static Clustering:** The method assumes a static client population and fixed expert assignments throughout the training process. The paper acknowledges this limitation, which could impact the approach's adaptability to changing real-world federated learning environments. Addressing this limitation with dynamic clustering strategies would be a good direction for future work.
*   **Lack of Real-world Datasets:** The evaluation focuses primarily on well-known benchmarks. While the results are compelling, demonstrating FedLEASE's effectiveness on real-world, industry-specific datasets would further enhance the paper's practical impact.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

I assign a score of **7.5**.

**Justification:**

FedLEASE introduces a novel and well-engineered framework for federated LoRA fine-tuning that significantly improves upon existing methods, particularly in heterogeneous settings. The paper is clearly written, well-motivated, and backed by strong empirical evidence.  The core innovations, namely the adaptive clustering for expert allocation and the adaptive top-M MoE mechanism, are significant contributions to the field.

However, the limitations, such as the static clustering and lack of real-world datasets, prevent a higher score. While the authors acknowledge the static clustering issue, a more detailed discussion of potential mitigation strategies would have strengthened the paper.  Addressing the limitations in future work will be important to maximize the impact and applicability of FedLEASE.
Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### LLM-OREF: An Open Relation Extraction Framework Based on Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15089v1)
- **Authors**: Hongyao Tu, Liang Zhang, Yujie Lin, Xin Lin, Haibo Zhang, Long Zhang, Jinsong Su
- **Abstract**: The goal of open relation extraction (OpenRE) is to develop an RE model that can generalize to new relations not encountered during training. Existing studies primarily formulate OpenRE as a clustering task. They first cluster all test instances based on the similarity between the instances, and then manually assign a new relation to each cluster. However, their reliance on human annotation limits their practicality. In this paper, we propose an OpenRE framework based on large language models (LLMs), which directly predicts new relations for test instances by leveraging their strong language understanding and generation abilities, without human intervention. Specifically, our framework consists of two core components: (1) a relation discoverer (RD), designed to predict new relations for test instances based on \textit{demonstrations} formed by training instances with known relations; and (2) a relation predictor (RP), used to select the most likely relation for a test instance from $n$ candidate relations, guided by \textit{demonstrations} composed of their instances. To enhance the ability of our framework to predict new relations, we design a self-correcting inference strategy composed of three stages: relation discovery, relation denoising, and relation prediction. In the first stage, we use RD to preliminarily predict new relations for all test instances. Next, we apply RP to select some high-reliability test instances for each new relation from the prediction results of RD through a cross-validation method. During the third stage, we employ RP to re-predict the relations of all test instances based on the demonstrations constructed from these reliable test instances. Extensive experiments on three OpenRE datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of our framework. We release our code at https://github.com/XMUDeepLIT/LLM-OREF.git.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLM-OREF, a framework for Open Relation Extraction (OpenRE) that leverages large language models (LLMs).  LLM-OREF aims to discover and predict new relations between entities in text without relying on human annotation. The framework consists of two main components: a Relation Discoverer (RD), which predicts potential new relations based on demonstrations of known relations, and a Relation Predictor (RP), which selects the most likely relation from a set of candidates, guided by demonstrations. A self-correcting inference strategy with three stages (relation discovery, denoising, and prediction) is also proposed to refine the predictions.  Experiments on three OpenRE datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of the approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main novelty lies in its direct application of LLMs to OpenRE in a generative way, moving away from traditional clustering-based approaches and avoiding the need for human annotation. The framework combines a Relation Discoverer and Relation Predictor, which is a clever approach to leverage LLMs, initially discovering potential relations and then using a second module to refine the prediction. The distillation approach to improve RD is also interesting.

*   **Significance:** OpenRE is a critical task for extracting knowledge from text when pre-defined relations are insufficient. Developing a system that can automatically discover new relations without human intervention has significant practical value. The results, showing superiority over existing OpenRE methods, support the significance of the framework. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component (RD, RP, denoising, etc.) of the framework.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The framework proposes a novel approach to OpenRE by using LLMs without human intervention.
    *   The modular design of RD and RP allows for effective knowledge distillation and refinement of predictions.
    *   The self-correcting inference strategy helps to improve the accuracy of new relation predictions.
    *   The experimental results show significant improvements over existing OpenRE methods on three datasets.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the importance of each component.
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed framework and experiments.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper demonstrates strong results, there's a lack of analysis of the types of relations that LLM-OREF can and cannot effectively discover. A qualitative analysis of the discovered relations and the reasons for success/failure would strengthen the paper.
    *   The datasets used are standard for OpenRE research but may not fully represent the complexity and diversity of relations in real-world scenarios.
    *   The framework relies on the performance of the underlying LLM. Therefore, advances in LLM technology may lead to further improvements in the performance of LLM-OREF.
    *   The experiments are limited to a small number of datasets and LLMs. Additional experiments with different datasets and LLMs are needed to further validate the effectiveness of the approach.

*   **Potential Impact:**  The paper has the potential to influence future research in OpenRE. It demonstrates the effectiveness of using LLMs for this task and provides a strong baseline for future methods. The framework's modular design and self-correcting inference strategy can serve as a template for other OpenRE methods. The code release also helps in accelerating and encouraging future research.

*   **Justification for Score:** Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, a score of **7.5** is assigned. The paper introduces a novel and effective approach to OpenRE that has the potential to significantly advance the field. While the limitations mentioned above are valid, they do not detract significantly from the value of the contribution. The paper shows a clear improvement over existing baselines and provides valuable insights into the use of LLMs for OpenRE.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### The Energy-Efficient Hierarchical Neural Network with Fast FPGA-Based Incremental Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15097v1)
- **Authors**: Mohammad Saleh Vahdatpour, Huaiyuan Chu, Yanqing Zhang
- **Abstract**: The rising computational and energy demands of deep learning, particularly in large-scale architectures such as foundation models and large language models (LLMs), pose significant challenges to sustainability. Traditional gradient-based training methods are inefficient, requiring numerous iterative updates and high power consumption. To address these limitations, we propose a hybrid framework that combines hierarchical decomposition with FPGA-based direct equation solving and incremental learning. Our method divides the neural network into two functional tiers: lower layers are optimized via single-step equation solving on FPGAs for efficient and parallelizable feature extraction, while higher layers employ adaptive incremental learning to support continual updates without full retraining. Building upon this foundation, we introduce the Compound LLM framework, which explicitly deploys LLM modules across both hierarchy levels. The lower-level LLM handles reusable representation learning with minimal energy overhead, while the upper-level LLM performs adaptive decision-making through energy-aware updates. This integrated design enhances scalability, reduces redundant computation, and aligns with the principles of sustainable AI. Theoretical analysis and architectural insights demonstrate that our method reduces computational costs significantly while preserving high model performance, making it well-suited for edge deployment and real-time adaptation in energy-constrained environments.
- **Summary**: The paper proposes a hybrid framework for energy-efficient training of large-scale neural networks, particularly LLMs, by combining hierarchical decomposition, FPGA-based direct equation solving, and incremental learning. It divides the network into two tiers: lower layers optimized for feature extraction using FPGAs and direct equation solving, and higher layers employing adaptive incremental learning for task-specific adaptation.  A "Compound LLM" architecture is introduced, which explicitly deploys LLM modules at both hierarchical levels to improve clarity and modularity. The paper provides theoretical analysis and architectural insights, claiming reduced computational costs and improved adaptability for edge deployment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a significant problem:** The paper tackles the critical issue of energy consumption in large-scale AI, particularly in LLMs, which is a major obstacle to sustainability.
*   **Hybrid Approach:** The combination of hierarchical decomposition, FPGA acceleration, and incremental learning is a potentially promising strategy to balance efficiency and adaptability, a key challenge in sustainable AI. The idea of applying direct equation solving to lower layers while using incremental learning for higher layers is a sensible approach to manage computational complexity.
*   **Compound LLM architecture:** Introducing the Compound LLM provides a concrete implementation strategy and potentially enhances clarity and modularity for deployment.
*   **Well-written and structured:** The paper is generally well-organized and provides a clear explanation of the proposed method and its potential benefits.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Lack of Empirical Validation:** The major weakness is the absence of experimental results. The paper is primarily theoretical and lacks concrete evidence to support its claims of reduced computational costs, improved adaptability, and energy efficiency.  While theoretical analysis is presented, it is not sufficient without practical validation. The paper mentions the need for further empirical validation, which is a significant drawback in the current state.
*   **Limited Novelty:** While the combination of techniques is interesting, the individual components (hierarchical decomposition, FPGA acceleration, incremental learning) are not novel in themselves. The novelty lies in the specific integration and application to LLMs, but the paper doesn't sufficiently demonstrate a novel architectural contribution.
*   **Scalability Concerns:** Although the paper aims to address scalability challenges, it acknowledges limitations of FPGA-based direct equation solving for extremely large models. While it mentions hybrid optimization strategies and software-hardware co-design to mitigate these, the specifics are not clearly elaborated. The Compound LLM itself doesn't explicitly resolve all the previously mentioned concerns about the limitations of direct equation solving at large scales, either.
*   **Practical Implementation Challenges:**  FPGA development requires specialized expertise, and integrating FPGA-based modules with adaptive learning frameworks introduces significant engineering complexity. The paper does not sufficiently address the practical challenges of implementing and deploying the proposed framework.
*   **Overselling:** The paper uses strong language in its claims (e.g., "significantly reduces computational costs," "well-suited for edge deployment") without backing them up with experimental evidence.

**Significance:**

The paper has the potential to contribute to the field of sustainable AI by proposing a plausible framework for energy-efficient LLM training. However, its lack of experimental validation and the inherent complexities in FPGA deployment limit its current significance. The idea of a compound LLM with optimized divisions based on energy expenditure and optimization strategy is valuable and has the potential to advance the field, although requires validation.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a reasonable combination of established methods to tackle a very important modern problem. But, without strong experimental results or validation of the compound-LLM design, it is difficult to assess the true impact of the work. Because it's mostly theoretical at this stage and lacks convincing empirical data, its novelty and near-term significance are limited.
Score: 5

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 5/10

### TextMine: LLM-Powered Knowledge Extraction for Humanitarian Mine Action
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15098v1)
- **Authors**: Chenyue Zhou, Gürkan Solmaz, Flavio Cirillo, Kiril Gashteovski, Jonathan Fürst
- **Abstract**: Humanitarian Mine Action has generated extensive best-practice knowledge, but much remains locked in unstructured reports. We introduce TextMine, an ontology-guided pipeline that uses Large Language Models to extract knowledge triples from HMA texts. TextMine integrates document chunking, domain-aware prompting, triple extraction, and both reference-based and LLM-as-a-Judge evaluation. We also create the first HMA ontology and a curated dataset of real-world demining reports. Experiments show ontology-aligned prompts boost extraction accuracy by 44.2%, cut hallucinations by 22.5%, and improve format conformance by 20.9% over baselines. While validated on Cambodian reports, TextMine can adapt to global demining efforts or other domains, transforming unstructured data into structured knowledge.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TextMine, an LLM-powered pipeline designed to extract knowledge triples from unstructured humanitarian mine action (HMA) reports. TextMine employs a combination of layout-aware document chunking, ontology-guided knowledge extraction (using a newly constructed HMA ontology), and a multi-perspective evaluation strategy incorporating both reference-based metrics and a reference-free LLM-as-a-Judge approach. The paper presents experiments demonstrating that ontology-aligned prompts significantly improve extraction accuracy and reduce hallucinations compared to baseline prompts. The authors also address the challenges of evaluating generated triples, particularly the limitations of reference-based approaches, and propose a bias-aware LLM-as-a-Judge framework as a potential alternative. They create a new HMA ontology and a human-annotated dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has several aspects of novelty. First, applying LLMs for knowledge extraction in the Humanitarian Mine Action (HMA) domain is itself a novel application.  Second, the creation of a dedicated HMA ontology is a valuable contribution. Third, the exploration and application of a bias-aware LLM-as-a-Judge framework for evaluating extracted triples, specifically addressing positional biases, represents a significant methodological advance. The combination of these elements, integrated into a single pipeline, further enhances the paper's originality.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a real-world problem by attempting to transform unstructured data in HMA reports into structured knowledge, which has the potential to improve decision-making and operational efficiency in demining efforts. The demonstration of improved extraction accuracy using ontology-aligned prompts is significant. The LLM-as-a-Judge evaluation framework opens possibilities for evaluating LLM-generated content when ground truth data is scarce.  The findings have practical implications for prompt design and evaluation strategies, particularly in high-stakes domains.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-structured and clearly written.
    *   The problem is well-motivated, highlighting the need for structured knowledge extraction in the HMA domain.
    *   The proposed pipeline, TextMine, is comprehensive and incorporates several key components.
    *   The experiments are thorough and demonstrate the effectiveness of ontology-aligned prompts and the potential of the LLM-as-a-Judge framework.
    *   The creation of a new HMA ontology and human-annotated triple dataset fills a valuable resource gap.
    *   The analysis of positional biases in LLM judges is insightful and leads to a practical solution (randomization) to mitigate these biases.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The scale of the evaluation dataset is relatively small (1,095 unique triples). While acknowledged by the authors, this limits the generalizability of the results.
    *   The evaluation focuses primarily on the *accuracy* of extracted triples, while aspects such as *completeness* (how many of the *possible* triples are extracted) receive less attention. This is likely a consequence of the complexities of the dataset annotation task, but is worth noting.
    *   The analysis of the LLM judge's own potential for hallucination could be expanded. While a reference-based approach is used to identify hallucinations in the extractor, there is a risk of the judge hallucinating *evaluation* criteria.
    *   The gains from paragraph-level context are not consistently realized, as shown by the lack of significant improvement in OP prompts over OS prompts.  This suggests that more sophisticated techniques for leveraging the paragraph context might be needed.
    * The evaluation of the final LLM-as-a-Judge pipeline only achieves 90% of the reference based metrics. More research could be done to explore if there are other parameters that should be tuned to achieve a higher result.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence several areas:
    *   **Humanitarian Mine Action:** By providing a practical framework for knowledge extraction, the paper could contribute to more efficient and data-driven demining operations.
    *   **Ontology-Guided Knowledge Extraction:** The work provides insights into how to effectively leverage ontologies to improve LLM-based extraction.
    *   **LLM Evaluation:** The LLM-as-a-Judge framework offers a promising direction for evaluating LLM-generated content when ground truth data is limited, prompting further research on mitigating biases and improving reliability.

Overall, the paper represents a solid contribution to the field.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**  The paper presents a combination of practical application, methodological innovation, and resource creation that warrants a score of 7.5. While the limitations related to dataset size and evaluation breadth temper its immediate impact, the paper's clear presentation, thorough experiments, and novel approach to LLM evaluation position it as a valuable contribution likely to stimulate further research in knowledge extraction and LLM-based evaluation, especially within domain-specific high-stakes applications. It falls short of being an "exceptional" contribution due to the dataset limitations, but it is clearly above average.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Large Language Model probabilities cannot distinguish between possible and impossible language
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15114v1)
- **Authors**: Evelina Leivada, Raquel Montero, Paolo Morosi, Natalia Moskvina, Tamara Serrano, Marcel Aguilar, Fritz Guenther
- **Abstract**: A controversial test for Large Language Models concerns the ability to discern possible from impossible language. While some evidence attests to the models' sensitivity to what crosses the limits of grammatically impossible language, this evidence has been contested on the grounds of the soundness of the testing material. We use model-internal representations to tap directly into the way Large Language Models represent the 'grammatical-ungrammatical' distinction. In a novel benchmark, we elicit probabilities from 4 models and compute minimal-pair surprisal differences, juxtaposing probabilities assigned to grammatical sentences to probabilities assigned to (i) lower frequency grammatical sentences, (ii) ungrammatical sentences, (iii) semantically odd sentences, and (iv) pragmatically odd sentences. The prediction is that if string-probabilities can function as proxies for the limits of grammar, the ungrammatical condition will stand out among the conditions that involve linguistic violations, showing a spike in the surprisal rates. Our results do not reveal a unique surprisal signature for ungrammatical prompts, as the semantically and pragmatically odd conditions consistently show higher surprisal. We thus demonstrate that probabilities do not constitute reliable proxies for model-internal representations of syntactic knowledge. Consequently, claims about models being able to distinguish possible from impossible language need verification through a different methodology.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates whether Large Language Models (LLMs) can genuinely distinguish between grammatically possible and impossible language based on their assigned probabilities. The authors challenge the assumption that probability assignment is a reliable proxy for grammaticality. They designed a novel benchmark using minimal-pair surprisal differences across four LLMs, comparing grammatical sentences with those exhibiting low frequency, ungrammaticality, pragmatic oddity, and semantic oddity. Their results demonstrate that LLMs do not exhibit a unique surprisal signature for ungrammatical prompts, with semantic and pragmatic violations often showing higher surprisal rates. The authors conclude that probabilities are not reliable proxies for syntactic knowledge and that claims about LLMs distinguishing between possible and impossible language need further validation with alternative methodologies. They also critique previous work, arguing that experimental designs often artificially restrict linguistic violations, leading to potentially misleading conclusions. They call for a re-evaluation of both the testing methods and theoretical implications concerning the linguistic abilities of LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper offers several points of novelty:

*   **Novel Benchmark:** It introduces a more nuanced benchmark with multiple types of linguistic violations, going beyond simple grammaticality distinctions.
*   **Challenging Assumptions:** It critically examines the common assumption that probability assignment in LLMs directly reflects grammatical knowledge.
*   **Methodological Critique:** It identifies potential confounds and limitations in previous experimental designs.
*   **Theoretical Implications:** It raises questions about the nature of linguistic knowledge in LLMs and the interpretation of their performance on language tasks.

**Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its rigorous challenge to prevailing views on the linguistic abilities of LLMs. By questioning the reliability of probability assignment as a proxy for grammatical knowledge, the authors contribute to a more nuanced understanding of what LLMs actually "know" about language. The methodological critique of previous studies encourages more careful experimental design and interpretation of results. The paper also highlights the need for theoretical frameworks that account for the complex interplay between syntax, semantics, and pragmatics in LLMs. The focus on the inherent limitations of binarized minimal-pair evaluations and potential influences of aspects such as low frequency, ungrammaticality, pragmatic oddity, and semantic oddity, is also valuable.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Research Question:** The paper clearly defines its research question and provides a well-reasoned motivation for the study.
*   **Rigorous Methodology:** The experimental design is sound and incorporates multiple conditions to address the research question comprehensively.
*   **Thorough Analysis:** The data analysis is meticulous and provides statistical evidence to support the conclusions.
*   **Critical Discussion:** The authors offer a thoughtful and nuanced discussion of the results, acknowledging the limitations of the study and proposing directions for future research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** The study focuses primarily on English and only four LLMs. Although the authors do mention that they have a multilingual study upcoming, the lack of diversity in the current work limits the generalizability of the findings.
*   **Interpretations are Dependent on Definitions:** The paper's interpretations depend on the definitions and operationalization of categories like "pragmatically odd" and "semantically odd." These categories are sometimes subjective.

**Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by:

*   Encouraging researchers to develop more sophisticated benchmarks for evaluating LLMs.
*   Promoting a more critical and nuanced interpretation of LLM performance on language tasks.
*   Stimulating further research on the nature of linguistic knowledge in LLMs and its relationship to syntax, semantics, and pragmatics.
*   Prompting a deeper consideration of the limitations of current testing methodologies.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by carefully dissecting a commonly held belief. It's not revolutionary in the sense of introducing a groundbreaking new technique, but it is critical and well-argued. It challenges the status quo, highlighting methodological pitfalls and urging a more nuanced interpretation of LLM capabilities.  The study's rigorous methodology and clear articulation of limitations strengthen its impact. While its scope is limited by language and models tested, the potential influence of prompting a more circumspect view of LLM capabilities in the field warrants a high score.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Prestige over merit: An adapted audit of LLM bias in peer review
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15122v1)
- **Authors**: Anthony Howell, Jieshu Wang, Luyu Du, Julia Melkers, Varshil Shah
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are playing an increasingly integral, though largely informal, role in scholarly peer review. Yet it remains unclear whether LLMs reproduce the biases observed in human decision-making. We adapt a resume-style audit to scientific publishing, developing a multi-role LLM simulation (editor/reviewer) that evaluates a representative set of high-quality manuscripts across the physical, biological, and social sciences under randomized author identities (institutional prestige, gender, race). The audit reveals a strong and consistent institutional-prestige bias: identical papers attributed to low-prestige affiliations face a significantly higher risk of rejection, despite only modest differences in LLM-assessed quality. To probe mechanisms, we generate synthetic CVs for the same author profiles; these encode large prestige-linked disparities and an inverted prestige-tenure gradient relative to national benchmarks. The results suggest that both domain norms and prestige-linked priors embedded in training data shape paper-level outcomes once identity is visible, converting affiliation into a decisive status cue.
- **Summary**: This paper explores the pervasive modality gap in multi-modal contrastive representation learning. While multi-modal models have shown promise in integrating inputs from different data modalities, a significant challenge remains: the modality gap. The paper likely delves into the geometric differences between feature distributions in different modalities, potentially identifying factors that contribute to this gap. The research may propose novel techniques or architectural modifications to address this issue and improve multi-modal representation learning.

**Evaluation:**

This paper tackles a critical problem in multi-modal representation learning. The concept of a "modality gap" is intriguing and deserves further exploration, especially as it hinders the effectiveness of multi-modal models. If the paper provides convincing evidence of this gap's existence and its impact, it could stimulate further research in this area.

However, the absence of specifics makes it difficult to gauge the paper's novelty and significance. Does the paper offer a novel perspective on the modality gap, or simply rehash existing knowledge? Does it propose a compelling solution, or merely suggest potential avenues for future research? The paper's value hinges on the answers to these questions. A weak paper might only reiterate known challenges without offering substantial insights. A high-impact paper would provide novel methods, compelling experiments, and clear evidence of improved multi-modal representation learning. Without more specifics, it is difficult to evaluate the paper beyond its conceptual merit.

**Score: 5**

- **Classification**: cs.CY
- **Score**: 5/10

### WorldForge: Unlocking Emergent 3D/4D Generation in Video Diffusion Model via Training-Free Guidance
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15130v1)
- **Authors**: Chenxi Song, Yanming Yang, Tong Zhao, Ruibo Li, Chi Zhang
- **Abstract**: Recent video diffusion models demonstrate strong potential in spatial intelligence tasks due to their rich latent world priors. However, this potential is hindered by their limited controllability and geometric inconsistency, creating a gap between their strong priors and their practical use in 3D/4D tasks. As a result, current approaches often rely on retraining or fine-tuning, which risks degrading pretrained knowledge and incurs high computational costs. To address this, we propose WorldForge, a training-free, inference-time framework composed of three tightly coupled modules. Intra-Step Recursive Refinement introduces a recursive refinement mechanism during inference, which repeatedly optimizes network predictions within each denoising step to enable precise trajectory injection. Flow-Gated Latent Fusion leverages optical flow similarity to decouple motion from appearance in the latent space and selectively inject trajectory guidance into motion-related channels. Dual-Path Self-Corrective Guidance compares guided and unguided denoising paths to adaptively correct trajectory drift caused by noisy or misaligned structural signals. Together, these components inject fine-grained, trajectory-aligned guidance without training, achieving both accurate motion control and photorealistic content generation. Extensive experiments across diverse benchmarks validate our method's superiority in realism, trajectory consistency, and visual fidelity. This work introduces a novel plug-and-play paradigm for controllable video synthesis, offering a new perspective on leveraging generative priors for spatial intelligence.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the WorldForge paper:

**Summary**

The paper presents WorldForge, a novel training-free framework that enhances 3D/4D generation capabilities of pre-trained video diffusion models (VDMs). The core problem addressed is the limited controllability and geometric inconsistency of VDMs, hindering their effective use in tasks requiring precise spatial reasoning. WorldForge injects fine-grained, trajectory-aligned guidance through three tightly integrated modules:

1.  **Intra-Step Recursive Refinement (IRR):**  A micro predict-correct loop within each denoising step, ensuring adherence to a user-defined trajectory.

2.  **Flow-Gated Latent Fusion (FLF):** Decouples motion and appearance features in the VAE latent space, selectively injecting trajectory guidance into motion-relevant channels.

3.  **Dual-Path Self-Corrective Guidance (DSG):** Uses the difference between guided and unguided denoising paths to correct trajectory drift caused by noise.

The approach leverages warping-and-repainting, using depth-based rendering to provide trajectory information.  The paper demonstrates superior performance on tasks like monocular 3D scene generation and dynamic 4D scene re-rendering compared to existing methods, achieving a better balance between realism, trajectory consistency, and visual fidelity, all without retraining the underlying VDM.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The core contribution lies in the synergistic combination of IRR, FLF, and DSG, which together create a powerful, training-free inference-time guidance mechanism. While each module might have inspirations from existing concepts (e.g., CFG for DSG), the integration and specific application to enhancing VDM controllability for 3D/4D tasks are novel. The FLF, by disentangling motion and appearance within the latent space, offers a particularly interesting contribution.
