It would be beneficial to include a discussion on the computational overhead.
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Justification:** While the empirical results are convincing, a deeper theoretical justification for why these specific SSL techniques are effective in addressing the identified properties would enhance the paper's contribution.

*   **Potential Impact:** If ST-AR can be successfully applied to other datasets and model architectures, it could become a valuable tool for improving the performance of autoregressive models for image generation and potentially other generative tasks. It offers a way to significantly enhance image understanding within the models, removing reliance on pre-trained models.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of autoregressive image generation. It clearly identifies limitations, proposes a novel and effective training framework, and provides comprehensive experimental results. While the paper could benefit from more extensive evaluation and a deeper theoretical analysis, the demonstrated performance improvements and the potential for impact justify a score of 7. The score is not higher because the individual components of ST-AR (MIM and contrastive learning) are not entirely novel, and the approach is, to some degree, an adaptation of existing SSL techniques to the AR setting. However, the specific application within AR models, the targeted approach towards identified limitations, and the empirical gains are significant.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Fast and Fluent Diffusion Language Models via Convolutional Decoding and Rejective Fine-tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15188v1)
- **Authors**: Yeongbin Seo, Dongha Lee, Jaehyung Kim, Jinyoung Yeo
- **Abstract**: Autoregressive (AR) language models generate text one token at a time, which limits their inference speed. Diffusion-based language models offer a promising alternative, as they can decode multiple tokens in parallel. However, we identify a key bottleneck in current diffusion LMs: the long decoding-window problem, where tokens generated far from the input context often become irrelevant or repetitive. Previous solutions like semi-autoregressive address this issue by splitting windows into blocks, but this sacrifices speed and bidirectionality, eliminating the main advantage of diffusion models. To overcome this, we propose Convolutional decoding (Conv), a normalization-based method that narrows the decoding window without hard segmentation, leading to better fluency and flexibility. Additionally, we introduce Rejecting Rule-based Fine-Tuning (R2FT), a post-hoc training scheme that better aligns tokens at positions far from context. Our methods achieve state-of-the-art results on open-ended generation benchmarks (e.g., AlpacaEval) among diffusion LM baselines, with significantly lower step size than previous works, demonstrating both speed and quality improvements.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper identifies a key challenge in diffusion language models (DLMs), termed the "long decoding-window problem" (LDW), where tokens generated far from the input context often become irrelevant or repetitive, hindering fluency.  The authors propose two solutions: 1) Convolutional decoding (Conv), a normalization-based method to narrow the decoding window without hard segmentation, enhancing fluency and flexibility, and 2) Rejecting Rule-based Fine-Tuning (R2FT), a post-hoc training scheme to better align tokens at distant positions from context. The authors demonstrate state-of-the-art performance among diffusion LM baselines on open-ended generation benchmarks (e.g., AlpacaEval) with significantly smaller step sizes than prior work, suggesting both speed and quality improvements.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The LDW problem is a valuable insight and represents a genuine issue hindering DLM performance, even if it is a problem already recognised in the community, just not so explicitly framed.  The Conv decoding method is reasonably novel, leveraging a normalization technique to narrow the decoding window, which is different than previous hard segmentation methods.  The R2FT approach, while drawing inspiration from DPO/SimPO, demonstrates a novel application in the context of diffusion LMs and addresses their unique challenges. However, the basic premise of R2FT does bear some resemblance to existing repetition penalties, although applied in a fine-tuning scenario. The combination of Conv and R2FT is definitely a novel contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical limitation in DLMs, increasing their potential for broader adoption. The improvements in both speed and quality are significant and demonstrate that DLMs can potentially overcome the limitations of autoregressive models. Showing that these DLMs can perform at SOTA levels with significantly smaller step sizes highlights the potential of this approach. The focus on fluent, open-ended text generation is valuable, as it showcases the practicality of DLMs for real-world applications beyond NLU tasks. The empirical results, especially on AlpacaEval with G-Eval, strengthen the paper's claims.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and insightful analysis of the LDW problem.
    *   Well-motivated solutions with Conv and R2FT.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation on open-ended generation benchmarks.
    *   Demonstration of speed and quality improvements compared to existing diffusion LMs.
    *   The code release enhances reproducibility and encourages further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Although the introduction argues for bidirectionality as a strength of DLMs, the empirical evaluation is mainly focused on unidirectional generation.
    *   The impact of R2FT might be somewhat limited by the simplicity of the rule-based corruption. A more sophisticated corruption strategy could potentially lead to further improvements.
    *   Limited ablation of individual components to fully understand their individual contributions beyond the main ablation on semi-AR vs Conv.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper could influence the field in several ways:
    *   Encouraging further research into addressing the LDW problem in DLMs.
    *   Motivating the development of more efficient and fluent DLMs.
    *   Providing a practical approach for improving the performance of DLMs on open-ended generation tasks.

*   **Justification for Score:**

The paper offers a valuable contribution to the field. It provides a clear analysis of a relevant problem, with well-motivated solutions that lead to empirical improvements on meaningful tasks. Although some elements of the proposed methods are inspired by existing techniques, their adaptation and combination for diffusion LMs represent a genuine advancement. While the work has weaknesses, its strengths are significant, and it has the potential to influence future research in this area. Given these factors, the paper warrants a solid score, though some refinements could elevate its score closer to the highest end.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Score**: 7/10

### Evolving Language Models without Labels: Majority Drives Selection, Novelty Promotes Variation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15194v1)
- **Authors**: Yujun Zhou, Zhenwen Liang, Haolin Liu, Wenhao Yu, Kishan Panaganti, Linfeng Song, Dian Yu, Xiangliang Zhang, Haitao Mi, Dong Yu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly trained with reinforcement learning from verifiable rewards (RLVR), yet real-world deployment demands models that can self-improve without labels or external judges. Existing label-free methods, confidence minimization, self-consistency, or majority-vote objectives, stabilize learning but steadily shrink exploration, causing an entropy collapse: generations become shorter, less diverse, and brittle. Unlike prior approaches such as Test-Time Reinforcement Learning (TTRL), which primarily adapt models to the immediate unlabeled dataset at hand, our goal is broader: to enable general improvements without sacrificing the model's inherent exploration capacity and generalization ability, i.e., evolving. We formalize this issue and propose EVolution-Oriented and Label-free Reinforcement Learning (EVOL-RL), a simple rule that couples stability with variation under a label-free setting. EVOL-RL keeps the majority-voted answer as a stable anchor (selection) while adding a novelty-aware reward that favors responses whose reasoning differs from what has already been produced (variation), measured in semantic space. Implemented with GRPO, EVOL-RL also uses asymmetric clipping to preserve strong signals and an entropy regularizer to sustain search. This majority-for-selection + novelty-for-variation design prevents collapse, maintains longer and more informative chains of thought, and improves both pass@1 and pass@n. EVOL-RL consistently outperforms the majority-only TTRL baseline; e.g., training on label-free AIME24 lifts Qwen3-4B-Base AIME25 pass@1 from TTRL's 4.6% to 16.4%, and pass@16 from 18.5% to 37.9%. EVOL-RL not only prevents diversity collapse but also unlocks stronger generalization across domains (e.g., GPQA). Furthermore, we demonstrate that EVOL-RL also boosts performance in the RLVR setting, highlighting its broad applicability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Evolving Language Models without Labels: Majority Drives Selection, Novelty Promotes Variation":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of entropy collapse in label-free reinforcement learning (RL) for large language models (LLMs). When LLMs are trained to improve themselves based on objectives like self-consistency or majority voting, they tend to narrow their exploration, leading to shorter, less diverse, and more brittle generations.  The authors propose EVOL-RL, a novel rule that combines stability with variation under a label-free setting. EVOL-RL uses the majority-voted answer as a stable anchor (selection) and adds a novelty-aware reward that favors responses with different reasoning (variation), measured in semantic space. They implement EVOL-RL using GRPO, with asymmetric clipping and entropy regularization to prevent premature convergence. Experiments show that EVOL-RL avoids diversity collapse, maintains longer, more informative reasoning chains, and improves performance on mathematical reasoning benchmarks, demonstrating strong cross-task generalization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of combining majority-vote stability with novelty-driven variation is genuinely novel in the context of label-free LLM self-improvement. Prior work in TTRL relies mostly on majority voting, which, as the authors point out, causes entropy collapse.  While evolutionary algorithms have long used variation/selection concepts, the specific application and implementation within LLM label-free RL is new. The specific novelty reward function—penalizing conformity to the group average and duplication of other responses—is a reasonable and well-motivated design. The use of asymmetric clipping to preserve gradient signals from novel solutions is a worthwhile contribution.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a practical problem in LLM self-improvement: ensuring exploration and generalization when labels are unavailable. Preventing entropy collapse is crucial for building truly autonomous and adaptable AI systems. The empirical results, especially the significant gains on AIME25 and GPQA, demonstrate the potential of EVOL-RL to improve LLM reasoning capabilities beyond what's achievable with majority-voting alone. The fact that it improves both in-domain and OOD performance highlights that the method is learning more robust and generalizable reasoning strategies.  The application of EVOL-RL to supervised GRPO (RLVR) to improve results is a plus.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and well-motivated approach.
    *   Solid theoretical rationale for combining majority vote and novelty.
    *   Comprehensive experimental validation across multiple datasets and model scales.
    *   Detailed ablation study demonstrating the importance of each component of EVOL-RL.
    *   Analysis of the training dynamics provides insights into how EVOL-RL escapes entropy collapse.
    *   The paper shows it is possible to improve on RLVR techniques by including EVOL-RL

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper explains the design of the novelty reward, further justification for the specific values of a and normalization (min-max) would strengthen the explanation.
    *   The novelty computation relies on semantic similarity. Performance depends on the quality of the embedding model.
    *   More detailed discussion of the computational overhead involved in calculating novelty scores would be beneficial. Also, there is little discussion around sample complexity. More samples are needed to calculate novelty compared to TTRL.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in LLM self-improvement, continual learning, and reinforcement learning without human labels. It provides a practical solution to a critical challenge and demonstrates the value of combining stability and variation in these settings. Researchers could build upon EVOL-RL by exploring different novelty metrics, adaptive mechanisms for balancing exploration and exploitation, or applications to other LLM tasks beyond mathematical reasoning.

**Overall Assessment:** The paper makes a substantial contribution to the field by identifying and addressing the problem of entropy collapse in label-free LLM reinforcement learning. The proposed EVOL-RL method is novel, well-motivated, and empirically validated. While there are some limitations in the form of better justification for parameters or limitations in sample complexity, the strengths outweigh these weaknesses. The clear writing style and thorough experimental evaluation make this paper a valuable contribution to the field.
Score: 8
Rigorous Rationale:
A score of 8 reflects that this is a very strong paper with novel ideas but a few minor shortcomings as outlined above.  The empirical gains are impressive, the approach is well motivated, and the analysis is thorough. The work presents a significant step forward in label-free LLM self-improvement, and has the potential to influence the direction of research. However, the limitations mentioned above prevent it from achieving a higher score. An 8 represents that this paper is excellent and has a high likelihood of influencing future work in the area of label-free LM training.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Beyond Surface Alignment: Rebuilding LLMs Safety Mechanism via Probabilistically Ablating Refusal Direction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15202v1)
- **Authors**: Yuanbo Xie, Yingjie Zhang, Tianyun Liu, Duohe Ma, Tingwen Liu
- **Abstract**: Jailbreak attacks pose persistent threats to large language models (LLMs). Current safety alignment methods have attempted to address these issues, but they experience two significant limitations: insufficient safety alignment depth and unrobust internal defense mechanisms. These limitations make them vulnerable to adversarial attacks such as prefilling and refusal direction manipulation. We introduce DeepRefusal, a robust safety alignment framework that overcomes these issues. DeepRefusal forces the model to dynamically rebuild its refusal mechanisms from jailbreak states. This is achieved by probabilistically ablating the refusal direction across layers and token depths during fine-tuning. Our method not only defends against prefilling and refusal direction attacks but also demonstrates strong resilience against other unseen jailbreak strategies. Extensive evaluations on four open-source LLM families and six representative attacks show that DeepRefusal reduces attack success rates by approximately 95%, while maintaining model capabilities with minimal performance degradation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "DeepRefusal," a novel safety alignment framework for Large Language Models (LLMs) designed to enhance robustness against jailbreak attacks.  Current safety alignment methods often focus on surface-level features, making them vulnerable to attacks that manipulate initial tokens or exploit internal defense weaknesses. DeepRefusal addresses these limitations by forcing the model to rebuild its refusal mechanisms from within by probabilistically ablating the refusal direction during fine-tuning across various layers and token depths. This adversarial training process aims to simulate jailbreak scenarios internally and strengthen the model's internal safety mechanisms. Experimental results across four open-source LLM families and six representative attacks demonstrate significant reductions in attack success rates (approximately 95%) while maintaining model capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its approach to safety alignment via probabilistically ablating the refusal direction at multiple layers and token depths. While adversarial training and internal representation manipulation are not entirely new concepts in LLM safety, the specific method of forcing the model to rebuild its refusal behavior under simulated jailbreak conditions is a unique contribution. It shifts the focus from superficial prompt manipulation to a deeper, more resilient internal defense.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from the demonstrated vulnerability of existing LLM safety mechanisms to jailbreak attacks, especially those exploiting the "alignment depth problem" and internal defense weaknesses. The DeepRefusal framework provides a practical and effective method for addressing these issues, showing substantial improvement in robustness against various attack types. The results suggest that future safety alignment strategies must consider deeper, more integrated approaches to counter sophisticated adversarial attacks.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The identification and articulation of the "alignment depth problem" and the vulnerability of existing internal defense mechanisms are clear and insightful.
    *   The DeepRefusal framework is well-motivated and effectively implemented.  The description of the method and its components (layer-wise and token-wise PAA) are thorough.
    *   The experimental evaluation is comprehensive, covering multiple LLM families, a diverse set of attacks, and comparisons with existing defense baselines. The results consistently demonstrate the effectiveness of DeepRefusal.
    *   The ablation study provides valuable insights into the importance of different components of the framework.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The method relies on the concept of a "refusal direction," which, while useful, might not be universally applicable to all LLM architectures or future safety mechanisms. The paper acknowledges this limitation and the need for further exploration in multimodal contexts.
    *   The computational cost of identifying the optimal refusal direction and conducting fine-tuning with PAA is not explicitly addressed. While the paper states that fine-tuning takes approximately 45 minutes on an A100, more details on the resource requirements and scalability would be beneficial.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of why DeepRefusal is more effective than existing methods. While the results clearly show improved robustness, a deeper dive into the underlying mechanisms and interactions with the model's internal representations would strengthen the contribution.
    *   There is limited discussion on what specific aspects of the model are altered by DeepRefusal. The effectiveness of the ablation approach implies that the model is sensitive to these interventions, but the precise effects are unclear.
    *   The ethics discussion is relatively minimal. A more detailed exploration of potential unintended consequences, such as the model generating misleading content or refusing safe requests, is warranted.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper could influence the direction of future research in LLM safety alignment, pushing the field toward deeper, more integrated defense mechanisms.
    *   The DeepRefusal framework could be adopted and further developed by other researchers and practitioners, leading to more robust and reliable LLMs.
    *   The insights from the paper could inform the design of future LLM architectures and training procedures to enhance inherent safety.

*   **Score Justification:**

    The paper presents a novel and effective approach to LLM safety alignment, addressing significant limitations of existing methods. The thorough experimental evaluation supports the effectiveness of DeepRefusal, and the ablation study provides valuable insights. While the method has some limitations and areas for further investigation, its contribution to the field is substantial.
    Based on these considerations, I assign a score of:
    Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Fair-GPTQ: Bias-Aware Quantization for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15206v1)
- **Authors**: Irina Proskurina, Guillaume Metzler, Julien Velcin
- **Abstract**: High memory demands of generative language models have drawn attention to quantization, which reduces computational cost, memory usage, and latency by mapping model weights to lower-precision integers. Approaches such as GPTQ effectively minimize input-weight product errors during quantization; however, recent empirical studies show that they can increase biased outputs and degrade performance on fairness benchmarks, and it remains unclear which specific weights cause this issue. In this work, we draw new links between quantization and model fairness by adding explicit group-fairness constraints to the quantization objective and introduce Fair-GPTQ, the first quantization method explicitly designed to reduce unfairness in large language models. The added constraints guide the learning of the rounding operation toward less-biased text generation for protected groups. Specifically, we focus on stereotype generation involving occupational bias and discriminatory language spanning gender, race, and religion. Fair-GPTQ has minimal impact on performance, preserving at least 90% of baseline accuracy on zero-shot benchmarks, reduces unfairness relative to a half-precision model, and retains the memory and speed benefits of 4-bit quantization. We also compare the performance of Fair-GPTQ with existing debiasing methods and find that it achieves performance on par with the iterative null-space projection debiasing approach on racial-stereotype benchmarks. Overall, the results validate our theoretical solution to the quantization problem with a group-bias term, highlight its applicability for reducing group bias at quantization time in generative models, and demonstrate that our approach can further be used to analyze channel- and weight-level contributions to fairness during quantization.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Fair-GPTQ: Bias-Aware Quantization for Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of increased bias in large language models (LLMs) after quantization.  While quantization methods like GPTQ are effective in reducing memory and computational costs, they can exacerbate existing biases. To mitigate this, the authors introduce Fair-GPTQ, a novel quantization method that incorporates group-fairness constraints into the quantization objective. This is achieved by adding a term that minimizes the difference in model behavior between paired stereotypical and anti-stereotypical inputs. The method is evaluated on stereotype generation tasks spanning gender, race, and religion, showing reduced unfairness while preserving performance and the benefits of 4-bit quantization. The authors also analyze the contribution of different matrix types and layers to fairness during quantization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the explicit inclusion of group-fairness constraints within the GPTQ quantization process.  Prior work had observed bias increases *after* quantization but hadn't directly addressed fairness during the quantization *optimization* itself.  The paper makes a compelling connection between the input-weight product error minimization in GPTQ and the potential for bias amplification, offering a new perspective on the interaction between compression and fairness. The specific adaptation of the OBS framework to account for group bias during quantization is a solid methodological contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper has significant practical relevance. As LLMs become increasingly pervasive, addressing fairness is crucial. The proposed Fair-GPTQ offers a method to deploy compressed LLMs with reduced group bias, making it valuable for real-world applications where fairness is a concern. Quantization is essential for efficient deployment, especially on resource-constrained devices; therefore, a bias-aware approach to quantization is a welcome development.  The analysis of weight and layer contributions is also useful for future research in understanding and mitigating bias in LLMs.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of bias amplification during quantization.
    *   **Sound Methodology:**  The Fair-GPTQ method is well-motivated and mathematically sound. The adaptation of the OBS framework is clearly explained.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The evaluation includes various fairness benchmarks (CrowS-Pairs, StereoSet, BBQ, SoFA) and performance metrics (perplexity, zero-shot accuracy), providing a holistic assessment.
    *   **Insightful Analysis:** The analysis of matrix-type and layer depth contributions provides valuable insights into the sources of bias.
    *   **Code Availability**: While the code will be released upon acceptance, it's not currently available for full replication by the wider research community.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Calibration Data:**  The calibration data relies on relatively short paired sentences. While this is understandable for creating stereotype/anti-stereotype pairs, it may not fully capture the nuances of bias in longer text generation. Furthermore the code is not yet available, limiting reproducibility.
    *   **Model Family Restriction:** The experiments primarily focus on OPT and Mistral models. While these are representative, demonstrating the effectiveness of Fair-GPTQ on a broader range of architectures would strengthen the claims.
    *   **Performance Trade-off:** Some results show a slight degradation in zero-shot performance compared to the baseline GPTQ method. While the improvement in fairness arguably justifies this, it's important to acknowledge the trade-off.
    *   **Hyperparameter sensitivity:** The paper indicates experimentation with different alpha values but doesn't fully explore the sensitivity of the results to changes in alpha or provide a clear strategy for selecting the optimal alpha for a given model and task.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper is likely to influence future research in bias mitigation and quantization. It provides a solid foundation for developing more sophisticated bias-aware quantization methods and for exploring the interaction between compression and fairness in LLMs. The weight analysis can inspire future works on identifying influential layers and weights for group bias and could be applied to training strategies.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents a novel and practically relevant approach to mitigating bias during quantization of LLMs.  The methodology is sound, and the evaluation is comprehensive. However, the limited scope of calibration data, slight performance trade-off, and restricted evaluation to OPT and Mistral families slightly reduce the overall impact.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Geometric Image Synchronization with Deep Watermarking
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15208v1)
- **Authors**: Pierre Fernandez, Tomáš Souček, Nikola Jovanović, Hady Elsahar, Sylvestre-Alvise Rebuffi, Valeriu Lacatusu, Tuan Tran, Alexandre Mourachko
- **Abstract**: Synchronization is the task of estimating and inverting geometric transformations (e.g., crop, rotation) applied to an image. This work introduces SyncSeal, a bespoke watermarking method for robust image synchronization, which can be applied on top of existing watermarking methods to enhance their robustness against geometric transformations. It relies on an embedder network that imperceptibly alters images and an extractor network that predicts the geometric transformation to which the image was subjected. Both networks are end-to-end trained to minimize the error between the predicted and ground-truth parameters of the transformation, combined with a discriminator to maintain high perceptual quality. We experimentally validate our method on a wide variety of geometric and valuemetric transformations, demonstrating its effectiveness in accurately synchronizing images. We further show that our synchronization can effectively upgrade existing watermarking methods to withstand geometric transformations to which they were previously vulnerable.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "SyncSeal," a deep learning-based watermarking method for robust image synchronization. SyncSeal aims to improve the resilience of existing watermarking techniques against geometric transformations (e.g., rotation, cropping). It uses an embedder network to imperceptibly embed a synchronization watermark into an image, and an extractor network to predict the parameters of geometric transformations that might have been applied. Both networks are trained end-to-end, with a loss function that encourages accurate synchronization and high perceptual quality. The authors validate SyncSeal on various geometric and valuemetric transformations, showing it effectively synchronizes images and enhances the robustness of existing watermarking schemes against geometric distortions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using deep learning to predict and invert geometric transformations for watermarking isn't entirely novel. Previous works have explored similar approaches with feature matching or dedicated synchronization patterns.  However, this paper presents a refined approach by directly predicting the corner coordinates of the transformed image and optimizing end-to-end. The incorporation of a discriminator network to maintain image quality during embedding is a standard practice in GAN-based approaches but crucial for the success of this method. Compared to previous approaches, the improvements provided in the breadth of the types of transformations robustly handled represents a real step forward.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant challenge in watermarking: robustness against geometric transformations.  While many modern watermarking techniques attempt to achieve this through data augmentation, their effectiveness can be limited. SyncSeal provides a complementary approach that can be applied on top of existing methods, significantly boosting their robustness. Demonstrating this enhancement on a variety of existing watermarking schemes is a key strength. The impact is likely to be incremental rather than revolutionary, as it doesn't introduce a fundamentally new watermarking paradigm but improves existing ones.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   End-to-end training allows for joint optimization of embedding and extraction.
    *   The evaluation is thorough, considering a wide range of geometric and valuemetric transformations.
    *   The paper demonstrates that SyncSeal can be used to upgrade existing watermarking techniques.
    *   The method operates well by directly predicting the geometric mapping.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The method cannot predict scale changes. This limitation is explicitly acknowledged but restricts its applicability in scenarios where image resizing is a concern.
    *   While comparisons are made to WMAR and SIFT, the comparison to SIFT is not perfectly fair, as SIFT is a non-blind approach.
    *   There is a risk that compounding errors can arise from synchronization and watermark extraction steps.
    *   The introduction of SyncSeal inevitably increases the computational complexity.

*   **Impact:** SyncSeal has the potential to become a valuable tool for improving the robustness of watermarking schemes, particularly in applications where geometric distortions are likely. By combining SyncSeal with existing methods, practitioners can achieve greater reliability and security. The impact will likely be concentrated in practical applications rather than theoretical breakthroughs in the field. The thorough analysis and open-source code could promote its widespread adoption.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper addresses a relevant problem and presents a robust solution. It demonstrates its practical value through comprehensive experiments and integration with existing watermarking methods. However, the core idea isn't entirely novel, and it has some limitations. The improvement provided with this approach makes it a relevant technique that can advance the applicability of modern watermarking methods.

- **Score**: 7/10

### Evil Vizier: Vulnerabilities of LLM-Integrated XR Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15213v1)
- **Authors**: Yicheng Zhang, Zijian Huang, Sophie Chen, Erfan Shayegani, Jiasi Chen, Nael Abu-Ghazaleh
- **Abstract**: Extended reality (XR) applications increasingly integrate Large Language Models (LLMs) to enhance user experience, scene understanding, and even generate executable XR content, and are often called "AI glasses". Despite these potential benefits, the integrated XR-LLM pipeline makes XR applications vulnerable to new forms of attacks. In this paper, we analyze LLM-Integated XR systems in the literature and in practice and categorize them along different dimensions from a systems perspective. Building on this categorization, we identify a common threat model and demonstrate a series of proof-of-concept attacks on multiple XR platforms that employ various LLM models (Meta Quest 3, Meta Ray-Ban, Android, and Microsoft HoloLens 2 running Llama and GPT models). Although these platforms each implement LLM integration differently, they share vulnerabilities where an attacker can modify the public context surrounding a legitimate LLM query, resulting in erroneous visual or auditory feedback to users, thus compromising their safety or privacy, sowing confusion, or other harmful effects. To defend against these threats, we discuss mitigation strategies and best practices for developers, including an initial defense prototype, and call on the community to develop new protection mechanisms to mitigate these risks.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Evil Vizier: Vulnerabilities of LLM-Integrated XR Systems" investigates the security risks introduced when Large Language Models (LLMs) are integrated into Extended Reality (XR) systems (e.g., AR glasses, VR headsets). The authors argue that while LLM integration enhances XR applications, it also creates new attack surfaces. They categorize LLM-integrated XR systems based on their architecture, inputs, outputs, and triggering mechanisms. They propose a common threat model where an attacker manipulates public context accessible to the LLM, causing it to generate harmful responses. They demonstrate proof-of-concept attacks on several commercial and open-source XR platforms, showing how attackers can cause confusion, safety risks, data exfiltration, and other harmful effects. Finally, they discuss mitigation strategies and provide initial defense prototypes, calling for further research in this area.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in specifically examining the security implications of LLM *integration* within *XR* systems. While LLM security and XR security are individually researched areas, their combination creates unique vulnerabilities that this paper begins to explore. Prior work has mainly focused on individual security concerns within these domains, rather than the interplay between the two. Specifically, examining client-side attacks and a common threat model is novel.
*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because it highlights a growing security concern as LLM-integrated XR systems become more prevalent. XR systems interact directly with users' perceptions and actions in the real world, making security breaches potentially dangerous. The proof-of-concept attacks demonstrate real vulnerabilities in existing systems, showing that the issue is not merely theoretical. The exploration of outcomes such as confusion, safety compromises, data exfiltration, and escalation of scope are all relevant and concerning.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Systematic Categorization:** The paper provides a structured way to understand the different types of LLM-integrated XR systems, which helps in identifying potential attack vectors.
    *   **Practical Demonstrations:** The proof-of-concept attacks are well-explained and demonstrate the feasibility of the proposed threat model on diverse XR platforms (Meta Quest, Ray-Ban, HoloLens, etc.).
    *   **Unified Threat Model:** The paper proposes a realistic threat model.
    *   **Mitigation Strategies:**  The discussion of mitigation strategies and the development of a prototype defense provide a starting point for future research.
    *   **Timeliness:** The paper addresses a relevant and emerging area of research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The attacks focus primarily on manipulating *public* context or prompts. More sophisticated attacks that exploit deeper vulnerabilities in LLM architecture, model biases, or communication protocols within the XR system are not explored in detail. The paper only consider client-side attacks and does not require access to remote servers.
    *   **Generality of Defenses:** The proposed mitigation strategies are relatively high-level and may not be sufficient to address all potential attacks. The limited success rate of the LLM detection for malicious code generation highlights this.
    *   **Lack of Extensive Empirical Evaluation of the Defense Prototype:** A more thorough evaluation of the initial defense prototype in a variety of attack scenarios is needed to determine its effectiveness.
    *   **Focus on a specific attack surface**: It only focuses on publicly available methods and APIs within XR game engines.
    *   **Limited generality of attacks**: The attacks heavily rely on the capabilities and limitations of specific LLMs and XR frameworks, potentially limiting their generalizability.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper is likely to have a positive influence on the field by raising awareness of the security risks associated with LLM-integrated XR systems. It can serve as a foundation for future research on developing more robust security mechanisms and best practices for developers. It should encourage closer collaboration between the XR and LLM security communities.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the nascent field of LLM-integrated XR security. It's well-structured, presents a clear threat model, and provides practical demonstrations of real vulnerabilities. However, the limitations regarding the scope of attacks, the generality of defenses, and the depth of evaluation prevent it from achieving a higher score.  The paper's strengths lie in bringing the issue to the forefront and providing a starting point for further investigation.

**Score: 7**

- **Score**: 7/10

### Assessing Historical Structural Oppression Worldwide via Rule-Guided Prompting of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15216v1)
- **Authors**: Sreejato Chatterjee, Linh Tran, Quoc Duy Nguyen, Roni Kirson, Drue Hamlin, Harvest Aquino, Hanjia Lyu, Jiebo Luo, Timothy Dye
- **Abstract**: Traditional efforts to measure historical structural oppression struggle with cross-national validity due to the unique, locally specified histories of exclusion, colonization, and social status in each country, and often have relied on structured indices that privilege material resources while overlooking lived, identity-based exclusion. We introduce a novel framework for oppression measurement that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate context-sensitive scores of lived historical disadvantage across diverse geopolitical settings. Using unstructured self-identified ethnicity utterances from a multilingual COVID-19 global study, we design rule-guided prompting strategies that encourage models to produce interpretable, theoretically grounded estimations of oppression. We systematically evaluate these strategies across multiple state-of-the-art LLMs. Our results demonstrate that LLMs, when guided by explicit rules, can capture nuanced forms of identity-based historical oppression within nations. This approach provides a complementary measurement tool that highlights dimensions of systemic exclusion, offering a scalable, cross-cultural lens for understanding how oppression manifests in data-driven research and public health contexts. To support reproducible evaluation, we release an open-sourced benchmark dataset for assessing LLMs on oppression measurement (https://github.com/chattergpt/llm-oppression-benchmark).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a framework for assessing historical structural oppression across different countries using large language models (LLMs). It addresses the limitations of traditional structured indices that often fail to capture nuanced, localized histories of exclusion and rely heavily on material deprivation metrics. The authors use free-text responses from a multilingual COVID-19 study regarding self-identified ethnicity and country of residence.  They develop rule-guided prompting strategies for LLMs, aiming to generate context-sensitive scores of historical disadvantage while grounding the models in sociological theory. The authors evaluate these strategies across several state-of-the-art LLMs and release a dataset and benchmark for oppression measurement.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a Critical Gap:** The paper tackles an important and complex problem – how to measure and compare historical structural oppression across different national contexts. Traditional methods often lack the nuance needed to capture the localized nature of oppression.
*   **Novel Methodology:** The use of LLMs for this purpose is innovative. By prompting models to reason about local histories and systemic exclusion, the authors attempt to move beyond simplistic demographic indicators. The rule-guided prompting is a key strength, as it helps to mitigate biases and steer the models towards theoretically grounded estimations.
*   **Multilingual and Global Scope:** The use of multilingual data and a focus on diverse geopolitical settings enhances the generalizability and relevance of the framework.
*   **Rigorous Evaluation:** The authors systematically evaluate their prompting strategies across multiple LLMs and compare their outputs with human expert annotations. The use of various metrics (MAE, accuracy, Kappa, Pearson and Spearman correlation) provides a comprehensive assessment. The error analysis gives valuable insights into the LLM's performance on the task.
*   **Dataset and Benchmark:** The release of a dataset and benchmark is a significant contribution, enabling further research and development in this area.
*   **Strong Theoretical Foundation:** The method is grounded in relevant sociological and public health theories, ensuring that the framework is not just a technical exercise but is also theoretically sound.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Oversimplification:**  The transformation of multidimensional oppression into a five-level ordinal scale, while necessary for quantitative analysis, inevitably abstracts away from the complex reality of lived experiences. This raises questions about the validity and granularity of the resulting scores. The 5-level scale, even with rule-guided reasoning, can still be rather broad for certain nuanced cases.
*   **LLM Biases:** Despite the rule-guided prompts, LLMs are known to exhibit biases based on their training data. The paper acknowledges this but the reliance on these models still introduces the potential for perpetuating or amplifying existing societal biases, especially when the histories are underrepresented in the training data. The models still struggle to classify some cases properly and overgeneralize the context of ambiguous cases.
*   **Limited Dataset Size and Scope:** While diverse, the dataset used for annotation and evaluation is relatively small (334 responses across 10 countries) and may not fully represent the complexity of global identities and contexts. Expansion of the training and evaluation sets would further contribute to the reliability of the system.
*   **Annotation Subjectivity:** While the annotation process is designed to be rigorous, human expert annotations are still subjective and can be influenced by the annotators' own biases and perspectives. Inter-annotator agreement should also be discussed.
*   **Western Bias:**  Even with efforts to be global, the LLM training data likely has a Western bias, potentially explaining the better performance in countries like Brazil, Canada, and the US. The paper acknowledges this.
*   **Limited Exploration of Alternative LLM Techniques:** The paper focuses primarily on rule-guided prompting, with limited exploration of other potentially beneficial LLM techniques (e.g., few-shot learning with carefully curated examples, fine-tuning LLMs on the annotation data, using retrieval augmented models).

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper is novel in its application of LLMs to the task of assessing historical structural oppression. While LLMs have been used in social sciences, their application to this specific problem, combined with the rule-guided prompting and focus on global, identity-based oppression, is a unique contribution.  It provides a valuable complement to traditional methods and offers a scalable, cross-cultural lens for understanding how oppression manifests. The release of the dataset and benchmark can spur further research and development in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

Despite the inherent limitations of using LLMs and the challenges of measuring such a complex concept, the paper offers a significant step forward in developing more nuanced and scalable methods for assessing historical structural oppression.  The rule-guided prompting is a key innovation, and the rigorous evaluation provides strong evidence of the framework's potential. The work directly addresses a gap in existing measurement methods, and demonstrates the efficacy of the use of LLMs with the right prompt engineering in the area of social science, particularly when the concept to be measured is not straightforward.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Generalizable Geometric Image Caption Synthesis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15217v1)
- **Authors**: Yue Xin, Wenyuan Wang, Rui Pan, Ruida Wang, Howard Meng, Renjie Pi, Shizhe Diao, Tong Zhang
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models have various practical applications that demand strong reasoning abilities. Despite recent advancements, these models still struggle to solve complex geometric problems. A key challenge stems from the lack of high-quality image-text pair datasets for understanding geometric images. Furthermore, most template-based data synthesis pipelines typically fail to generalize to questions beyond their predefined templates. In this paper, we bridge this gap by introducing a complementary process of Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) into the data generation pipeline. By adopting RLVR to refine captions for geometric images synthesized from 50 basic geometric relations and using reward signals derived from mathematical problem-solving tasks, our pipeline successfully captures the key features of geometry problem-solving. This enables better task generalization and yields non-trivial improvements. Furthermore, even in out-of-distribution scenarios, the generated dataset enhances the general reasoning capabilities of multimodal large language models, yielding accuracy improvements of $2.8\%\text{-}4.8\%$ in statistics, arithmetic, algebraic, and numerical tasks with non-geometric input images of MathVista and MathVerse, along with $2.4\%\text{-}3.9\%$ improvements in Art, Design, Tech, and Engineering tasks in MMMU.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the lack of high-quality, cross-modal (image-text) datasets for geometric reasoning, a weakness in existing multimodal large language models (MLLMs).  It introduces GeoReasoning-10K, a new dataset of 10,000 geometric image-caption pairs, generated and refined using a novel Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) framework.  The RLVR process is designed to ensure better alignment between visual and textual information by using mathematical problem-solving tasks to derive reward signals for caption refinement. Experiments show that MLLMs trained on GeoReasoning-10K exhibit improved performance on geometric image textualization tasks and enhanced generalization capabilities across other mathematical and even non-mathematical domains (MathVerse, MathVista, MMMU). The paper introduces a rule-based geometric data synthesis pipeline, complemented by an RL-based refinement engine, to produce high-quality data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The combination of rule-based geometric data synthesis with RLVR is a significant strength and a clear novelty. While rule-based approaches are not entirely new in this area, the iterative refinement through RL, guided by verifiable mathematical rewards, is a valuable contribution.  The emphasis on creating *fully aligned* visual and textual data, addressing a critical limitation of prior datasets, adds to the novelty.

**Significance:** The significance stems from the demonstrated improvement in MLLM performance on tasks that demand strong cross-modal reasoning. The ability to generalize to out-of-distribution mathematical problems and even to non-mathematical areas such as art, design, and engineering further underscores the value of the approach. The potential to improve mathematical reasoning is an area of active research with impact on a wide range of applications.

**Strengths:**

*   **Addressing a Real Problem:** The paper tackles a genuine weakness in current MLLMs: the inability to effectively reason about geometric images due to a lack of suitable training data.
*   **Innovative Approach:** The RLVR framework offers a promising method for generating and refining high-quality, cross-modal geometric datasets.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of GeoReasoning-10K and the RLVR framework, both in-domain and out-of-domain.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a thorough evaluation across several benchmarks, including MathVista, MathVerse, and MMMU, and includes ablation studies.
*   **Clear and Well-Written:** The paper is well-organized and clearly explains the methodology and experimental results.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** While the results show impressive generalization, the core RLVR process is focused on geometric problems.  The broader applicability of this specific framework to *other* types of visual reasoning tasks might be limited.
*   **Complexity:** The RLVR approach adds complexity to the data generation pipeline, requiring careful design of reward functions and RL training procedures. The reliance on a pre-trained LLM (Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct) is a possible concern as it anchors the method to the capabilities of this LLM.
*   **Dependency on External Tools**: The evaluation depends on the  GPT-4o-mini for answer checking, which brings a cost and a potential reliance on proprietary technology.

**Justification for the Score:**

This paper makes a solid contribution to the field. It tackles a key challenge, presents a novel and well-executed approach, and provides substantial experimental evidence to support its claims. While the scope is limited to geometric problems, the insights gained and the demonstrated improvements have the potential to influence future research in cross-modal reasoning and dataset generation.

The innovative RLVR data refinement strategy and the clear demonstration of improved general reasoning, even on non-geometric tasks, justifies a high score. The weaknesses regarding the complexity of the RLVR approach and dependency on external LLM keep it from receiving a perfect score. A score of 8 reflects the paper's significant contribution to the field while acknowledging the limitations.

### LNE-Blocking: An Efficient Framework for Contamination Mitigation Evaluation on Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15218v1)
- **Authors**: Ruijie Hou, Yueyang Jiao, Hanxu Hu, Yingming Li, Wai Lam, Huajian Zhang, Hongyuan Lu
- **Abstract**: The problem of data contamination is now almost inevitable during the development of large language models (LLMs), with the training data commonly integrating those evaluation benchmarks even unintentionally. This problem subsequently makes it hard to benchmark LLMs fairly. Instead of constructing contamination-free datasets (quite hard), we propose a novel framework, \textbf{LNE-Blocking}, to restore model performance prior to contamination on potentially leaked datasets. Our framework consists of two components: contamination detection and disruption operation. For the prompt, the framework first uses the contamination detection method, \textbf{LNE}, to assess the extent of contamination in the model. Based on this, it adjusts the intensity of the disruption operation, \textbf{Blocking}, to elicit non-memorized responses from the model. Our framework is the first to efficiently restore the model's greedy decoding performance. This comes with a strong performance on multiple datasets with potential leakage risks, and it consistently achieves stable recovery results across different models and varying levels of data contamination. We release the code at https://github.com/RuijieH/LNE-Blocking to facilitate research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "LNE-Blocking: An Efficient Framework for Contamination Mitigation Evaluation on Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel framework, LNE-Blocking, for evaluating the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) in the presence of data contamination (leakage of evaluation benchmarks into the training data). The framework comprises two key components: contamination detection using Length-Normalized Entropy (LNE) and disruption operation called Blocking (suppressing high-probability tokens during decoding). LNE detects the degree of contamination in a model's output, and this information is then used to adaptively control the intensity of the Blocking operation. The goal is to restore the model's performance to a state prior to contamination, thus allowing for a fairer evaluation. The authors conduct experiments on code generation and arithmetic reasoning tasks, demonstrating the effectiveness and robustness of their approach across various models and contamination levels.  They compare their method against TED, a prior contamination mitigation evaluation method.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's main contribution lies in the LNE-Blocking framework itself, a new approach to contamination mitigation evaluation. The idea of combining contamination detection (LNE) with a controlled disruption operation (Blocking) during inference is a novel combination. While previous methods have focused on creating contamination-free datasets or relying on extensive sampling, LNE-Blocking offers a more efficient and targeted approach to estimate the genuine performance of contaminated models under greedy decoding. The use of length normalized entropy to estimate the level of data leakage in the model can be seen as novel.
*   **Significance:** Data contamination is a growing problem in the field of LLMs, making it difficult to accurately benchmark and compare models. The LNE-Blocking framework directly addresses this challenge by providing a way to evaluate models on potentially contaminated datasets and restore model performance. While the paper's scope is primarily evaluation (as opposed to mitigating the root cause of contamination), it contributes to fairer and more reliable benchmarking of LLMs.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Efficient and Targeted:**  LNE-Blocking doesn't rely on computationally expensive or time consuming sampling. The adaptive nature of the Blocking operation, guided by LNE, makes it more efficient compared to methods that uniformly disrupt generation.
    *   **Robustness:** Experiments demonstrate the framework's stability across different models and contamination levels, suggesting a degree of generalizability.
    *   **Practicality:**  The framework can be applied online during the generation phase and can directly be implemented into evaluation pipelines.
    *   **Comparison to Baseline:** The paper includes a useful comparison to TED which strengthens the arguments for efficiency and provides empirical evidence of relative performance on the contamination mitigation evaluation task.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Hyperparameter Tuning:** Threshold_Task is task specific and requires manual tuning; however, the paper provides some limited guidance on how to set this parameter. While claimed to be independent of the model, the task-specific hyperparameter may limit the framework's "out-of-the-box" usability.
    *   **Limited Scope:** While the method restores performance to a certain extent, it might not fully capture the underlying generalization ability of the model before contamination.
    *   **Coherence and Generation Quality:** While coherence metrics are provided to support its use, the disruption strategy could negatively impact coherence or fluency. The paper should explore more advanced metrics to assess potential side effects.
    *   **Simulation of Contamination:** The approach to simulating contamination via continued pretraining (LORA) or using test data is a simplification of real-world data leakage scenarios. A more diverse and realistic simulation would further strengthen the robustness claims.

*   **Potential Influence:** The LNE-Blocking framework has the potential to become a standard technique for evaluating LLMs in the presence of data contamination, especially when creating or curating contamination-free datasets is impractical. It might also inspire further research into more sophisticated methods for contamination detection and mitigation.

**Justification for Score:**

The LNE-Blocking framework addresses an important and timely problem in LLM research. The proposed approach is novel, efficient, and shows promise in restoring model performance in the face of data contamination. While there are some limitations regarding hyperparameter tuning and the simulation of contamination, the framework's strengths outweigh these weaknesses. The method's robustness, practicality, and ability to facilitate fairer evaluations make it a significant contribution to the field. Therefore,

### Lightweight and Accurate Multi-View Stereo with Confidence-Aware Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.15220v1)
- **Authors**: Fangjinhua Wang, Qingshan Xu, Yew-Soon Ong, Marc Pollefeys
- **Abstract**: To reconstruct the 3D geometry from calibrated images, learning-based multi-view stereo (MVS) methods typically perform multi-view depth estimation and then fuse depth maps into a mesh or point cloud. To improve the computational efficiency, many methods initialize a coarse depth map and then gradually refine it in higher resolutions. Recently, diffusion models achieve great success in generation tasks. Starting from a random noise, diffusion models gradually recover the sample with an iterative denoising process. In this paper, we propose a novel MVS framework, which introduces diffusion models in MVS. Specifically, we formulate depth refinement as a conditional diffusion process. Considering the discriminative characteristic of depth estimation, we design a condition encoder to guide the diffusion process. To improve efficiency, we propose a novel diffusion network combining lightweight 2D U-Net and convolutional GRU. Moreover, we propose a novel confidence-based sampling strategy to adaptively sample depth hypotheses based on the confidence estimated by diffusion model. Based on our novel MVS framework, we propose two novel MVS methods, DiffMVS and CasDiffMVS. DiffMVS achieves competitive performance with state-of-the-art efficiency in run-time and GPU memory. CasDiffMVS achieves state-of-the-art performance on DTU, Tanks & Temples and ETH3D. Code is available at: https://github.com/cvg/diffmvs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel multi-view stereo (MVS) framework that utilizes conditional diffusion models to improve 3D reconstruction from calibrated images. The core idea is to formulate depth map refinement as a conditional diffusion process, guided by a learned condition encoder that fuses matching information, image context, and depth context features.  The authors propose a lightweight diffusion network, using 2D U-Nets and convolutional GRUs for efficient refinement.  A confidence-based sampling strategy adaptively generates depth hypotheses.  Two specific MVS methods, DiffMVS and CasDiffMVS, are derived from the framework, demonstrating competitive and state-of-the-art performance, respectively, with good efficiency. The code will be made publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the integration of diffusion models within the MVS pipeline.  While diffusion models have been applied to monocular depth estimation and other vision tasks, their application to multi-view stereo for refinement, particularly with a conditional architecture tailored to this task, seems novel. The condition encoder and confidence-based sampling are also contributions, though somewhat incremental improvements. A weakness is that the overall pipeline still relies on established techniques like FPNs, cost volume construction, and U-Nets; thus, while the integration is novel, it is not a completely disruptive approach.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the potential to improve both accuracy and efficiency in MVS. Learning-based MVS methods often struggle with balancing computational cost and reconstruction quality. The authors demonstrate a method (DiffMVS) that can achieve competitive performance while being very efficient, and a method (CasDiffMVS) that achieves SOTA performance with good efficiency. This makes the approach practically relevant. Also, it has demonstrated generalizability of its method over multiple known benchmark datasets, making the results more credible and valuable.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Strong experimental results on standard datasets (DTU, Tanks & Temples, ETH3D)
    *   The focus on efficiency (run-time, GPU memory) is practically important.
    *   The clear architecture of the conditional diffusion model with specific design choices (condition encoder, lightweight network) is well-explained.
    *   Ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of different components.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the diffusion model is cleverly integrated, the individual components used (U-Net, GRU) are well-established. The paper lacks a more fundamental theoretical advancement.
    *   The improvement stems from integrating a diffusion model; while the contribution lies in *how* it is integrated for MVS problems; it's not *what* that is significant.
    *   The gains from the confidence-based sampling, while positive, do not seem revolutionary based on the ablation study results. The same with noise parameters adjustment on multiple datasets.
    *   The hyperparameter choices (noise scales, number of refinement steps, sampling ranges) feel a bit ad-hoc; a more systematic analysis of the impact of these parameters would strengthen the paper. Also, details on some parameters and architectures are not clear, thus making the replication difficult.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper could influence the MVS community to explore diffusion models as a tool for refinement and uncertainty modeling. Its focus on efficiency could also inspire the development of more practical MVS systems. Future work can focus on making the framework more adaptable to different scenes and improve the generalization ability. A better theoretical understanding of how diffusion models interact with MVS constraints would be helpful too.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and valuable integration of diffusion models in the context of multi-view stereo, leading to both improved accuracy and efficiency. This is significant as it provides a practical and effective solution to the challenges faced by learning-based MVS methods. The strengths of the paper include its comprehensive experimental validation, clear architecture, and ablation studies. However, the paper's novelty is somewhat incremental as it builds upon well-established techniques, and it relies heavily on hyperparameter tuning. This suggests a high degree of sensitivity to the initial design choices for hyperparameters. Additionally, the gains from the confidence-based sampling could be more pronounced, and a more rigorous analysis of the impact of design parameters would have been beneficial. Nevertheless, the overall contribution is solid, and the paper is likely to stimulate further research in this direction, justifying a score of 7. The practicality and strong experimental validation make it a worthwhile contribution to the field, even if the theoretical underpinnings or individual component contributions aren't groundbreaking.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10
